# Twelve Years — the event pool

**Session 2 · 11–23 AH · 632–644 CE · a 45-minute slot.**

> **THIS FILE IS NOT THE LECTURE. IT IS THE POOL YOU CUT DOWN TO ONE.**
> `DECISIONS.md` #20. Delete the rows you do not want. What survives is the evening. Overflow is
> not compressed — it rolls into session 3. Coverage is never bought by speeding up, which is the
> one thing the room objected to last time.

**How to cut.** Read top to bottom; it is already in narrative order. Delete whole cards. Keep
roughly **22 cards** for a 45-minute slot told calmly — the rest is deliberate over-build.

| | |
|---|---|
| Cards in the pool | **373** |
| If every card were told | **≈ 664 minutes** |
| The slot | **45 minutes** |
| So you are cutting | **about 93%** |

**Tier means:** `CORE` the evening breaks without it · `GOOD` include if time · `CUT` recorded so it
is never re-researched, not planned for delivery.

**Every card carries:** what happened, in tellable English · the date with its certainty label ·
the map move · a verbatim Arabic statement with its printed page and shamela link (or an explicit
"none in the sources we use") · one عبرت line · and whether it opens with a hands-up question.

## Setting: the tribal map, and why the ردة took its shape

*Source note: [`arabian-tribes-and-the-ridda-setup.md`](../docs/research/arabian-tribes-and-the-ridda-setup.md) — read it for the pages behind these cards.*

### ATA/E-TB01 · Arabia was a map of kin, not a map of provinces
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** ۱۱ھ, before any arrow is drawn `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** The whole
peninsula with **no borders on it at all**. Two great feathered zones only — a northern one labelled
**عدنان**, a southern one labelled **قحطان** — and nothing between them but the desert.
**What happened:** Ibn Kathīr never once names a province of Arabia. He names tribes, and the men
who led them. His genealogy of the whole peninsula runs back to two ancestors and no more. From
ʿAdnān come the Arabs of the Ḥijāz and Najd, and Quraysh are of them. From Qaḥṭān come the Arabs of
Yemen, and the Anṣār are of them. So if you had asked a man in that country who he was, the answer
would have been a line of fathers, not a place on a map.
**The statement:**
> وأما معد: فوُلِد له أربعة: نزار، وقضاعة، وقُنُص، وإياد … وأما نزار: فولد لهم ربيعة ومضر وأنمار.
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۲ ص۴۷۵ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/903
> *English:* "As for Maʿadd: four were born to him — Nizār, Quḍāʿa, Qunuṣ and Iyād … And as for
> Nizār: Rabīʿa, Muḍar and Anmār were born to them."

**عبرت:** The unit of that country was the line of fathers, and everything that happened next has to
be read on that map.
**Hands-up?** Before telling it: *"If you had asked a man in Arabia that year where he was from,
what would he have answered?"*
⚠ Ibn Kathīr records a **خلاف** on that same page about where قُضاعة belongs; the line above is
**ابن إسحاق's** view as Ibn Kathīr reports it. Do not present the placement of قُضاعة as settled.

---

### ATA/E-TB02 · Ten sons of Sabaʾ, in the Prophet's ﷺ own words
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** n/a — this is the frame `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** Yemen fills with six names
at once — **مذحج · كِنْدة · الأزد · الأشعريون · أنمار · حِمْيَر**. Then four more appear far to the
north on the Syrian side — **لخم · جُذام · عامِلة · غَسّان**.
**What happened:** A man asked the Prophet ﷺ about Sabaʾ — was it a man, or a woman, or a land? The
answer was that Sabaʾ was a man who had ten children. Six of them settled in Yemen and four in
Syria. And then he named all ten. Ibn Kathīr quotes it through Ibn ʿAbbās ؓ, and a few pages later
he restates the same list as his own conclusion. One question, one answer, and the whole southern
half of the tribal map is set out.
**The statement:**
> بل هو رجل ولد عشرة، فسكن اليمن منهم ستة، وبالشام منهم أربعة. فأما اليمانيون فمَذْحِج وكِنْدة والأَزْد والأشعريون وأَنْمار وحِمْيَر. وأما الشامية فلَخْم وجُذام وعامِلة وغَسّان.
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۲ ص۴۰۷ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/835
> *English:* "Rather, he was a man who had ten children. Six of them settled in Yemen and four of
> them in Syria. As for the Yemeni ones: Madhḥij, Kinda, al-Azd, al-Ashʿarīyūn, Anmār and Ḥimyar.
> And as for the Syrian ones: Lakhm, Judhām, ʿĀmila and Ghassān."

**عبرت:** Half the tribal map of that country is contained in one answer to one question.
**Hands-up?** no
**Note for the deck:** this is the single most useful ḥadīth for the map in the whole safe list, and
it is on one page. **هَمْدان is not among the ten** — do not add them to the diagram (§6.4).

---

### ATA/E-TB03 · The dam broke, and the tribes went out
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** long before Islam `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** **The biggest map moment of the
first half.** From **مأرب** in Yemen, four arrows go out and each one ends on a name the room will
hear again: **آل جفنة** north to الشام · **الأوس والخزرج** north to **يثرب** · **خزاعة** to **مَرّ**,
outside مكة · **أزد السراة** to السراة · **أزد عُمان** to **عُمان**.
**What happened:** The book carries an account of why the southern tribes left Yemen — the dam at
Maʾrib, and the flood. ʿAmr b. ʿĀmir left, and the Azd said they would not stay behind him. They
sold their property and went out with him. Then they scattered through the lands, and Ibn Kathīr
names in one sentence where each group came to rest. Two of those names matter for everything that
follows: the Aws and the Khazraj, who came to Yathrib and were the first to live there — and the Azd
of Oman, who went to the far eastern coast.
**The statement:**
> وقالت الأزد: لا نتخلّف عن عمرو بن عامر، فباعوا أموالهم وخرجوا معه … قال: فارتحلوا عنهم، فتفرَّقوا في البلاد. فنزل آل جفنة بن عمرو بن عامر الشام، ونزل الأوس والخزرج يثرب، ونزلت خزاعة مَرّا، ونزلت أزد السراة السراة، ونزلت أزد عُمان عُمان.
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۲ ص۴۱۰ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/838
> *English:* "The Azd said: we will not stay behind ʿAmr b. ʿĀmir — so they sold their property and
> went out with him … He said: they departed and scattered through the lands. The house of Jafna b.
> ʿAmr b. ʿĀmir settled in Syria; the Aws and the Khazraj settled at Yathrib; Khuzāʿa settled at
> Marr; the Azd of al-Sarāt settled at al-Sarāt; and the Azd of Oman settled in Oman."

**عبرت:** The people who would one day receive the hijra and the people who would one day rise in
Oman came out of the same migration.
**Hands-up?** Before telling it: *"The Anṣār of Medina — where were they from, before Medina?"*
⚠ **Narrate this as the book's account**, which Ibn Kathīr gives from ابن إسحاق from
أبو زيد الأنصاري. **Do not present the mechanism or the sequencing as settled history** (§1.2).

### ATA/E-TB04 · Where Quraysh begins, and the two answers the book keeps
**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** n/a `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** n/a. A short chain across the slide:
عدنان → مَعَدّ → نِزار → مُضَر → إلياس → مُدْرِكة → خُزَيْمة → كِنانة → **النَّضْر**.
**What happened:** Ibn Kathīr runs the line of Quraysh back to ʿAdnān name by name. At the end of it
he records a disagreement inside his own sources about where the name Quraysh actually begins. Ibn
Hishām says it begins at al-Naḍr b. Kināna — whoever is of his children is a Qurashī, and whoever is
not, is not. Others named in the book said it begins one generation later, at Fihr b. Mālik. Ibn
Kathīr writes both down and does not choose between them.
**The statement:**
> قال ابن هشام: النضر هو قُريش، فمن كان من ولده فهو قرشي، ومن لم يكن من ولده فليس بقرشي.
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۲ ص۴۷۷ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/905
> *English:* "Ibn Hishām said: al-Naḍr is Quraysh. Whoever is of his children is a Qurashī, and
> whoever is not of his children is not a Qurashī."

**عبرت:** Even the boundary of the most famous tribe in Arabia had two answers, and the book keeps
both of them.
**Hands-up?** no
⚠ **Do not adjudicate between the two views on a slide.** Ibn Kathīr does not.

---

### ATA/E-TB05 · Quraysh and the Anṣār were the two halves of the peninsula
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** from the hijra onward `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** The peninsula tinted in two —
**عدنان** north, **قحطان** south — and one line drawn from **مكة** to **المدینہ**, so that the city
at the end of it holds both tints inside one wall.
**What happened:** Quraysh are ʿAdnānī: northern Arabs of the Ḥijāz. The Anṣār are Qaḥṭānī: the Aws
and the Khazraj, of al-Azd, of Kahlān, of Sabaʾ — southern Arabs whose ancestors had come north
generations before and were the first to settle Yathrib. So the Muhājirūn and the Anṣār were not two
clans of one people. They were the two ancestral halves of Arabia, put inside one city by the hijra.
That is not an inference; it is on the pages.
**The statement:**
> ومنهم الأوس والخزرج، نزلوا بيثرب وهي المدينة النبوية اليوم، فكانوا أول من سكنها
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۲ ص۴۰۹ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/837
> *English:* "And among them were the Aws and the Khazraj: they settled at Yathrib — which today is
> the Prophet's city — and they were the first to live in it."

**عبرت:** The first thing the hijra did was to put the two ends of Arabia inside one set of walls.
**Hands-up?** no
**Cross-reference:** this is the fact underneath the سقيفة material in session 1 and underneath the
whole of tonight's first half. Page-cited at ج۲ ص۴۰۷، ص۴۰۹–۴۱۰، ص۴۷۵–۴۷۷.

---

### ATA/E-TB06 · What the break actually was — Ibn Kathīr's own sorting
**Tier:** CORE — **this is the diagram slide of the first half** · **When:** ۱۱ھ `[SOURCED]` ·
**Map:** none. This is a `diagram_slide`, not a map.
**What happened:** Do not import a modern classification onto this year. The book supplies one, and
it is better. Before a word of narrative, Ibn Kathīr's own chapter heading has already split the
crisis in two: the people of the ridda, and those who withheld the zakāt. Then his opening paragraph
names four things happening at once. Many settlements of the bedouin turned. Hypocrisy surfaced
inside Medina itself. Banū Ḥanīfa and a great many others in al-Yamāma went over to Musaylima. And
Banū Asad and Ṭayyiʾ and many besides gathered round Ṭulayḥa — who had made the same claim.
**The statement:**
> فصل في تصدّي الصِّدّيق لِقتالِ أهلِ الرِّدة ومانِعي الزَّكاة
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۱۷ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3163
> *English:* "A section on al-Ṣiddīq's undertaking to fight the people of the ridda and those who
> withheld the zakāt."

**عبرت:** The book refuses to call the whole year one thing, and the sorting on the screen is the
author's own, not ours.
**Hands-up?** Before showing it: *"When the news went out to the tribes, was there one reaction, or
more than one?"*

**Diagram rows** — ready for `deck2.diagram_slide(prs, headline, rows=…)`; four cards, every sub
under the 20-word cap:

| # | label | sub |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Left Islam outright | Many settlements of the bedouin turned when the news of the Prophet's ﷺ death reached them. |
| 2 | Hypocrisy inside the city | It surfaced in Medina itself — not out in the desert, but at home. |
| 3 | Massed behind a claimant | Banū Ḥanīfa and many others to Musaylima at al-Yamāma; Asad and Ṭayyiʾ to Ṭulayḥa. |
| 4 | Kept the prayer, refused the payment | And this group split again — over the payment itself, and over who it was owed to. |

**headline:** `What the break actually was` · **caption:** `The sorting is the author's own, not
ours.`

**The four in the book's own words, on one page — ج۷ ص۱۷** (https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3163):
> قد تقدَّم أنّ رسولَ الله ﷺ لمّا توفي ارتدَّت أحياءٌ كثيرةٌ من الأعراب، ونجمَ النّفاق بالمدينة، وانحازَ إلى مُسَيْلمَةَ الكَذَّاب بنو حَنيفةَ وخلقٌ كثيرٌ باليمامة، والتفَّت على طُلَيْحة الأسدي بنو أَسَدٍ وطيِّء، وبشرٌ كثيرٌ أيضًا، وإدَّعى النبوَّة أيضًا، كما إدّعاها مُسَيْلمةُ الكَذَّاب، وعظمَ الخَطْبُ واشتدّتِ الحالُ …
> *English:* "It has already been said that when the Messenger of Allah ﷺ died, many settlements of
> the bedouin apostatised, and hypocrisy appeared in Medina; Banū Ḥanīfa and a great many people at
> al-Yamāma went over to Musaylima the liar; and Banū Asad and Ṭayyiʾ, and a great many people
> besides, gathered round Ṭulayḥa al-Asadī — and he too claimed prophethood, as Musaylima the liar
> had claimed it; and the matter grew grave and the situation grew hard …"

⚠ **Never compose an Arabic label.** If the four boxes are to carry Arabic rather than English, use
only the two terms the heading itself supplies — «أهل الرِّدة» and «مانِعي الزَّكاة» — and leave the
other two boxes in English. Arabic and English must not share a box.

### ATA/E-TB07 · Two refusals, not one
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** ۱۱ھ, as the delegations arrive `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** المدینہ at the
centre, with delegation arrows coming in from every direction and going out again unchanged.
**What happened:** Delegations of the Arabs began arriving in Medina. They affirmed the prayer and
refused to hand over the zakāt. And then Ibn Kathīr splits them again inside a single clause. Some
refused the payment itself, and had a Qurʾānic argument for it: the verse tells the Prophet ﷺ to
take a ṣadaqa from their wealth and pray over them, because his prayer is a repose for them — so, they
said, we pay it only to a man whose prayer is a repose for us. And some of them refused to pay it to
Abū Bakr ؓ. That second group is not refusing the obligation at all. It is refusing the addressee.
**The statement:**
> وجَعَلَتْ وفودُ العرب تقدم المدينة يقرُّون بالصلاة ويمتنعون من أداء الزكاة، ومنهم من امتنعَ من دفعها إلى الصّدّيق
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۱۸ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3164
> *English:* "The delegations of the Arabs began coming to Medina, affirming the prayer and refusing
> to render the zakāt — and among them were those who refused to pay it to al-Ṣiddīq."

**عبرت:** Two men can refuse the same payment for two different reasons, and the book takes care to
say which is which.
**Hands-up?** Before telling it: *"A tribe says: we will pray, but we will not pay. Is that one
position, or two?"*
**Also on the page** — the verse they argued from and their own sentence:
> ﴿خُذْ مِنْ أَمْوَالِهِمْ صَدَقَةً تُطَهِّرُهُمْ وَتُزَكِّيهِمْ بِهَا وَصَلِّ عَلَيْهِمْ إِنَّ صَلَاتَكَ سَكَنٌ لَهُمْ﴾ [التوبة: ١٠٣]
> قالوا: فلسنا ندفعُ زكاتنا إلَّا إلى منْ صلاتُهُ سكنٌ لنا
> *English:* "They said: we do not pay our zakāt except to one whose prayer is a repose for us."

---

### ATA/E-TB08 · The Companions proposed going easy, and he refused
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** ۱۱ھ `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** n/a — a large-statement slide.
**What happened:** The Companions came to Abū Bakr ؓ with a proposal about the tribes that were
withholding the zakāt. Leave them and what they are upon for now. Win them over until faith takes
hold in their hearts, and after that they will pay. It is a serious proposal made by serious men,
and the book records it without any embarrassment at all. He refused it, and would not have it.
**The statement:**
> وقد تكلَّم الصحابةُ مع الصّديق في أن يتركهم وما هم عليه من منعِ الزكاةِ ويتألَّفهم حتى يمكّنَ الإيمانُ في قلوبهم: ثم هم بعد ذلك يُزَكُّونَ، فامتنعَ الصّديق من ذلك وأباه.
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۱۸ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3164
> *English:* "The Companions spoke to al-Ṣiddīq about leaving them and what they were upon of
> withholding the zakāt, and conciliating them until faith took hold in their hearts, and after that
> they would pay the zakāt. But al-Ṣiddīq refused that and would not have it."

**عبرت:** The decision that held the state together was taken against the advice of the men best
placed to give advice, and the book keeps the advice on the page.
**Hands-up?** Before telling it: *"Half the country will not pay. Your closest advisers say: let it
go for a year and win them back. What do you do?"*

---

### ATA/E-TB09 · «لو مَنَعوني عِقالًا» — and what ʿUmar ؓ said afterwards
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** ۱۱ھ `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** n/a — a large-statement slide.
**What happened:** ʿUmar ؓ put the objection to Abū Bakr ؓ directly: on what basis are you fighting
people who say the shahāda, when the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said that once they say it they have
protected their blood and their property from me, except for its due? Abū Bakr's ؓ answer was not
about politics. It was that the zakāt is one of the dues on that property — and that if they
withheld from him a young goat, or a hobbling-cord, that they had been rendering to the Messenger of
Allah ﷺ, he would fight them for withholding it. And ʿUmar ؓ, who had raised the objection, said
afterwards that when he saw Allah had opened Abū Bakr's ؓ breast to fighting, he knew it was the
truth.
**The statement:**
> أنَّ عمرَ بن الخطاب قال لأبي بكر: علامَ تقاتلُ الناسَ؟ وقد قال رسول الله ﷺ: "أمرت أن أقاتلَ الناسَ حتى يشهدوا أن لا إله إلا الله وأن محمدًا رسولُ الله، فإذا قالوها عصموا مني دماءهم وأموالهم إلا بحقها". فقال أبو بكر: والله لو مَنَعوني عَناقًا، وفي رواية: عِقالًا كانوا يؤدّونه إلى رسول الله ﷺ لأقاتِلَنَّهم على منعها، إنَّ الزكاةَ حقُّ المال، واللهِ لأقاتِلَنَّ منْ فرَّقَ بينَ الصلاةِ والزكاةِ، قال عمر: فما هو إلا أن رأيتُ اللهَ قد شرحَ صدرَ أبي بكر للقتال، فعرفتُ أنه الحقُّ.
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۱۸ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3164
> *English:* "ʿUmar b. al-Khaṭṭāb said to Abū Bakr: on what basis do you fight the people, when the
> Messenger of Allah ﷺ has said: 'I have been commanded to fight the people until they bear witness
> that there is no god but Allah and that Muḥammad is the Messenger of Allah; and when they say it,
> they have protected from me their blood and their property, except for its due'? Abū Bakr said: by
> Allah, if they withheld from me a young she-goat — and in another wording, a hobbling-cord — that
> they used to render to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, I would fight them over withholding it. The zakāt
> is a due upon wealth. By Allah, I will fight whoever separates the prayer from the zakāt. ʿUmar
> said: it was no more than that I saw Allah had opened Abū Bakr's breast to the fighting — and I
> knew that it was the truth."

**عبرت:** The argument was settled on a point of law about a hobbling-cord, and the man who had
objected said so himself.
**Hands-up?** no
**Takhrīj, exactly as Ibn Kathīr's editor prints it in the footnote on that page** — copy this, do
not retype it from memory: البخاري **٦٩٢٤ و ٦٩٢٥** في استتابة المرتدين · مسلم **٢٠** في الإيمان ·
أبو داود **١٥٥٦** في الزكاة · الترمذي **٢٦٠٧** في الإيمان · النسائي **٥/١٤** في الزكاة ·
مسند أحمد **٢/٥٢٨**. Ibn Kathīr's own text says «رواه الجماعة … سوى ابن ماجه».
**The gloss, also footnoted there** — ابن حجر رحمہ اللہ: «**العَناق** — بفتح المهملة والنون — الأنثى
من ولد المعز» (فتح الباري ١٢/٢٧٨): *a young female of the goats.*
**The same thing in one clause, through القاسم بن محمد, at ج۷ ص۱۹** (https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3165):
«فعزم الله لأبي بكر على الحق وقال: لو مَنَعوني عِقالًا لجاهَدْتُهم».
⚠ **One Shamela typo to be aware of when the page image is checked:** the cached ج۷ ص۱۸ prints
«**كانو** يؤدّونه» without the final alif. This note reads it as «كانوا», which is plainly right —
but that is a *reading*, so **verify it on the image** before it is printed. (Same class of thing as
the «قى / قد» typo already logged in the campaign note.)

---

### ATA/E-TB10 · One tribe, three answers
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** ۱۱ھ `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** **بنو تَميم** in eastern Najd shaded in three
shades at once, with the three-part key printed on the slide beside it.
**What happened:** In the middle of the narrative the book stops and says what Banū Tamīm did, and it
is not one thing. Their views differed in the days of the ridda. Some of them turned and withheld the
zakāt. Some of them sent the ṣadaqa money in to Abū Bakr ؓ. And some of them held back to see how the
matter would go. That is one tribe in three states at the same moment. Any map that shades Banū Tamīm
a single colour is contradicted by the book it claims to be drawn from.
**The statement:**
> كانت بنو تميم قد اختلفتْ أراؤُهم أيام الرِّدَّة، فمنهم من ارتدَّ ومنعَ الزكاة، ومنهم منْ بعثَ بأموالِ الصَّدقات إلى الصدِّيق، ومنهم من توقف لينظر في أمره.
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۲۸ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3174
> *English:* "The views of Banū Tamīm differed in the days of the ridda: some of them apostatised and
> withheld the zakāt; some of them sent the ṣadaqa monies to al-Ṣiddīq; and some of them held back to
> see how the matter would go."

**عبرت:** The honest map has three colours inside one tribe, and the sentence that licenses it is the
book's own.
**Hands-up?** Before showing it: *"One tribe. How many different answers can it give at the same
moment?"*
**Cross-reference:** `ridda-campaign-the-conduct-of-the-wars.md` defers to this card rather than
carding it again, and adds only the collectors' names and the mechanism.

---

### ATA/E-TB11 · ʿUyayna b. Ḥiṣn says why he followed Ṭulayḥa
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** ۱۱ھ, and again at المدینہ afterwards `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** **فَزارة**'s
zone in Najd tied by a short line to **بني أسد**'s zone — an alliance made on descent, not on belief.
**What happened:** ʿUyayna b. Ḥiṣn brought Fazāra in behind Ṭulayḥa, and he told his own people why
in one sentence. A prophet from Banū Asad, he said, is dearer to me than a prophet from Banū Hāshim.
Muḥammad ﷺ has died, and here is Ṭulayḥa — follow him. His people agreed with him. Ibn Kathīr does
not argue with the sentence; he prints it. And later, when ʿUyayna was brought into Medina in bonds
and the children of the city crowded round and taunted him for having left Islam, he answered them:
by Allah, I never believed at all.
**The statement:**
> والله لَنبيٌّ من بني أسدٍ أحبُّ إليَّ من نبيٍّ من بني هاشم، وقد مات محمدٌ وهذا طُلَيْحةُ فاتَّبعوه
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۲۶ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3172
> *English:* "By Allah, a prophet from Banū Asad is dearer to me than a prophet from Banū Hāshim.
> Muḥammad has died, and this is Ṭulayḥa — so follow him."

**عبرت:** He was not choosing between two prophecies. He was choosing between two lines of descent,
and he said so out loud.
**Hands-up?** Before telling it: *"He is fighting for a man he will soon call a liar to his face.
Ask him why he followed him — what will he answer?"*
**And afterwards, on the same page**, when the boys of Medina jeered at him: «**والله ما كنتُ آمنتُ
قطُّ**» — *"By Allah, I never believed at all."*
**Cross-reference:** this is the same man as the بُزاخة card in
`ridda-campaign-the-conduct-of-the-wars.md`, caught at the moment his following begins rather than
the moment it ends. Tell this one first.

---

### ATA/E-TB12 · The man who followed Musaylima knowing he was lying
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** ۱۱ھ–۱۲ھ, اليمامة `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** not a map — two trunk names on the
screen, **رَبيعة** and **مُضَر**, with the fork shown one level below **عدنان**, so the room can see
that both sides of this sentence are northern Arabs.
**What happened:** A bedouin said to Musaylima's face that he bore witness Musaylima was a liar and
that Muḥammad ﷺ was truthful. And then he said the rest of it. But a liar of Rabīʿa, he said, is
dearer to us than a truthful man of Muḍar. He followed him, and he was killed with him at ʿAqrabāʾ.
Banū Ḥanīfa are of Rabīʿa and Quraysh are of Muḍar, and both trunks are ʿAdnānī. So the fault line
is not only north against south. It forks again, one level down.
**The statement:**
> فقال أشهد أنَّك كذّابٌ وأن محمدًا صادقٌ، ولكن كذّابَ رَبيعة أحبُّ إلينا من صادقِ مُضَر
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۳۷ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3183
> *English:* "He said: I bear witness that you are a liar and that Muḥammad is truthful — but a liar
> of Rabīʿa is dearer to us than a truthful man of Muḍar."

**عبرت:** A man can know which of two claims is true and still act on which of two families is his.
**Hands-up?** no
**Note:** the clause that follows on the page carries Ibn Kathīr's own imprecation against the man;
the quotation stands complete without it. This is the most efficient sentence in the whole strand —
the tribal principle stated by a man who admits the religious claim is false.

---

### ATA/E-TB13 · The verse that puts the objection as politics
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** ۱۱ھ `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** n/a — a large-statement slide.
**What happened:** The book twice prints a verse from the refusing side, and it is worth hearing
exactly what the objection says. We obeyed the Messenger of Allah while he was among us — and then,
in astonishment: what is this about the rule of Abū Bakr? The longer version at ج۷ ص۲۰ asks whether
he is to leave it to Bakr after him, and calls that, by God's life, a thing that breaks the back.
Nothing in those lines denies the prayer, and nothing in them denies the Prophet ﷺ. What they object
to is who is to be obeyed now.
**The statement:**
> أطعنَا رسولَ اللهِ إذْ كانَ بَيْننا … فواعَجَبًا ما بَالُ مُلْكِ أبي بَكْرِ
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۱۸ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3164 (and again ج۷ ص۲۰ ·
> https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3166)
> *English:* "We obeyed the Messenger of Allah while he was among us — so how strange: what is this
> about the rule of Abū Bakr?"

**عبرت:** The refusal stated itself in the language of who should rule, and the answer it was given
was in the language of what is owed.
**Hands-up?** no
**The four-line version at ج۷ ص۲۰** (https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3166) adds:
«أيورثنا بَكْرًا إذا ماتَ بعدَه … وتِلكَ لَعمرُ الله قاصِمَةُ الظَّهرِ» — *"Is he to leave us to Bakr as
an inheritance when he dies? That, by God's life, breaks the back."*
⚠ **Say whose words these are before reading them.** They are a rebel's verse, printed by the book as
the objection that was being made against the caliphate — not as a description of it. Pair this card
with E-TB07 and E-TB09 so the room hears the objection and the answer in the same minute, and do not
leave the verse standing alone at the end of a block.

---

### ATA/E-TB14 · Four claimants, and when each of them began
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** ۱۰ھ–۱۲ھ `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** Four red marks — **صنعاء** (الأسود),
**اليمامة** (مسيلمة), **بُزاخة** in north Najd (طليحة), and an arrow coming down from **الجزيرة**
(سجاح). Beneath it, the timeline strip: three of the four begin **before** ۱۱ھ.
**What happened:** Four people claimed prophethood in this story, and the order matters more than the
number. Al-Aswad al-ʿAnsī, of ʿAns of Madhḥij, rose in Yemen and was killed before the Prophet ﷺ
died. Musaylima of Banū Ḥanīfa had come to Medina in the Prophet's ﷺ lifetime and written to him; he
was killed after. Ṭulayḥa of Banū Asad — the book says it plainly — apostatised in the Prophet's ﷺ
lifetime. Only Sajāḥ comes afterwards: she arrives from al-Jazīra in the middle of the ridda
narrative itself. So three of the four had already begun while the Prophet ﷺ was alive.
**The statement:**
> وقد كان طُلَيْحةُ الأسديُّ ارتدَّ في حياةِ النبي ﷺ
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۲۶ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3172
> *English:* "Ṭulayḥa al-Asadī had apostatised in the lifetime of the Prophet ﷺ."

**عبرت:** Most of what broke in that year had already begun before the year began.
**Hands-up?** Before telling it: *"Four people claimed prophethood. How many of them started only
after the Prophet ﷺ died?"*
**The table this card draws from is §4** — claimant, tribe, place, and before-or-after, each with its
page. A fifth man, **لقيط بن مالك الأزدي** in Oman, also claimed prophethood (E-TB25); the "four" is
the count of the famous claimants, not a count of all claims.

---

### ATA/E-TB15 · Musaylima's letter, and the reply
**Tier:** CORE — **the note's own judgement is that this is the best pair in the strand** ·
**When:** **آخر سنة عشر** `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** One line from **اليمامة** to **المدینہ** and one
back. Two documents side by side on the screen, nothing else.
**What happened:** Musaylima wrote to the Prophet ﷺ. The letter does not argue about revelation at
all. It proposes a partition: I have been given a share in the matter with you, half the matter is
ours and half is Quraysh's — but Quraysh are a people who transgress. The reply is on the same page,
and it refuses the premise rather than the arithmetic. The earth belongs to Allah; He gives it in
inheritance to whom He wills of His servants; and the outcome is for those who fear Him. The book
dates the exchange to the end of the year 10.
**The statement:**
> من مُسَيْلَمة رسولِ اللهِ إلى محمدٍ رسولِ الله، سلامٌ عَلَيْكَ، أما بعدُ، فإني قد أُشْرِكْتُ في الأمْرِ معكَ، فإنّ لنا نِصْفَ الأمْرِ، ولقُرَيشٍ نصف الأمْرِ، ولكن قُريشًا قومٌ يَعْتدون.
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۵ ص۲۴ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/2208
> *English:* "From Musaylima the Messenger of Allah, to Muḥammad the Messenger of Allah. Peace be
> upon you. To proceed: I have been given a share in the matter with you. Half the matter is ours
> and half the matter is Quraysh's — but Quraysh are a people who transgress."

**And the reply, same page:**
> بسم الله الرحمن الرحيم، من محمد رسول الله، إلى مُسَيْلَمة الكَذّاب، سلامٌ على مَنِ اتَّبَعَ الهُدَى، أما بعد، فإنَّ الأرضَ لله يُورِثُها منْ يَشاءُ من عباده والعاقبة للمتقين.
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۵ ص۲۴ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/2208
> *English:* "In the name of Allah, the Most Merciful, the Most Compassionate. From Muḥammad the
> Messenger of Allah, to Musaylima the liar. Peace be upon whoever follows the guidance. To proceed:
> the earth belongs to Allah; He gives it in inheritance to whomever He wills of His servants; and
> the outcome is for those who fear Him."

**عبرت:** One document divides the country between two tribes, and the other answers that the country
belongs to neither of them.
**Hands-up?** Before telling it: *"A man writes to the Prophet ﷺ proposing terms. What do you think
he asks for?"*
⚠ The editor footnotes a **variant wording** of the letter — «نصف **الأرض** ولقريش نصف **الأرض**»
(مجموعة الوثائق السياسية ٢٠٥). **Note both; never merge them into one.**

---

### ATA/E-TB16 · Musaylima at Medina, and the piece of palm-branch
**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** in the Prophet's ﷺ lifetime `[SOURCED]` — **صحيح البخاري ٤٣٧٣** ·
**Map:** n/a.
**What happened:** Musaylima came to Medina while the Prophet ﷺ was alive, with a large number of his
own people, and kept saying that if Muḥammad ﷺ would make the succession his, he would follow him.
The Prophet ﷺ came out to him with Thābit b. Qays b. Shammās ؓ, and there was a piece of a
palm-branch in his hand. And what he said to him was about that piece.
**The statement:**
> لو سألتني هذه القطعة ما أعطيتكها، ولن تعدوَ أمرَ اللهِ فيك، ولئن أدبرتَ ليعقرنَّك اللهُ
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۵ ص۲۱ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/2205 — **صحيح البخاري ٤٣٧٣**, via
> ابن عباس ؓ
> *English:* "If you asked me for this piece, I would not give it to you. You will not pass beyond
> Allah's decree concerning you. And if you turn away, Allah will surely destroy you."

**عبرت:** The claim was refused while it was still only a request, years before it had to be fought.
**Hands-up?** no
**Cross-reference:** the Companion standing beside the Prophet ﷺ in this scene, **ثابت بن قيس بن
شماس ؓ**, is the man killed carrying the banner of the Anṣār at اليمامة against this same claimant
(ج۷ ص۳۳–۳۴) — carded in `ridda-campaign-the-conduct-of-the-wars.md`. If both cards are used, put
this one first and let the room make the connection.

---

### ATA/E-TB17 · The two bracelets
**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** in the Prophet's ﷺ lifetime `[SOURCED]` — **صحيح البخاري ٤٣٧٥** ·
**Map:** two dots only, **صنعاء** and **اليمامة**, lit at the same moment.
**What happened:** The Prophet ﷺ said that while he was asleep he was brought the treasures of the
earth, and two bracelets of gold were placed in his hands, and they weighed on him. He was inspired
to blow on them; he blew, and they went. And he interpreted them as the two liars between whom he
stood — the one of Ṣanʿāʾ and the one of al-Yamāma. The two men who would break Arabia are named in
one sentence, by their cities, before either of them had risen.
**The statement:**
> بينا أنا نائم أتيتُ بخزائن الأرض، فوُضع في كفي سواران من ذهب، فكَبُرا عليّ، فأُوحي إليَّ أن انفُخْهما، فنفختُهما، فذهبا فأوَّلتهما الكَذَّابَيْن اللذين أنا بينهما؛ صاحبَ صَنْعاء، وصاحبَ اليَمامة
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۵ ص۲۲ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/2206 — **صحيح البخاري ٤٣٧٥**, via
> همّام بن مُنَبِّه from أبي هريرة ؓ
> *English:* "While I was sleeping I was brought the treasures of the earth, and two bracelets of
> gold were placed in my hands, and they weighed heavily on me. It was revealed to me that I should
> blow on them; so I blew on them, and they went. And I interpreted them as the two liars between
> whom I stand — the man of Ṣanʿāʾ and the man of al-Yamāma."

**عبرت:** The two claims that broke the peninsula are named together, by their cities, in one
sentence.
**Hands-up?** no
⚠ **OCR/typography caveat, logged in §4.2 and repeated here because this one is going on a slide.**
The Shamela text prints «انفُخْهَّما» with a stray shadda in the ٤٣٧٥ wording, while the ٤٣٧٣ wording
on ج۵ ص۲۱–۲۲ prints «انفُخْهُما». **Read this off a printed page image or a critical البخاري before
it reaches a slide.** One wrong letter kills the point of quoting it.

---

### ATA/E-TB18 · Sajāḥ — who she actually was
**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** ۱۱ھ `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** An arrow from **الجزيرة** in the far north,
down into نجد, on to **اليمامة**, and then back north again. **It never reaches Medina.**
**What happened:** Sajāḥ bint al-Ḥārith came down from al-Jazīra with troops of her own people and
whoever had joined them, having claimed prophethood — and, the book says, they had resolved on making
war on Abū Bakr ؓ. She was of Taghlib, and she was of the Christians of the Arabs. She was not of
Tamīm, which is only the tribe she gathered on the way. When she passed through the country of Banū
Tamīm she called them to her cause and most of them answered — Mālik b. Nuwayra ؓ among them, and
ʿUṭārid b. Ḥājib, and a number of the chiefs of Tamīm. Others of them held back.
**The statement:**
> إذ أقبلت سجاح بنتُ الحارث بن سويد بن عقفان التغلبية من الجزيرة، وهي من نصارى العرب، وقد ادَّعتِ النبوَّة
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۲۸ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3174
> *English:* "…when Sajāḥ bint al-Ḥārith b. Suwayd b. ʿAqfān al-Taghlibiyya came forward from
> al-Jazīra — and she was of the Christians of the Arabs — having claimed prophethood."

**عبرت:** The tribe that supplied her following was not the tribe she came from, and the book is
careful to say so.
**Hands-up?** no
**Also on the page** — a verse said about it by ʿUṭārid b. Ḥājib, one of the men who had answered
her: «**أمستْ نَبيَّتُنا أُنثَى نُطيفُ بها … وأصبحَتْ أنبياءُ النَّاس ذُكرانا**» — *"our prophetess has
become a woman we circle around, while the prophets of other people are men."*
⚠ **Correction recorded, because two existing artifacts have it wrong:** she is **Taghlibī**, not
Tamīmī. It is **مالك بن نويرة ؓ** who is التميمي اليربوعي (§6.2 item 3).
⚠ **مالك بن نويرة ؓ is named here only as one of the men who answered her call, which is what the
page says.** What happened to him afterwards at **البُطاح** is مشاجرات-adjacent, is carded separately
in `ridda-campaign-the-conduct-of-the-wars.md` under **⚠ SPEAKER'S DISCRETION**, and must not be
opened from this card.

---

### ATA/E-TB19 · What her claim was worth to the other claimant
**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** ۱۱ھ, at اليمامة, and afterwards `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** her arrow reaches
**اليمامة**, turns, and goes back north to **الجزيرة**.
**What happened:** The two claimants met at al-Yamāma, and he bought her off. She took half the
kharāj of his land and turned back to her own country, and she stayed among her people, Banū Taghlib,
until the time of Muʿāwiya. The dowry he paid her was the remission of two of the five prayers — the
dawn prayer and the night prayer. That is what one claim to prophethood was worth to the other. And
al-Suhaylī, whom Ibn Kathīr quotes, records that she became Muslim in the days of ʿUmar b. al-Khaṭṭāb
ؓ, and that her Islam was good.
**The statement:**
> ثم انثنت سَجَاح راجعةً إلى بلادها … فكرَّتْ راجعةً إلى الجزيرة بعدما قبضَتْ من مسيلمة نصفَ خراجِ أرضِهِ، فأقامت في قومها بني تَغْلِب، إلى زمان معاوية
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۳۰ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3176
> *English:* "Then Sajāḥ turned back to her own country … she went back to al-Jazīra after she had
> taken from Musaylima half the kharāj of his land, and she stayed among her people, Banū Taghlib,
> until the time of Muʿāwiya."

**عبرت:** A claim that could be settled for half a land-tax was priced by the man who bought it.
**Hands-up?** no
**And afterwards — البدایہ ج۵ ص۲۴** (https://shamela.ws/book/30097/2208), Ibn Kathīr quoting
السهيلي: «**وقد أسْلَمَتْ هي أيضًا أيام عُمَرَ بن الخطاب، فحسن إسلامُها**» — *"and she too became
Muslim in the days of ʿUmar b. al-Khaṭṭāb, and her Islam was good."* **End the card here**, not on
the withdrawal — the note flags this as the resolution the room should be given.
⚠ **The narrative of that meeting at ج۷ ص۲۹–۳۰ contains explicitly obscene material. It must not be
read, paraphrased, or alluded to in the hall.** The usable fact is exactly the one clean sentence
above: he bought her off, and the dowry he paid was two of the five prayers.

---

### ATA/E-TB20 · Ṭulayḥa comes back, and the letter about how to use him
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** after بُزاخة, in Abū Bakr's ؓ time `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** a thin blue line
returning from **الشام**, to **مكة** for ʿumra, and then to the Muslim army in the field.
**What happened:** Ṭulayḥa returned to Islam. He went to Mecca to perform ʿumra in Abū Bakr's ؓ days,
and he was ashamed to face him for as long as Abū Bakr ؓ lived. He came back and fought alongside
Khālid ؓ. And Abū Bakr ؓ wrote to Khālid ؓ about him with a single instruction: consult him in the
fighting, and do not put him in command. Ibn Kathīr explains why in his own words — he was dealt with
by the opposite of the thing he had secretly been after, which was leadership. And then Ibn Kathīr
says what he thinks of it: this is from the fiqh of al-Ṣiddīq ؓ.
**The statement:**
> وكتبَ الصّدّيق إلى خالدٍ، أن استَشِرْهُ في الحرب ولا تُؤَمِّره — يعني معاملته له بنقيض ما كان قصده من الرئاسة في الباطن — وهذا من فقه الصّدّيق ﵁
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۲۶ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3172
> *English:* "And al-Ṣiddīq wrote to Khālid: consult him in the fighting, and do not put him in
> command — meaning that he dealt with him by the opposite of what he had inwardly been aiming at, of
> leadership. And this is from the fiqh of al-Ṣiddīq ؓ."

**عبرت:** The man's ability was taken and his ambition was not fed, and the book calls that a point
of fiqh.
**Hands-up?** Before telling it: *"A man who had claimed prophethood comes back to Islam, and he is a
good soldier. What do you do with him?"*
**Also on the page:** «**وأما طُلَيْحة فإنه راجعَ الإسلام بعد ذلك أيضًا، وذهب إلى مكةَ مُعتمرًا أيامَ
الصّدّيق، واستحيى أن يواجهه مدةَ حياتِه**» — *"as for Ṭulayḥa, he returned to Islam after that as
well, and went to Mecca to perform ʿumra in the days of al-Ṣiddīq, and was ashamed to face him for as
long as he lived."*
⚠ **The book takes him no further than this** — عمرة in Abū Bakr's ؓ time and fighting under Khālid ؓ.
Claims that he died in the conquest of Iran are **not** on these pages (§6.4). Do not add them.

---

### ATA/E-TB21 · «ما خلا أهل المسجدين» — and the book's own corrections to it
**Tier:** CORE — **this is the closing map of the first half** · **When:** ۱۱ھ `[SOURCED]` ·
**Map:** the whole peninsula goes red except **مكة** and **المدینہ**. Then the corrections are added
one at a time, in front of the room: **الطائف** turns blue · **جُواثى** turns blue with a ring round
it · **طيّئ** turns from red to blue. Four keys, and no more: **held · held under siege · prayed but
withheld the zakāt · rose behind a claimant.**
**What happened:** Ibn Isḥāq states it in one sentence: the Arabs apostatised at the death of the
Messenger of Allah ﷺ, except the people of the two mosques — Mecca and Medina. Then he lists which
tribe went behind which man. Ibn Kathīr prints that sentence, and then, in his own pages, corrects it
three times. Thaqīf at Ṭāʾif stood firm on Islam; they neither fled nor turned. Juwāthā in Bahrayn
held out under siege. And Ṭayyiʾ, who are in the list, were brought back before the fighting reached
them. The famous sentence is the headline. The same book is the correction.
**The statement:**
> قال محمد بن إسحاق: ارتدَّتِ العربُ عند وفاةِ رسول الله ﷺ ما خلا أهل المسجدين، مكة، والمدينة
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۱۹ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3165
> *English:* "Muḥammad b. Isḥāq said: the Arabs apostatised at the death of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ,
> except the people of the two mosques — Mecca and Medina."

**عبرت:** The most quotable sentence about that year is corrected three times by the same book that
carries it.
**Hands-up?** Before showing it: *"When the news went out, how much of Arabia held?"*
⚠ **Do not shade جُواثى and مكة the same colour.** Ibn Kathīr distinguishes them: Mecca and Medina
kept the جمعة throughout, and Juwāthā was the **first to resume** it «بعد رجوع الناس إلى الحق»
(ج۷ ص۹ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3155, citing **صحيح البخاري ٨٩٢ و ٤٣٧١** via ابن عباس ؓ).
**The rest of Ibn Isḥāq's list** — which tribe behind which man — is at the same page and is the
skeleton the eleven arrows of the campaign are drawn onto.

---

### ATA/E-TB22 · Thaqīf had been Muslim for two years, and did not break
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** ۱۱ھ; the delegation was in **سنة تسع** `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** **الطائف**
stays blue, alone in its part of the map, while everything around it goes red.
**What happened:** Thaqīf at Ṭāʾif had come into Islam only in the year 9 — two years before all
this. They did not flee and they did not turn. And what was standing there was one man. The
delegation of Thaqīf had come to the Prophet ﷺ in the year 9 and become Muslim, and he put ʿUthmān b.
Abī al-ʿĀṣ ؓ over them for what he saw of his intelligence and his eagerness for good — and he was
the youngest man in the delegation. Abū Bakr ؓ then confirmed him over Ṭāʾif, and after him ʿUmar ؓ.
Tribes that had been inside Islam far longer broke. So the variable was not the age of the
conversion.
**The statement:**
> وقد كانت ثقيفُ بالطائف ثَبَتوا على الإسلام، لم يفرُّوا ولا ارتدّوا
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۹ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3155
> *English:* "Thaqīf at Ṭāʾif had stood firm upon Islam: they did not flee and they did not
> apostatise."

**عبرت:** The newest Muslims in the peninsula held, and what was standing among them was a man the
Prophet ﷺ had chosen and Abū Bakr ؓ left exactly where he was.
**Hands-up?** Before telling it: *"Which would you expect to break first — the oldest Muslims, or the
newest?"*
**The man, from سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۲ ص۳۷۴** (https://shamela.ws/book/10906/2354), الذہبی رحمہ اللہ
on **عثمان بن أبي العاص ؓ الثقفي الطائفي**:
> قَدِمَ فِي وَفْدِ ثَقِيْفٍ عَلَى النَّبِيِّ ﷺ فِي سَنَةِ تِسْعٍ، فَأَسْلَمُوا، وَأَمَّرَهُ عَلَيْهِم لِمَا رَأَى مِنْ عَقْلِهِ وَحِرْصِهِ عَلَى الخَيْرِ وَالدِّيْنِ، وَكَانَ أَصْغَرَ الوَفْدِ سِنّاً. ثُمَّ أَقَرَّهُ أَبُو بَكْرٍ عَلَى الطَّائِفِ، ثُمَّ عُمَرُ
> *English:* "He came in the delegation of Thaqīf to the Prophet ﷺ in the year 9, and they became
> Muslim; and he put him in command over them for what he saw of his intelligence and his eagerness
> for good and for religion — and he was the youngest of the delegation in years. Then Abū Bakr
> confirmed him over Ṭāʾif, and then ʿUmar."

⚠ **The famous line often attributed to عثمان بن أبي العاص ؓ — that Thaqīf were the last to enter
Islam and would not be the first to leave it — could not be found on any page fetched in this pass.**
It is not at سیر ج۲ ص۳۷۴–۳۷۵ and not at البدایہ ج۷ ص۹. **Do not use it.** `(to verify)`

---

### ATA/E-TB23 · ʿAdī b. Ḥātim ؓ, and the two names Ṭayyiʾ called Abū Bakr ؓ
**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** ۱۱ھ, before بُزاخة `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** Ṭayyiʾ's country, between the two
mountains **أجَأ وسَلْمى**, turns from red to blue; then a thin arrow of a thousand riders runs from
it to the Muslim army.
**What happened:** Abū Bakr ؓ sent ʿAdī b. Ḥātim ؓ ahead of the army to his own tribe with one
instruction: reach your people before they join Ṭulayḥa, or it will be their destruction. Ṭayyiʾ
refused him first. They said: we will never give the bayʿa to Abū al-Faḍl — meaning Abū Bakr ؓ. And
he answered that an army was coming that would not stop fighting them until they knew he was Abū
al-Faḥl al-Akbar. Three days later ʿAdī ؓ came back with five hundred fighting men who had returned
to the truth. Then he went to Banū Jadīla and stayed with them until they followed him too, and a
thousand riders of them joined the Muslims. Ibn Kathīr's verdict on him is one line.
**The statement:**
> فكان عديٌّ خيرَ مولودٍ وأعظمَهُ بركةً على قومه ﵃
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۲۵ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3171
> *English:* "ʿAdī was the best child ever born and the greatest of them in blessing upon his own
> people ؓ."

**عبرت:** A tribe that is on the list of those who broke is on it because of one of its own men, and
off it again because of another.
**Hands-up?** no
**The brief he was given, at ج۷ ص۲۴** (https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3170): «**أدرك قومك لا يلحقوا
بطليحة فيكون دمارهم**» — *"reach your people before they join Ṭulayḥa, for that would be their
destruction."* **And their refusal, same page:** «**لا نبايع أبا الفضل أبدًا**».
**He came to the Prophet ﷺ «فِي وَسْطِ سَنَةِ سَبْعٍ»** and is «**وَلَدُ حَاتِمِ طَيٍّ الَّذِي يُضْرَبُ
بِجُوْدِهِ المَثَلُ**» — سیر أعلام النبلاء **ج۳ ص۱۶۳** · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/2772
(the note's §5.4 gives the span ج۳ ص۱۶۲–۱۶۳; **both of those sentences are on ص۱۶۳**)
**Cross-reference:** `ridda-campaign-the-conduct-of-the-wars.md` cards the three days Khālid ؓ halted
the army at his request. That card and this one are the same operation from two sides; if both are
used, this one goes first.

---

### ATA/E-TB24 · Al-Jārūd ؓ asks his tribe five questions
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** ۱۱ھ, البحرين `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** **البحرين in two colours**, a few days'
ride apart — the rising in red, **عبد القيس** in blue.
**What happened:** In Bahrayn one side had a slogan: if Muḥammad had been a prophet, he would not
have died. A man of ʿAbd al-Qays, al-Jārūd b. al-Muʿallā ؓ, gathered his people and answered it
without arguing with anybody. He told them he was going to ask them about something, and that they
should answer if they knew and stay silent if they did not. They said: ask. Do you know that Allah
had prophets before Muḥammad ﷺ? They said yes. Do you know that, or do you merely think it? They
said: we know it. What did they do? They said: they died. And he said: then Muḥammad ﷺ has died as
they died — and I bear witness that there is no god but Allah and that Muḥammad is the Messenger of
Allah. And they said: and we bear witness too. They held to their Islam and left the rest of the
people to what they were in.
**The statement:**
> فإنَّ محمدًا ﷺ ماتَ كما ماتوا، وإني أشهدُ أن لا إله إلا الله وأن محمدًا رسول الله
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۳۸ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3184
> *English:* "Muḥammad ﷺ has died just as they died — and I bear witness that there is no god but
> Allah and that Muḥammad is the Messenger of Allah."

**عبرت:** Both sides in that region were arguing from the same fact, and the difference was one man
who asked his questions in the right order.
**Hands-up?** Before telling it: *"Their slogan was: if he had been a prophet, he would not have
died. How would you answer it?"*
**The slogan itself, on the same page:** «وقال قائلهم: **لو كان محمد نبيًا ما مات**».
**And on the same page, the besieged at جُواثى** put their message out in four lines of verse:
«**ألا أبلغْ أبا بَكْرٍ رَسولًا … وَفتيانَ المَدينةِ أجْمَعينا / فهَلْ لكُمُ إلى قومٍ كرامٍ … قُعود في جُواثا
مُحصرينا**». ⚠ The last word of the verse differs between البدایہ and الکامل; **read it off a page
image before printing it** (logged in the campaign note).

---

### ATA/E-TB25 · The Azd stood on both sides of the map
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** ۱۱ھ `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** **Two markers in the same colour-family at
opposite ends of the peninsula** — عُمان in the far east, المدینہ in the west — both labelled with
one tribal name: **الأزد**.
**What happened:** This is the fact that stops the genealogy from turning into a theory. The Anṣār of
Medina are of al-Azd, and so are the Azd of Oman. In the same months, the Azd of Oman were in open
revolt behind Laqīṭ b. Mālik al-Azdī, who was called Dhū al-Tāj and who also claimed prophethood —
while the Azd of Medina were carrying the banner of the Anṣār at al-Yamāma under Thābit b. Qays b.
Shammās ؓ, who dug his feet into the ground to his shins and died standing there. One descent, two
ends of the map, two opposite choices in the same year.
**The statement:**
> فنبغَ فيهم رجلٌ يقالَ له: ذو التاج لَقيطُ بن مالك الأَزدي، وكان يُسَمَّى في الجاهلية الجُلَنْدَى، فادّعى النُّبوَّة أيضًا
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۴۱ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3187
> *English:* "There arose among them a man called Dhū al-Tāj, Laqīṭ b. Mālik al-Azdī — who used to be
> called al-Julandā in the Jāhiliyya — and he too claimed prophethood."

**عبرت:** Descent explains the vocabulary people used that year. It does not predict where any of
them stood.
**Hands-up?** Before telling it: *"The Anṣār of Medina, and the tribe that rose in Oman — how are the
two related?"*
**This عبرت line is the safest and truest thing the speaker can say about the whole عدنان/قحطان
strand**, and it is what keeps the fault-line slides from becoming an ethnic theory. If only one
sentence of §2 survives the cut, keep this one.

---

### ATA/E-TB26 · «قُتل العنسيُّ البارحةَ»
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** before the Prophet's ﷺ death `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** **صنعاء** in red, and
one line running not from the map at all but straight down onto المدینہ.
**What happened:** The first of the four claimants was killed inside Ṣanʿāʾ, by three men of the
city, while the Prophet ﷺ was still alive. And Ibn Kathīr says the news reached the Prophet ﷺ that
same night, from above, before any rider could have carried it. He told them: al-ʿAnsī was killed
last night; a blessed man of a blessed household killed him. They asked who. He said: Fayrūz. Fayrūz.
**The statement:**
> أتى الخبرُ إلى النبي ﷺ من السماء الليلةَ التي قُتل فيه العنسي ليبشّرنا، فقال: قُتل العنسيُّ البارحةَ، قتله رجلٌ مباركٌ من أهل بيتٍ مباركين، قيل: ومنْ؟ قال: فيروزُ فيروزُ.
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۱۷ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3163
> *English:* "The news came to the Prophet ﷺ from heaven on the night al-ʿAnsī was killed, to give us
> good tidings. He said: al-ʿAnsī was killed last night — a blessed man of a blessed household killed
> him. It was said: and who? He said: Fayrūz. Fayrūz."

**عبرت:** The first of the four claims was already finished before the crisis that is named after it
began.
**Hands-up?** no
⚠ **Teach the distinction precisely, because the two dates are different things.** The **killing** is
before the Prophet's ﷺ death. **The news reaching المدینہ** is at the end of ربيع الأول ۱۱ھ, after
جيش أسامة marched — and Ibn Kathīr records a second report that the good news arrived on the morning
the Prophet ﷺ died, then says «**والأول أشهر**». **Do not adjudicate between the two on a slide.**
**Cross-reference:** the arrival of the news is already carded, as CUT, in the pool
(`ABU/E-U9`, البدایہ ج۷ ص۱۲). This card is the killing itself, which is a different night.

---

### ATA/E-TB27 · Three days of prayer, and then the ground moved
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** ربيع الأول ۱۱ھ, صنعاء `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** صنعاء turns from red back to
blue — and then, three days later, the whole peninsula begins to change colour behind it.
**What happened:** Fayrūz ؓ tells it himself, and it is the best account in the sources of what the
news of the Prophet's ﷺ death actually did. We killed al-Aswad, he says, and our affair in Ṣanʿāʾ
went back to what it had been. They sent for Muʿādh b. Jabal ؓ and agreed on him, and he led them in
prayer in Ṣanʿāʾ. And by Allah, he says, he had led us in prayer for no more than three days when the
news came to us of the death of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. Then everything came apart. We found
ourselves refusing much of what we used to acknowledge. And the earth shook.
**The statement:**
> قتلنا الأسودَ، وعاد أمرُنا في صنعاء كما كان إلا أنّا أرسلنا إلى معاذ بن جبل فتراضَيْنا عليه، فكان يُصلّي بنا في صنعاء، فوالله ما صلّى بنا إلا ثلاثةَ أيام حتى أتانا الخبرُ بوفاةِ رسولِ الله ﷺ، فانتقضت الأمور، وأنكرنا كثيرًا مما كنا نعرفُ، واضطربتِ الأرض.
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۱۷ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3163
> *English:* "We killed al-Aswad, and our affair in Ṣanʿāʾ returned to what it had been, except that
> we sent to Muʿādh b. Jabal and agreed on him, and he used to lead us in prayer in Ṣanʿāʾ. And by
> Allah, he led us in prayer for no more than three days before the news reached us of the death of
> the Messenger of Allah ﷺ — and matters came apart, and we found ourselves rejecting much of what we
> had known, and the earth shook."

**عبرت:** A man who had just put down a rebellion had three quiet days, and then heard the news that
undid them.
**Hands-up?** no
**This is the best single opening for the whole ردة block**, because it is spoken by somebody who was
standing in it, and because it says what the crisis was without naming a single tribe.

---

### ATA/E-TB28 · How long the first false prophet ruled
**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** the months before ۱۱ھ `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** **كهف خُبّان** → **نجران** →
**صنعاء**, three marks in sequence with the intervals written between them: **ten nights** and
**twenty-five nights** from his coming out.
**What happened:** Al-Aswad al-ʿAnsī came out from a place called Kahf Khubbān with seven hundred
fighting men. Ten nights from his coming out he had Najrān. Twenty-five nights from his coming out he
had Ṣanʿāʾ, and he killed Shahr b. Bādhām and married his widow. The Prophet's ﷺ governors in Yemen
fled or hid. And then the book says how long the whole thing lasted — and refuses to be certain even
about that.
**The statement:**
> وقد قيل: إنَّ مدةَ مُلكه منذ ظهر إلى أن قتل ثلاثة أشهر، ويقال: أربعة أشهر، فالله أعلم
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۱۷ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3163
> *English:* "It has been said that the duration of his rule, from when he appeared until he was
> killed, was three months — and it is said four months. And Allah knows best."

**عبرت:** The whole of it took less than a season, and the book still writes "Allah knows best" over
the number.
**Hands-up?** Before telling it: *"He starts with seven hundred men. How long do you think it took
him to hold Yemen?"*
**His three deputies and his three killers, at ج۷ ص۱۴ and ج۷ ص۴۲:** the army over مذحج under
**عمرو بن معدي كرب**, الجُنْد under **قيس بن عبد يغوث** (the same man the book elsewhere calls
**قيس بن مكشوح**), and الأبناء under **فيروز الديلمي** and **داذويه** — and it was قيس، فيروز and
داذويه who killed him.

---

### ATA/E-TB29 · Why the map has no lines on it
**Tier:** CUT — this is the standing drawing rule, recorded so it is never re-researched ·
**When:** n/a · **Map:** the rule itself.
**What happened:** The sources place tribes **relatively** — «بأرض نجد»، «باليمامة»، «بالبحرين» — and
almost never absolutely. Even the editor's own footnotes, quoting معجم البلدان, locate the famous
places only against other places. **بُزاخة** is «a water of Ṭayyiʾ in the land of Najd, and it is
said, of Banū Asad». **دارين** is «a landing-place in Bahrayn, between it and the coast a day and a
night». So every polygon on every map of this course is a modern reconstruction. Draw feathered
zones and arrows. Never a point, never a boundary line, never a coordinate on the slide face.
**The statement:**
> بالضم والخاء المعجمة: ماء لطيِّئ بأرض نجد، وقيل لبني أسد، كانت فيه وقعة عظيمة في أيام أبي بكر الصديق مع طليحة الأسدي
> — البدایہ والنہایہ, **the دار ابن كثير editor's footnote** at ج۷ ص۲۵ (quoting معجم البلدان), not
> Ibn Kathīr's own text · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3171
> *English:* "Buzākha — with a ḍamma and the pointed khāʾ: a water of Ṭayyiʾ in the land of Najd, and
> it is said, of Banū Asad. In it was a great engagement in the days of Abū Bakr al-Ṣiddīq, against
> Ṭulayḥa al-Asadī."

**عبرت:** —
**Hands-up?** no
**Use the map to teach structure, not precision.** That sentence, and the four-key convention in
E-TB21, are what `MAPS.md` should carry.

---

---

## The ردة wars: the campaign, front by front

*Source note: [`ridda-campaign-the-conduct-of-the-wars.md`](../docs/research/ridda-campaign-the-conduct-of-the-wars.md) — read it for the pages behind these cards.*

### RCT/E-RC01 · The night raid on Medina
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** ۱۱ھ, within days of the delegation's return `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** المدينة
lights up. Short red arrows onto the city's passes from the north-east. A second red block halts at
**ذو حُسى** — a valley in the country of عبس and غطفان (البدایہ ed. fn. ج۷ ص۲۰); **no page gives its
distance, so do not label one.** A blue arrow goes out from المدينة, reaches ذو حُسى, and
recoils back to المدينة.
**What happened:** The delegation that had offered the prayer and refused the zakāt went home. On the
way out they counted the men in the city. Three days later the raiders came at night. Half of them
stayed back at a valley called Dhū Ḥusā as a reserve. Abū Bakr ؓ came out with whoever was in the
mosque, on the watering-camels, and drove the raiders off. Then he pursued them — straight into the
reserve, which rolled inflated skins down at the Muslims' camels. The camels bolted and carried
their riders all the way home.
**The statement:**
> فَخَرَجَ عَلَيْهِمُ الرِّدْءُ بِأَنْحَاءَ قَدْ نَفَخُوهَا … فَنَفَرَتْ إِبِلُ الْمُسْلِمِينَ وَهُمْ عَلَيْهَا، وَرَجَعَتْ بِهِمْ إِلَى الْمَدِينَةِ، وَلَمْ يُصْرَعْ مُسْلِمٌ.
> — الکامل فی التاریخ ج۲ ص۲۰۲ · https://shamela.ws/book/21712/893
> *English:* "The reserve came out at them with skins they had inflated … The Muslims' camels bolted
> with them on their backs and carried them back to Medina — and not one Muslim was thrown."

**عبرت:** The first engagement of the war was a setback, and the book records it without
embarrassment.
**Hands-up?** Before telling it: *"A delegation comes to negotiate, and goes home again. What have
they taken with them besides your answer?"*

---

### RCT/E-RC02 · The counter-attack before dawn
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** ۱۱ھ, the same night as the reverse `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** One blue arrow
from المدينة to **ذو القصّة**, one day's ride out. ذو القصّة changes from red to blue and gets a
garrison marker.
**What happened:** They thought the Muslims were finished. Abū Bakr ؓ spent that night forming his men
up — the three brothers of Muqarrin on the right, the left and the rear. He marched in the dark. At
first light the two forces were standing on the same ground, and the enemy had not heard them come.
The swords were among them before the sun's rim showed. By sunrise they were running. He pursued and
camped at Dhū al-Qaṣṣa, left a detachment there under al-Nuʿmān b. Muqarrin ؓ, and went home.
**The statement:**
> فَمَا طَلَعَ الْفَجْرُ إِلَّا وَهُمْ وَالْعَدُوُّ عَلَى صَعِيدٍ وَاحِدٍ … فَمَا ذَرَّ قَرْنُ الشَّمْسِ حَتَّى وَلَّوْهُمُ الْأَدْبَارَ … وَكَانَ أَوَّلَ الْفَتْحِ.
> — الکامل ج۲ ص۲۰۳ · https://shamela.ws/book/21712/894
> *English:* "Dawn did not break until they and the enemy were on one ground … and the sun's rim had
> not risen before they turned their backs … and that was the first victory."

**عبرت:** The recovery came out of the very night the reverse happened, spent preparing.
**Hands-up?** no

---

### RCT/E-RC03 · Abū Bakr ؓ rides out himself — الأبرق
**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** **جمادى الآخرة ۱۱ھ** `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** The blue arrow extends past
ذو القصّة to **الأبرق**, in the **الرَّبَذة**. الأبرق is shaded as taken ground.
**What happened:** Usāma ؓ came back from the north. Abū Bakr ؓ put him in charge of Medina, told his
troops to rest their mounts, and rode out again himself. The Muslims implored him to stay behind. He
refused. They fought at al-Abraq, in the Rabadha, and the tribes there broke. He then stayed camped
on that ground for some days and declared it forfeit — it became protected pasture for the state's
own animals and its zakāt herds.
**The statement:**
> فَنَاشَدَهُ الْمُسْلِمُونَ لِيُقِيمَ، فَأَبَى وَقَالَ: لَأُوَاسِيَنَّكُمْ بِنَفْسِي.
> — الکامل ج۲ ص۲۰۳ · https://shamela.ws/book/21712/894
> *English:* "The Muslims implored him to stay. He refused and said: I will share with you in my own
> person."

**عبرت:** He asked no man to stand anywhere he had not stood himself.
**Hands-up?** no

---

### RCT/E-RC04 · The rules the first army carried
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** **ربيع الأول ۱۱ھ**, at الجُرْف `[SOURCED]` — البدایہ ج۷ ص۱۲: «أمضى أبو بكر
جيش أسامة بن زيد **في آخر ربيع الأول**» (https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3158) · **Map:** A single long arrow
north from المدينة towards الشام, and back. No battle marker.
**What happened:** Usāma's ؓ army was the last thing the Prophet ﷺ had ordered, and Abū Bakr ؓ sent it
out while Arabia was breaking apart. He walked the army out of camp on foot while its commander rode
— and would not mount when Usāma ؓ protested. Then he gave them their instructions. He was sending
soldiers into a country that had just turned against him, and what he told them was mostly a list of
things they were not to do.
**The statement:**
> لَا تَخُونُوا، وَلَا تَغْدِرُوا، وَلَا تَغُلُّوا، وَلَا تُمَثِّلُوا، وَلَا تَقْتُلُوا طِفْلًا وَلَا شَيْخًا كَبِيرًا وَلَا امْرَأَةً، وَلَا تَعْقِرُوا نَخْلًا وَتُحْرِقُوهُ، وَلَا تَقْطَعُوا شَجَرَةً مُثْمِرَةً، وَلَا تَذْبَحُوا شَاةً وَلَا بَقَرَةً وَلَا بَعِيرًا إِلَّا لِمَأْكَلَةٍ … انْدَفِعُوا بِاسْمِ اللَّهِ.
> — الکامل فی التاریخ ج۲ ص۱۹۶ · https://shamela.ws/book/21712/887
> *English:* "Do not betray; do not break faith; do not steal from the spoils; do not mutilate; do
> not kill a child, nor a very old man, nor a woman; do not cut down a date-palm or burn it; do not
> cut a fruit-bearing tree; do not slaughter a sheep or a cow or a camel except for food … Set out,
> in the name of Allah."

**عبرت:** The first thing the state did when it had almost no strength was to limit what its own
soldiers were allowed to do.
**Hands-up?** Before telling it: *"An army is marching out and the ruler has one minute to speak to
it. What does he say?"*
⚠ **Say "the instruction he gave the first army out" — these were given to Usāma's ؓ force, not to
the eleven commanders.** Read the Arabic only as far as «انْدَفِعُوا بِاسْمِ اللَّهِ»; the intervening
clause on that page is not part of the protective list.

---

### RCT/E-RC05 · Eleven banners at ذو القصّة
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** ۱۱ھ, after Usāma's ؓ army had rested `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** **The evening's
biggest map moment.** One dot at ذو القصّة, one day out from المدينة. Eleven arrows leave it at once —
north-east to نجد, east to اليمامة, north to الشام and to قُضاعة, south-east to البحرين, far east to
عمان, south-east to مَهْرة, south to اليمن and to تِهامة, south to حضرموت. Leave them as outlines;
they fill in one by one across the rest of the evening.
**What happened:** He rode out a third time with his sword drawn, and ʿAlī ؓ walked at his camel's
head. This time the Companions would not let him go on. He agreed, tied eleven banners on that
ground, gave each commander his objective and his written orders, and went back to Medina. Then the
eleven separated — each with his own troops joining him — and rode off in eleven directions from one
spot of desert.
**The statement:**
> فَفَصَلَتِ الْأُمَرَاءُ مِنْ ذِي الْقَصَّةِ، وَلَحِقَ بِكُلِّ أَمِيرٍ جُنْدُهُ، وَعَهِدَ إِلَى كُلِّ أَمِيرٍ.
> — الکامل ج۲ ص۲۰۴ · https://shamela.ws/book/21712/895
> *English:* "The commanders separated from Dhū al-Qaṣṣa, and each commander's troops joined him, and
> he gave each commander his commission."

**عبرت:** The whole war was set moving from one camp, in one sitting, by a man who had just been told
to go home.
**Hands-up?** Ask the room to count with you as each arrow goes up.
⚠ Ibn Kathīr announces eleven and prints ten; **الکامل prints all eleven.** If a listener has counted
ten in some other book, that is why. **Do not print the ninth commander's name — three sources give
three forms of it.**

---

### RCT/E-RC06 · The letter that went ahead of the armies
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** ۱۱ھ, with the eleven `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** Thin dotted lines running ahead
of each of the eleven arrows — messengers arriving before soldiers.
**What happened:** Before the armies came, the letters came. One wording, copied out many times, sent
with messengers who rode ahead of the columns. The messenger was to read it aloud in every assembly
of the tribe. And the letter set a single test that any man could apply from a distance, with no
lawyer and no court. Call the adhān when you halt. If they answer it, stop. If they do not, ask them
what they owe.
**The statement:**
> وقد أمرتُ رسولي أن يقرأ كتابه في كلِّ مَجْمعٍ لكم، والداعية الأذان، فإذا إذَّنَ المسلمون فأذنوا فكُفُّوا عنهم، وإن لم يُؤَذِّنوا فسلوهم ما عليهم.
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۲۴ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3170
> *English:* "I have ordered my messenger to read his letter in every assembly of yours. The summons
> is the adhān: when the Muslims call it and they call it too, hold off from them; and if they do not
> call it, ask them what they owe."

**عبرت:** The test was something the other side could pass, and passing it stopped the war where they
stood.
**Hands-up?** no
⚠ The same letter contains a harsh clause. **Do not quote it and do not deny it.** If raised: it is
on that page, transmitted by Sayf b. ʿUmar; the operative test on the same page is the adhān; and the
question is for the fuqahāʾ. Logged for `QA_BANK.md`.

---

### RCT/E-RC07 · ʿAdī b. Ḥātim ؓ buys three days
**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** ۱۱ھ, before بُزاخة `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** Khālid's ؓ arrow from ذو القصّة
**stops** short of **أجَأ وسَلْمى**, the two mountains of Ṭayyiʾ. A single figure moves ahead of it.
Then Ṭayyiʾ's zone turns from amber to blue, and a thin arrow of a thousand riders joins Khālid ؓ.
**What happened:** Abū Bakr ؓ had sent ʿAdī b. Ḥātim ؓ ahead of the army, to his own tribe. Ṭayyiʾ had
already sent men to Ṭulayḥa. If they changed sides now, Ṭulayḥa would kill their kinsmen in his camp.
So ʿAdī ؓ rode back out to meet Khālid ؓ and asked him to halt the army for three days while Ṭayyiʾ
got their own men out. Khālid ؓ halted. Three days later Ṭayyiʾ came in — and then ʿAdī ؓ asked for
more days, and went and brought in Banū Jadīla as well.
**The statement:**
> اسْتَقْبِلِ الْجَيْشَ فَأَخِّرْهُ عَنَّا حَتَّى نَسْتَخْرِجَ مَنْ عِنْدَ طُلَيْحَةَ مِنَّا؛ لِئَلَّا يَقْتُلَهُمْ … فَتَأَخَّرَ خَالِدٌ.
> — الکامل ج۲ ص۲۰۴ · https://shamela.ws/book/21712/895
> *English:* "Go to meet the army and hold it back from us until we get out those of ours who are
> with Ṭulayḥa, so he does not kill them … And Khālid held back."

**عبرت:** A commander in a hurry gave away three days, and a whole tribe came over instead of being
fought.
**Hands-up?** no
**Also on the page:** when Ṭayyiʾ then offered to fight anyone except Banū Asad, who were their
confederates, ʿAdī ؓ refused the exemption — «وَاللَّهِ لَا أَمْتَنِعُ عَنْ جِهَادِ بَنِي أَسَدٍ لِحِلْفِهِمْ»
(الکامل ج۲ ص۲۰۴–۲۰۵) — and Khālid ؓ told him not to go against his own men's view.

---

### RCT/E-RC08 · The two scouts
**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** ۱۱ھ, approaching بُزاخة `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** Two small markers ahead of
Khālid's ؓ arrow, then two crosses.
**What happened:** Khālid ؓ sent ʿUkkāsha b. Miḥṣan ؓ and Thābit b. Aqram ؓ forward to see what was in
front of him. They ran into Ṭulayḥa's brother Ḥibāl, and killed him. Then Ṭulayḥa and his other
brother came out after them, and killed them both. The army came up the road and found the two of
them lying there.
**The statement:**
> وَأَقْبَلَ خَالِدٌ بِالنَّاسِ فَرَأَوْا عُكَّاشَةَ وَثَابِتًا قَتِيلَيْنِ، فَجَزِعَ لِذَلِكَ الْمُسْلِمُونَ.
> — الکامل ج۲ ص۲۰۴ · https://shamela.ws/book/21712/895
> *English:* "Khālid came up with the men and they saw ʿUkkāsha and Thābit lying killed — and the
> Muslims were shaken by it."

**عبرت:** Two names are all that a whole army's reconnaissance amounted to, and the book keeps both.
**Hands-up?** no
**Needed later:** this is why ʿUmar ؓ told Ṭulayḥa, years afterwards, that he would never love him.

---

### RCT/E-RC09 · بُزاخة — the man who walked off the field
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** ۱۱ھ `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** Khālid's ؓ arrow moves from **أجَأ وسَلْمى** to
**بُزاخة**. A separate red arrow shows Ṭulayḥa moving up from **سُمَيْراء** to بُزاخة. A third block
sits nearby in amber, labelled **بنو عامر — watching**. After the battle: a thin red line runs from
بُزاخة north-west, all the way to **الشام**.
**What happened:** Ṭulayḥa sat wrapped in a cloak, waiting for revelation, while ʿUyayna b. Ḥiṣn did
the fighting with seven hundred men of Fazāra. Nearby, Banū ʿĀmir sat armed and waited to see which
way it would go. Three times ʿUyayna broke off the fighting, walked back to the cloak, and asked
whether Jibrīl had come. Twice the answer was no. The third time it was yes — and then Ṭulayḥa told
him what had been said.
**The statement:**
> قَالَ لِي: إِنَّ لَكَ رَحًا كَرَحَاهُ، وَحَدِيثًا لَا تَنْسَاهُ. فَقَالَ عُيَيْنَةُ: قَدْ عَلِمَ اللَّهُ أَنَّهُ سَيَكُونُ حَدِيثٌ لَا تَنْسَاهُ، انْصَرِفُوا يَا بَنِي فَزَارَةَ فَإِنَّهُ كَذَّابٌ.
> — الکامل فی التاریخ ج۲ ص۲۰۵ · https://shamela.ws/book/21712/896
> *English:* "He said to me: you have a mill like his mill, and a matter you will not forget. ʿUyayna
> said: Allah knows there will be a matter you will not forget. Withdraw, Banū Fazāra — the man is a
> liar."

**عبرت:** The claim collapsed the moment somebody asked it for something specific.
**Hands-up?** Before telling it: *"He had followed this man, and was fighting for him that morning.
What was the question that ended it?"*
**Ṭulayḥa's own exit, same page:** he had a horse ready and a mount for his wife, got away with her,
and called back over his shoulder — «يَا مَعْشَرَ فَزَارَةَ، مَنِ اسْتَطَاعَ أَنْ يَفْعَلَ هَكَذَا وَيَنْجُوَ
بِامْرَأَتِهِ فَلْيَفْعَلْ»: *"whoever can do this and get away with his wife, let him do it."*

---

### RCT/E-RC10 · The terms at Medina, and ʿUmar's ؓ objection
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** ۱۱ھ, after بُزاخة `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** Asad and Ghaṭafān turn from red to
blue. **No captives marker** — none were taken.
**What happened:** The delegation of Buzākha came to Medina asking for terms. Abū Bakr ؓ gave them a
choice of two, and named them: a war of expulsion, or a humbling settlement. They said they knew what
war meant — what was the settlement? He listed it. Weapons and horses surrendered. Compensation for
what you took from us, and none for what we took from you. You testify that our dead are in the
Garden and yours in the Fire. And you pay blood-money for our dead, and we pay none for yours. And
then ʿUmar ؓ interrupted him.
**The statement:**
> فَقَالَ عمر: أما قولك: تدون قتلانا، فإنّ قتلانا قتلوا على أمر الله لا ديات لهم … وقال عمر في الثاني: نعم ما رأيت.
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۲۷ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3173 — **صحيح البخاري ٧٢٢١**
> *English:* "ʿUmar said: as for your saying 'and you pay blood-money for our dead' — our dead were
> killed upon the command of Allah; there is no blood-money for them … And ʿUmar said of the rest:
> excellent is what you have judged."

**عبرت:** The terms were read out in front of the men they applied to, and one clause of them was
struck out in public.
**Hands-up?** no
**Two additions, both on the page:** Ibn Masʿūd ؓ defines the first option — «وَأَمَّا الْحَرْبُ
الْمُجْلِيَةُ فَأَنْ يُخْرَجُوا مِنْ دِيَارِهِمْ» (الکامل ج۲ ص۲۰۱). And **no captives were taken at بُزاخة**:
«وَلَمْ يُؤْخَذْ مِنْهُمْ سَبْيٌ … فَآمَنَهُمْ» (الکامل ج۲ ص۲۰۶).

---

### RCT/E-RC11 · The three things asked of the men who came back
**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** ۱۱ھ, after بُزاخة `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** Hawāzin, Sulaym and ʿĀmir all
change colour at once, with no battle marker.
**What happened:** When Ṭulayḥa was broken, the tribes who had been sitting and watching came in of
their own accord. Khālid ؓ took a pledge from them. It was three items long. Believe in Allah and His
Messenger; establish the prayer; pay the zakāt — and take the same pledge from your sons and your
women. There was nothing in it about the caliph's person and nothing about tribute. And there was one
thing he would not waive: the men who had killed Muslims during the rising were to be handed over.
**The statement:**
> عَلَيْكُمْ عَهْدُ اللَّهِ وَمِيثَاقُهُ لَتُؤْمِنُنَّ بِاللَّهِ وَرَسُولِهِ، وَلَتُقِيمُنَّ الصَّلَاةَ، وَلَتُؤْتُنَّ الزَّكَاةَ، وَتُبَايِعُونَ عَلَى ذَلِكَ أَبْنَاءَكُمْ وَنِسَاءَكُمْ. فَيَقُولُونَ: نَعَمْ.
> — الکامل ج۲ ص۲۰۶ · https://shamela.ws/book/21712/897
> *English:* "Upon you is Allah's covenant and His pact that you will believe in Allah and His
> Messenger, and establish the prayer, and pay the zakāt — and that you will take this pledge from
> your sons and your women. And they would say: yes."

**عبرت:** What was being demanded was short enough to say in one breath, and a chief who had asked to
be excused it a month earlier now said it out loud.
**Hands-up?** no
⚠ **The reprisals against the men who were handed over belong beside this card and must not be
dropped from it** — see §5.4. Deliver in this order: the oath after the killings at ذو القصّة, then
the reprisals in one plain sentence, then ʿUmar's ؓ objection and Abū Bakr's ؓ answer. Then stop.

---

### RCT/E-RC12 · أم زمل
**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** ۱۱ھ, after بُزاخة `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** An unlocated arrow inside the
Ghaṭafān / Ṭayyiʾ country. **Do not place it.**
**What happened:** The men who ran from Buzākha did not go home. They gathered around a woman — Salmā,
daughter of Mālik, the daughter of Umm Qirfa. She had once been a captive in
Medina, and ʿĀʾisha ؓ had set her free; she had gone back to her people, and turned. Fugitives of five
tribes came in to her, and she ordered them to fight — and what had been a rout was an army again.
Khālid ؓ marched on her. She fought mounted on her mother's camel, the camel whose price was a
hundred camels to whoever touched it. They hamstrung it and killed her, and a hundred men were killed
around it.
**The statement:**
> فَأَعْتَقَتْهَا وَرَجَعَتْ إِلَى قَوْمِهَا وَارْتَدَّتْ، وَاجْتَمَعَ إِلَيْهَا الْفَلُّ، فَأَمَرَتْهُمْ بِالْقِتَالِ، وَكَثُفَ جَمْعُهَا وَعَظُمَتْ شَوْكَتُهَا.
> — الکامل ج۲ ص۲۰۷ · https://shamela.ws/book/21712/898
> *English:* "She freed her, and she went back to her people and apostatised. The routed men gathered
> to her and she ordered them to fight, and her gathering grew dense and her strength great."

**عبرت:** A defeat is not finished on the day of the battle; it is finished when the beaten have
nowhere left to gather.
**Hands-up?** no
⚠ Ibn Khaldūn places this at al-Ḥawʾab and attaches a ḥadīth to it. **Neither is in البدایہ or
الکامل. Use neither.**

---

### RCT/E-RC13 · الفُجاءة — the man who asked for weapons
**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** ۱۱ھ `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** An arrow from المدينة south-east into the Sulaym
country; then red raiding arrows in every direction from **الجِواء**; then a blue arrow bringing him
back to المدينة.
**What happened:** He came to Abū Bakr ؓ, said he was a Muslim, and asked to be armed and commissioned
to fight the apostates. He was given weapons, and a command. Then he camped at al-Jiwāʾ, appointed a
deputy over his raiding, and set about plundering every Muslim he could find among Sulaym, ʿĀmir and
Hawāzin. It took two officers to bring him in. His deputy was killed and he ran; Ṭurayfa b. Ḥājiz ran
him down, took him alive, and sent him to Medina.
**The statement:**
> فَلَمَّا قَدِمَ أَمَرَ أَبُو بَكْرٍ أَنْ تُوقَدَ لَهُ نَارٌ فِي مُصَلَّى الْمَدِينَةِ، ثُمَّ رُمِيَ بِهِ فِيهِ مَقْمُوطًا.
> — الکامل ج۲ ص۲۰۷ · https://shamela.ws/book/21712/898
> *English:* "When he arrived, Abū Bakr ordered a fire kindled for him in the prayer-ground of Medina,
> and he was thrown into it bound hand and foot."

**عبرت:** The one man the state armed on his own word did more damage with those weapons than the
tribes it was fighting.
**Hands-up?** Before telling it: *"A stranger arrives, says he is with you, and asks for weapons.
What do you do?"*
⚠ البدایہ ج۷ ص۲۷–۲۸ says البقيع, not the مصلى. **Two sources, two places.** ⚠ **The claim that
Abū Bakr ؓ later regretted this act is not on any page fetched. Do not repeat it.**

---

### RCT/E-RC14 · البُطاح and Mālik b. Nuwayra ؓ — ⚠ SPEAKER'S DISCRETION
**Tier:** GOOD (**cut this one first if time is short**) · **When:** ۱۱ھ `[SOURCED]` · **Map:**
Khālid's ؓ arrow continues from بُزاخة to **البُطاح**, in the Tamīm country. Draw the Anṣār contingent
detached from it for two days, then rejoining.
**What happened:** Khālid's ؓ written orders had named al-Buṭāḥ as his third stop. The Anṣār with him
refused to march: they said their orders ended at Buzākha. He told them he was the commander, that he
would not compel them, and that he was going. They followed two days later. Mālik b. Nuwayra ؓ was at
al-Buṭāḥ, undecided — he had dealt with Sajāḥ and then broken with her, and he had just told his own
tribe to disperse and submit. A patrol brought him in with a group of his men. And then the patrol
itself disagreed about what it had seen. Abū Qatāda ؓ testified that they had given the adhān and had
prayed. Others said they had not. They were killed.
**The statement:**
> فَقَالَ: هِيهِ يَا عُمَرُ! تَأَوَّلَ فَأَخْطَأَ، فَارْفَعْ لِسَانَكَ عَنْ خَالِدٍ، فَإِنِّي لَا أَشِيمُ سَيْفًا سَلَّهُ اللَّهُ عَلَى الْكَافِرِينَ.
> — الکامل فی التاریخ ج۲ ص۲۱۳ · https://shamela.ws/book/21712/904 (the speaker is أبو بكر ؓ,
> answering عمر ؓ's «إِنَّ سَيْفَ خَالِدٍ فِيهِ رَهَقٌ» on the same page; his name is **not** inside the
> sentence — do not put it there)
> *English:* "Abū Bakr said: Enough, ʿUmar! He interpreted and was mistaken. Lift your tongue off
> Khālid — for I do not sheathe a sword Allah has drawn against the disbelievers."

**عبرت:** Four books tell this and they do not tell it the same way, and that is the first thing to
say about it.
**Hands-up?** no — **and take no questions on it from the floor.**

> ⚠ **HOW TO DELIVER, IF AT ALL.** Show the disagreement, not a verdict. Agreed by all four: the
> Anṣār refused and then followed; Mālik ؓ was undecided; the patrol was divided; Abū Qatāda ؓ
> testified they had prayed; they were killed; Khālid ؓ married the widow; ʿUmar ؓ demanded his
> removal; Abū Bakr ؓ paid the blood-money, returned the captives, and kept him in command. Disputed:
> **how** they died — four incompatible accounts at §5.7(d). Say: *"The books do not agree, and where
> they do not agree we record and we stop."* Then use Ibn Kathīr's own sentence — «وإن كان قد اجتهد
> في قتلِ مالك بن نويرة وأخطأَ في قتله» (ج۷ ص۳۲) — and his own comparison to the Prophet ﷺ's handling
> of Khālid ؓ at Banū Jadhīma, where he paid the blood-money, disowned the act, and did not remove
> him either. Close on ʿUmar ؓ and Mutammim consoling each other (الکامل ج۲ ص۲۱۴). **Do not use the
> cooking-pot detail. Do not adjudicate. Defer everything else to the session that handles the
> مشاجرات with اصول.**

---

### RCT/E-RC15 · ʿIkrima ؓ goes too early, and the letter that follows him
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** ۱۱ھ, at اليمامة `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** ʿIkrima's ؓ arrow reaches **اليمامة**
and **recoils**. Shuraḥbīl's ؓ arrow halts short of it on the road. Then a **new, very long line**
starts from that point and runs east to عمان, then west along the southern edge — مَهْرة, أبْيَن,
