حضرموت. **That line is one man and it takes the rest of the evening to finish.**
**What happened:** ʿIkrima ؓ had the banner for Musaylima, and Shuraḥbīl ؓ was sent behind him. ʿIkrima
attacked before Shuraḥbīl came up — Ibn al-Athīr says he wanted to carry off the fame of it alone.
He was beaten. He wrote to Medina and reported it, and waited for his recall. What came back was not
a recall. He was not to come home and demoralise people. He was to keep going: join Ḥudhayfa and
ʿArfaja, fight in Oman and Mahra, and keep marching after that until he met al-Muhājir ؓ in Yemen and
Ḥaḍramawt.
**The statement:**
> لَا أَرَيَنَّكَ وَلَا تَرَانِي، لَا تَرْجِعَنَّ فَتُوهِنَ النَّاسَ، امْضِ إِلَى حُذَيْفَةَ وَعَرْفَجَةَ فَقَاتِلْ أَهْلَ عُمَانَ وَمَهْرَةَ، ثُمَّ تَسِيرُ أَنْتَ وَجُنْدُكَ تَسْتَبْرُونَ النَّاسَ، حَتَّى تَلْقَى مُهَاجِرَ بْنَ أَبِي أُمَيَّةَ بِالْيَمَنِ وَحَضْرَمَوْتَ.
> — الکامل فی التاریخ ج۲ ص۲۱۴ · https://shamela.ws/book/21712/905 → ج۲ ص۲۱۵ ·
> https://shamela.ws/book/21712/906 (it straddles the page break at «بِالْيَمَنِ | وَحَضْرَمَوْتَ»)
> *English:* "Let me not see you and do not you see me. Do not come back and weaken the people. Go on
> to Ḥudhayfa and ʿArfaja and fight the people of ʿUmān and Mahra; then you and your troops march on,
> testing the people, until you meet Muhājir b. Abī Umayya in Yemen and Ḥaḍramawt."

**عبرت:** He was not dismissed for the defeat; he was given more ground to cross.
**Hands-up?** Before telling it: *"A commander loses a battle by moving too soon. What should the
letter from the capital say?"*
**And then it happened again:** Shuraḥbīl ؓ, ordered to wait for Khālid ؓ, attacked ahead of him too
and was also beaten — «وَعَجَّلَ شُرَحْبِيلُ بْنُ حَسَنَةَ … فَنُكِبَ، فَلَامَهُ خَالِدٌ» (الکامل ج۲ ص۲۱۵). **Both
advance commanders were beaten in detail before the main force arrived. That is why al-Yamāma was so
hard.**

---

### RCT/E-RC16 · The tent and the prisoner — عَقْرَباء
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** ۱۱ھ→۱۲ھ `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** Khālid's ؓ arrow runs from البُطاح east to
**اليمامة**. The enemy camp is at **عَقْرَباء**, on the edge of the cultivated country, **with the
farmland and the households behind it**. Show that: the red block has no line of retreat.
**What happened:** Musaylima had about forty thousand fighting men. He camped at ʿAqrabāʾ with the
farmland and the families behind his back — which meant no man in his line could retreat without
running through his own household. The night before, Khālid's ؓ vanguard picked up a raiding party
coming home; he executed them and kept one man alive, because someone in the party had told him to:
if you mean anything at all for the people of Yamāma tomorrow, keep this one. His name was Mujjāʿa b.
Murāra, and he was put in irons in the tent with Khālid's ؓ wife.
**The statement:**
> أيها الرجل إن كنتَ تريد بأهل اليمامة غدًا خيرًا أو شرًا فاستبق هذا الرجل … فاستبقاه خالد مقيدًا، وجعله في الخيمة مع امرأته، وقال: استوصي به خيرًا.
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۳۳ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3179
> *English:* "Man — if you intend anything tomorrow for the people of Yamāma, good or ill, keep this
> man alive … So Khālid kept him, in irons, and put him in the tent with his wife, and said: treat
> him well."

**عبرت:** He kept one prisoner alive on a stranger's advice, and that decision settled the end of the
battle before it started.
**Hands-up?** no

---

### RCT/E-RC17 · The line breaks
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** ۱۱ھ→۱۲ھ, at عَقْرَباء `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** The blue block at عَقْرَباء is
pushed **backwards**. A red spike reaches the marker for Khālid's ؓ own camp.
**What happened:** One of their leaders told them that if they lost, their women would be taken. The
armies collided, and the Muslim line broke. The bedouin contingents ran. Banū Ḥanīfa came through and
into Khālid's ؓ own tent, and were going to kill his wife — and the prisoner in irons in that tent
stood up and gave her his protection. Later in the same day Muslims came into the same tent meaning
to kill him, and she gave him hers.
**The statement:**
> فكانت جولة وانهزمتِ الأعرابُ حتى دخلت بنو حنيفة خيمةَ خالد بن الوليد وهمّوا بقتل أم تميم، حتى أجارها مجاعة وقال: نعمتِ الحُرَّةُ هذه.
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۳۳ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3179
> *English:* "There was a rout: the bedouin broke, until Banū Ḥanīfa entered Khālid b. al-Walīd's own
> tent and were minded to kill Umm Tamīm — until Mujjāʿa gave her his protection and said: This is a
> fine free woman."

**عبرت:** Each of them saved the other's life on the same day, and the book keeps both halves.
**Hands-up?** no
**The mirror, from سیر:** «فَقَالَتْ أُمُّ تَمِيمٍ: وَاللَّهِ لَا يُقْتَلُ، وَأَجَارَتْهُ» — سیر أعلام النبلاء
(سير الخلفاء الراشدين) ص۴۷ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/1195.

---

### RCT/E-RC18 · Thābit b. Qays ؓ puts on his shroud
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** ۱۱ھ→۱۲ھ, at عَقْرَباء `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** No movement. Hold the map still
on عَقْرَباء for this card.
**What happened:** Anas ؓ came to him while the line was going backwards and found him rubbing himself
with burial perfume. He had put on two white garments — the ones he was buried in. Anas ؓ said: do
you not see what is happening? He said: in a moment, nephew. Then he came forward past the men who
were falling back, and told them to get out of his way, and said the thing that everyone in that
generation would have understood. Then he took the Anṣār's banner, dug his feet into the ground to
the middle of his shins, and did not move from there until he was killed.
**The statement:**
> هَكَذَا عَنْ وُجُوْهِنَا نُقَارِعُ القَوْمَ، بِئْسَ مَا عَوَّدْتُم أَقْرَانَكُم، مَا هَكَذَا كُنَّا نُقَاتِلُ مَعَ رَسُوْلِ اللهِ ﷺ.
> — سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۱ ص۳۱۱ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/1737 — **صحيح البخاري ٢٨٤٥**
> *English:* "Get out of our faces so we can strike the enemy. Wretched is what you have accustomed
> your opponents to! This is not how we used to fight alongside the Messenger of Allah ﷺ."

**عبرت:** He did not tell them what to do; he told them what they had already been.
**Hands-up?** no
**On the same field, in the same minutes, all three verbatim from البدایہ ج۷ ص۳۴:** the Muhājirūn
asked Sālim, the freedman of Abū Ḥudhayfa ؓ, whether he feared the line would break from his side —
«**بئس حامل القرآن أنا إذًا**». Zayd b. al-Khaṭṭāb ؓ: «**والله لا أتكلَّمُ حتى يهزمهم اللهُ أو ألقىَ
اللهَ فأكلِّمه بحجَّتي**». Abū Ḥudhayfa ؓ: «**يا أهلَ القرآنِ زيِّنوا القرآنَ بالفِعال**». And the whole
army calling to each other: «**يا أصحاب سورةِ البقرةِ، بَطلَ السِّحْرُ اليوم**» (ج۷ ص۳۳).
**Sālim ؓ is the man the Prophet ﷺ went out at night to listen to** — سیر ج۱ ص۱۶۸.

---

### RCT/E-RC19 · «امتازوا» — the order that turned the day
**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** ۱۱ھ→۱۲ھ, at عَقْرَباء `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** The blue block at عَقْرَباء
splits into separate labelled sections — Muhājirūn, Anṣār, and the tribes each under their own
banner.
**What happened:** Khālid ؓ looked at what was happening to his men and gave an order that had nothing
to do with weapons. Separate out. Every clan under its own banner, fighting under it. He wanted two
things: to know what each group was actually worth, and to know exactly where the line was being
broken. They separated. And once they had, they started saying to one another that today, running
would be shameful.
**The statement:**
> امْتَازُوا أَيُّهَا النَّاسُ لِنَعْلَمَ بَلَاءَ كُلِّ حَيٍّ، وَلِنَعْلَمَ مِنْ أَيْنَ نُؤْتَى … فَلَمَّا امْتَازُوا قَالَ بَعْضُهُمْ لِبَعْضٍ: الْيَوْمَ يُسْتَحَى مِنَ الْفِرَارِ.
> — الکامل فی التاریخ ج۲ ص۲۱۷ · https://shamela.ws/book/21712/908
> *English:* "Separate out, people, so we may know what each clan is worth, and know from where we
> are being broken … and when they had separated, they said to one another: Today, running is
> shameful."

**عبرت:** He did not make them braver; he made it impossible for anyone's failure to be anonymous.
**Hands-up?** Before telling it: *"The line is breaking and you do not know where. What do you
change?"*
⚠ Ibn al-Athīr adds, on the same page, that it did not settle who fought hardest — only that the dead
were more numerous among the Muhājirūn, the Anṣār and the townsmen than among the bedouin.

---

### RCT/E-RC20 · حديقة الموت — al-Barāʾ ؓ over the wall
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** ۱۱ھ→۱۲ھ `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** The red block collapses back from عَقْرَباء
into a small walled enclosure. The gate is drawn shut. Then it opens **from the inside**.
**What happened:** When Banū Ḥanīfa broke, one of their commanders called them into a walled garden
and shut the gate behind them. There were thousands of them inside and the wall was solid. Al-Barāʾ
b. Mālik ؓ — the brother of Anas ؓ, the Prophet's ﷺ servant — told the Muslims to throw him over it.
They refused. He made them do it. They lifted him on a shield raised on spear-shafts until he topped
the wall, and he went down among them alone, and fought his way to the gate, and opened it.
**The statement:**
> يَا مَعْشَرَ الْمُسْلِمِينَ، أَلْقُونِي عَلَيْهِمْ فِي الْحَدِيقَةِ. فَقَالُوا: لَا نَفْعَلُ، فَقَالَ: وَاللَّهِ لَتَطْرَحُنَّنِي عَلَيْهِمْ بِهَا! … وَقَاتَلَ عَلَى الْبَابِ وَفَتَحَهُ لِلْمُسْلِمِينَ.
> — الکامل فی التاریخ ج۲ ص۲۱۸ · https://shamela.ws/book/21712/909
> *English:* "Company of Muslims — throw me over onto them into the garden. They said: we will not.
> He said: By Allah, you will throw me onto them in it! … And he fought at the gate and opened it for
> the Muslims."

**عبرت:** One man asked to be thrown where nobody could follow him, and the whole army came in behind
him through the gate he opened.
**Hands-up?** Before telling it: *"The gate is shut, the wall is solid, and there are thousands
inside. What do you do?"*
**What it cost him — سیر ج۱ ص۱۹۶:** «فَجُرِحَ يَوْمَئِذٍ **بِضْعَةً وَثَمَانِيْنَ جُرْحاً**، وَلِذَلِكَ أَقَامَ
خَالِدُ بنُ الوَلِيْدِ عَلَيْهِ شَهْراً يُدَاوِي جِرَاحَهُ» — *some eighty-odd wounds, and Khālid ؓ stayed a
month over him treating them.* **He survived.** ⚠ Ibn Khaldūn appears to list him among the killed;
سیر contradicts it. **Do not say he died there.**

---

### RCT/E-RC21 · The death of Musaylima, and what Waḥshī ؓ said afterwards
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** ۱۱ھ→۱۲ھ `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** The enclosure fills blue. اليمامة turns from
red to blue.
**What happened:** They got through to him standing in a gap in the wall. Waḥshī b. Ḥarb ؓ — the man
who had killed Ḥamza ؓ at Uḥud, before he was a Muslim — threw his javelin, and it went through him
and out the other side; a second man struck him with a sword and he fell. A woman cried out from the
fort above. And afterwards, when Waḥshī ؓ was asked about the battle, what he said was not about
himself.
**The statement:**
> وَعَنْ وَحْشِيٍّ، قَالَ: لَمْ أَرَ قَطُّ أَصْبَرَ عَلَى الْمَوْتِ مِنْ أَصْحَابِ مُسَيْلِمَةَ، ثُمَّ ذَكَرَ أَنَّهُ شَارَكَ فِي قَتْلِ مُسَيْلِمَةَ.
> — سیر أعلام النبلاء (سير الخلفاء الراشدين) ص۴۸ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/1196
> *English:* "From Waḥshī: I never saw men more steadfast in the face of death than Musaylima's
> companions — then he mentioned that he had a share in killing Musaylima."

**عبرت:** The man who killed their leader is the one the book quotes praising their courage.
**Hands-up?** no
⚠ **Sources differ on the second man**: البدایہ ج۷ ص۳۴ names Abū Dujāna ؓ; الکامل ج۲ ص۲۱۸ says only
"a man of the Anṣār". **Say "a second man came up".** ⚠ **Do not use the anonymous saying on the same
سیر page about "the best of the people of the earth and the worst" — it makes a ranking claim the room
will want to argue with.** Use البدایہ's plain apposition instead: *Waḥshī b. Ḥarb ؓ, who had killed
Ḥamza ؓ.*
**And Khālid ؓ walking the dead with the prisoner** — البدایہ ج۷ ص۳۵: he was shown a body and asked, is
this him? «**لا، والله هذا خيرٌ منه**». Then they came to a small sallow man, and Khālid ؓ said: «**قبَّحكم
الله على اتّباعكم هذا**» — *"May Allah disfigure you for following this."*

---

### RCT/E-RC22 · The treaty he was tricked into, and kept
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** ۱۲ھ `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** The forts of اليمامة, ringed and then unringed.
**What happened:** The forts were still holding, and the Muslims were exhausted. The prisoner —
Mujjāʿa, now the negotiator — went in to consult his people and came back saying they refused the
terms. What he had actually done was dress the women and the old men in armour and put them up on the
walls. Khālid ؓ looked at the battlements, saw them full of heads, and settled. Then the forts were
opened and there was nobody inside but women, children and old men. And then a letter arrived from
Medina ordering him to kill every grown man among them.
**The statement:**
> وَوَصَلَ كِتَابُ أَبِي بَكْرٍ إِلَى خَالِدٍ أَنْ يَقْتُلَ كُلَّ مُحْتَلِمٍ، وَكَانَ قَدْ صَالَحَهُمْ، فَوَفَى لَهُمْ وَلَمْ يَغْدِرْ.
> — الکامل فی التاریخ ج۲ ص۲۱۹ · https://shamela.ws/book/21712/910
> *English:* "And Abū Bakr's letter reached Khālid, ordering him to kill every man who had reached
> puberty — but he had already made terms with them, so he kept faith with them and did not betray
> them."

**عبرت:** He had been deceived into the agreement and he kept it anyway, because the agreement had
already been made.
**Hands-up?** Before telling it: *"You have signed terms. Then you find out you were tricked into
them. Are you still bound?"*
**The exchange:** «**وَيْحَكَ! خَدَعْتَنِي!**» — «**هُمْ قَوْمِي، وَلَمْ أَسْتَطِعْ إِلَّا مَا صَنَعْتُ**»
(الکامل ج۲ ص۲۱۹). ابن خلدون ج۲ ص۵۰۳ confirms the kept treaty independently.
**Pair this card directly with E-RC11.** The same commander, in one campaign: reprisals by fire on
one page, and a treaty honoured against a caliph's written order on another. Say both.

---

### RCT/E-RC23 · Zayd b. al-Khaṭṭāb ؓ, and his brother
**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** ۱۲ھ, **ربيع الأول** `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** No movement.
**What happened:** He was older than ʿUmar ؓ and he had become a Muslim before him. At Badr, ʿUmar ؓ
offered him his own mail coat and he refused it — he said he wanted from martyrdom what his brother
wanted. At al-Yamāma he carried a banner and would not speak until it was over, and he was killed.
When the army came home, ʿUmar ؓ turned on his own son ʿAbdullāh ؓ for coming back alive.
**The statement:**
> وَكَانَ يَقُوْلُ: أَسْلَمَ قَبْلِي، وَاسْتُشْهِدَ قَبْلِي … وَكَانَ يَقُوْلُ: مَا هَبَّتِ الصَّبَا إِلاَّ وَأَنَا أَجِدُ رِيْحَ زَيْدٍ.
> — سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۱ ص۲۹۸ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/1724
> *English:* "He used to say: He became Muslim before me and was martyred before me … And he used to
> say: The east wind never blows but I find in it the scent of Zayd."

**عبرت:** The Companions' grief is recorded as carefully as their victories, and by the same men.
**Hands-up?** no
**The son's answer, on the page** — الکامل ج۲ ص۲۱۹: «**سَأَلَ اللَّهَ الشَّهَادَةَ فَأُعْطِيَهَا، وَجَهَدْتُ
أَنْ تُسَاقَ إِلَيَّ فَلَمْ أُعْطَهَا**» — *"He asked Allah for martyrdom and was given it; I strove for it
to be driven to me and I was not given it."*

---

### RCT/E-RC24 · جُواثى under siege
**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** ۱۱ھ, البحرين `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** البحرين goes red. One dot inside it —
**جُواثى** — stays blue, with a ring around it. Nearby, **عبد القيس** stays blue too.
**What happened:** When al-Mundhir b. Sāwā died, Bahrayn rose. They took al-Qaṭīf and Hajar, drew in
al-Khaṭṭ, sent a force to Dārīn, and made a king of the old Lakhmid line. And they besieged the
Muslims in one village called Juwāthā, and starved them. One of the besieged put a message into four
lines of verse, addressed to Abū Bakr ؓ and to the young men of Medina, and it is preserved.
**The statement:**
> أَلَا أَبْلِغْ أَبَا بَكْرٍ رَسُولًا … وَفِتْيَانَ الْمَدِينَةِ أَجْمَعِينَا
> فَهَلْ لَكُمُ إِلَى قَوْمٍ كِرَامٍ … قُعُودٍ فِي جُوَاثَا مُحْصَرِينَا
> — الکامل ج۲ ص۲۲۲ · https://shamela.ws/book/21712/913 (also البدایہ ج۷ ص۳۸)
> *English:* "Take a message to Abū Bakr, and to all the young men of Medina: have you any thought
> for a noble people, sitting besieged in Juwāthā?"

**عبرت:** The place that held out longest was one village, and its message out was four lines of
verse.
**Hands-up?** no
⚠ **The rising there was not all apostasy:** الکامل ج۲ ص۲۲۲ notes that men who had never been Muslims
joined al-Ḥuṭam — «مِمَّنْ لَمْ يَزَلْ مُشْرِكًا». And nearby, ʿAbd al-Qays held firm behind al-Jārūd ؓ.
**No honest map shades Bahrayn one colour.** ⚠ The last hemistich of the verse differs between the two
books (الصبر / النصر): **read it off a page image before it reaches a slide.**

---

### RCT/E-RC25 · The sixteen riders
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** ۱۱ھ `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** A **thin** arrow leaves المدينة for البحرين and
gets visibly **thicker** at each point on the road, then crosses the **الدَّهْناء** and reaches
**هَجَر**.
**What happened:** Al-ʿAlāʾ b. al-Ḥaḍramī ؓ left Medina with sixteen riders and a letter. The letter
said that every Muslim he passed on the road was to march out with him. And that is what happened.
Thumāma b. Uthāl ؓ joined him with the Muslims of Banū Ḥanīfa. Qays b. ʿĀṣim joined him and brought
the zakāt he had held back. ʿAmr, the Abnāʾ, Saʿd and the Rabāb joined — a body the size of the one
he already had. A column of sixteen crossed the sand desert as an army.
**The statement:**
> فَخَرَجَ مِنَ المَدِيْنَةِ فِي سِتَّةَ عَشَرَ رَاكِباً، وَكَتَبَ لَهُ كِتَاباً: أَنْ يَنْفِرَ مَعَهُ كُلُّ مَنْ مَرَّ بِهِ مِنَ المُسْلِمِيْنَ إِلَى عَدُوِّهِم.
> — سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۱ ص۲۶۴ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/1690
> *English:* "He left Medina with sixteen riders, and he wrote him a letter: that every Muslim he
> passed should march out with him against their enemy."

**عبرت:** The state had almost no soldiers to send; what it sent instead was a commander and a piece
of writing.
**Hands-up?** Before telling it: *"You have sixteen men and a province in revolt. What do you send?"*
**On the road:** in the middle of the Dahnāʾ the camels bolted at night with the water, the food and
the tents, and the men were left in open sand. He told them — «**لَنْ تُرَاعُوا، أَنْتُمُ الْمُسْلِمُونَ،
وَفِي سَبِيلِ اللَّهِ، وَأَنْصَارُ اللَّهِ، فَأَبْشِرُوا، فَوَاللَّهِ لَنْ تُخْذَلُوا**» (الکامل ج۲ ص۲۲۳), and prayed
with them until sunrise, and البدایہ ج۷ ص۳۹ says water appeared beside them and the camels came back
with nothing missing. **Narrate it as what the book narrates; do not build a doctrinal point on it.**

---

### RCT/E-RC26 · The trench month, and the nephew in the enemy camp
**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** ۱۱ھ, البحرين `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** Two trench lines facing each other
between **هَجَر** and **القَطيف**, for a month. Then the red line collapses.
**What happened:** Both sides dug in and fought for a month, going out and coming back to their
trenches. One night the Muslims heard uproar in the enemy camp. ʿAbdullāh b. Ḥadhf volunteered to go
and find out what it was, and was captured. His mother was of ʿIjl — so he shouted for his uncle by
name, and his uncle came and recognised him, and fed him, and gave him a camel, and let him out. The
uncle was too drunk to think about it. He came back and told al-ʿAlāʾ ؓ that the whole enemy camp was
drunk.
**The statement:**
> فَدَخَلَ عَسْكَرَ الْمُسْلِمِينَ فَأَخْبَرَهُمْ أَنَّ الْقَوْمَ سُكَارَى، فَخَرَجَ الْمُسْلِمُونَ عَلَيْهِمْ، فَوَضَعُوا فِيهِمُ السَّيْفَ كَيْفَ شَاءُوا.
> — الکامل ج۲ ص۲۲۳ · https://shamela.ws/book/21712/914
> *English:* "He entered the Muslims' camp and told them that the people were drunk. So the Muslims
> went out at them and put the sword into them as they pleased."

**عبرت:** A month of trenches was decided by one night on which one side could not think.
**Hands-up?** no
**The chief's death, and the regret** — البدایہ ج۷ ص۳۹: al-Ḥuṭam woke in confusion, his stirrup-leather
parted, a man offered to fix it and cut his foot off; he lay asking every passer-by to finish him, and
when Qays b. ʿĀṣim finally did and then saw the leg, he said: «**واسوأتاه، لو أعلمُ ما به لم
أحرِّكْهُ**» — *"What a shameful thing; had I known his state I would not have touched him."*

---

### RCT/E-RC27 · دارين — into the water
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** ۱۱ھ→۱۲ھ `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** The survivors take ship from the البحرين
coast to **دارين** — a crossing of a day and a night by sea. Blue lines seal every land road first.
Then a blue arrow goes **straight across the water** and comes straight back the same day.
**What happened:** The beaten took ship for Dārīn, an anchorage a day and a night out from the shore.
Al-ʿAlāʾ ؓ did not chase them straight away. He first wrote to the Muslims of Bakr b. Wāʾil to sit on
every road and close the land behind him. Then he brought his men to the water's edge — where the
ships would have been too slow — and told them what he intended.
**The statement:**
> قَدْ أَرَاكُمُ اللَّهُ مِنْ آيَاتِهِ فِي الْبَرِّ لِتَعْتَبِرُوا بِهَا فِي الْبَحْرِ، فَانْهَضُوا إِلَى عَدُوِّكُمْ وَاسْتَعْرِضُوا الْبَحْرَ.
> — الکامل فی التاریخ ج۲ ص۲۲۴ · https://shamela.ws/book/21712/915
> *English:* "Allah has shown you of His signs on land so that you may take heed of them at sea. Rise
> against your enemy, and go straight across the sea."

**عبرت:** He closed the roads behind the enemy before he opened one in front of himself.
**Hands-up?** no
**Both books describe the crossing the same way** — «يمشون على مثل رملةٍ دمثةٍ فوقها ماء لا يغمر أخفاف
الإبل» (البدایہ ج۷ ص۴۰), out and back in a single day, with nothing lost in the water except one
man's horse-fodder, which al-ʿAlāʾ ؓ went back and brought him. البدایہ ج۷ ص۴۰ prints the duʿāʾ they
said going in. And afterwards: «**وَضَرَبَ الْإِسْلَامُ فِيهَا بِجِرَانِهِ**» (الکامل ج۲ ص۲۲۴).

---

### RCT/E-RC28 · Thumāma b. Uthāl ؓ and the embroidered cloak
**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** ۱۲ھ, after دارين `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** A short return arrow from دارين
that stops.
**What happened:** He had held part of Banū Ḥanīfa against Musaylima while Musaylima was at his
strongest, and then joined al-ʿAlāʾ ؓ on the road to Bahrayn. Al-ʿAlāʾ ؓ gave him a share of the
spoils for it: al-Ḥuṭam's own embroidered cloak, the one the man used to show off in. Coming back
from Dārīn he was seen wearing it by Banū Qays b. Thaʿlaba, and they decided it meant he had killed
their chief.
**The statement:**
> فَقَالُوا لَهُ: أَنْتَ قَتَلْتَ الْحُطَمَ! فَقَالَ: لَمْ أَقْتُلْهُ، وَلَكِنِّي اشْتَرَيْتُهَا مِنَ الْمَغْنَمِ. فَوَثَبُوا عَلَيْهِ فَقَتَلُوهُ.
> — الکامل فی التاریخ ج۲ ص۲۲۴ · https://shamela.ws/book/21712/915
> *English:* "They said to him: You killed al-Ḥuṭam! He said: I did not kill him — I bought it from
> the spoils. So they fell on him and killed him."

**عبرت:** He survived the war and was killed for a piece of cloth by men who had already surrendered.
**Hands-up?** no
⚠ **This is not in البدایہ. Cite الکامل ج۲ ص۲۲۴ (and ابن خلدون ج۲ ص۵۰۶) only.**

---

### RCT/E-RC29 · دَبا — the relief that came from inside the theatre
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** ۱۱ھ→۱۲ھ, عُمان `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** Three arrows converge at **رِجام**,
near Oman, then move together on **دَبا**. The legitimate rulers are shown holding out at **صُحار**.
At the crisis, **two short arrows come in from inside the region itself** — Banū Nājiya and ʿAbd
al-Qays. **Not a long arrow from the north.**
**What happened:** Oman had gone to a man who called himself Dhū al-Tāj and claimed prophethood, and
he had pushed the two lawful rulers to the edges of the country. One of them wrote to Abū Bakr ؓ and
asked for help — so this front began with a request, not an invasion. Three Muslim columns joined
before they arrived, wrote to the rulers, and also wrote to the rebel's own chiefs, some of whom left
him. Then they fought at Dabā, the country's great market town. And they very nearly lost it.
**The statement:**
> وَاسْتَعْلَى لَقِيطٌ، وَرَأَى الْمُسْلِمُونَ الْخَلَلَ، وَرَأَى الْمُشْرِكُونَ الظَّفَرَ. فَبَيْنَمَا هُمْ كَذَلِكَ جَاءَتِ الْمُسْلِمِينَ مَوَادُّهُمُ الْعُظْمَى مِنْ بَنِي نَاجِيَةَ … وَمِنْ عَبْدِ الْقَيْسِ.
> — الکامل فی التاریخ ج۲ ص۲۲۶ · https://shamela.ws/book/21712/917
> *English:* "Laqīṭ got the upper hand; the Muslims saw the breach and the mushrikūn saw victory. And
> while they were in that state, the Muslims' great relief came in — from Banū Nājiya … and from
> ʿAbd al-Qays."

**عبرت:** The men who saved that day were from the same country as the men they were fighting.
**Hands-up?** Before telling it: *"The column from Medina is losing. Who is close enough to help?"*
⚠ Ibn Kathīr ج۷ ص۴۱ says the Oman claimant **claimed prophethood**; الکامل is more guarded. **Oman is
not a clean case of purely political secession.**

---

### RCT/E-RC30 · مَهْرة — the front won with a letter
**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** ۱۱ھ→۱۲ھ `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** ʿIkrima's ؓ long line continues from عمان
west to **مَهْرة**. The red zone there is drawn **split in two** from the start.
**What happened:** ʿIkrima ؓ came into Mahra and found not one enemy but two — two forces under two
chiefs who would not work together. So he wrote to the smaller one. That man answered, came over, and
became a Muslim; and with him the Muslims were strengthened and the other man's nerve failed. Then
ʿIkrima ؓ called that one too, and he refused, and the fighting that followed was harder than Dabā
had been. Afterwards, the fifth of the spoils was sent to Abū Bakr ؓ — carried to Medina by the man
who had been leading half the rebellion.
**The statement:**
> وهما مختلفان، وكان هذا الاختلاف رحمة على المؤمنين، فراسل عكرمةُ شخريت فأجابه وانضاف إلى عكرمة فقوي بذلك المسلمون، وضعف جأش المصبح.
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۴۲ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3188
> *English:* "The two were at odds, and that discord was a mercy upon the believers. ʿIkrima wrote to
> Shikhrīt, and he answered him and joined ʿIkrima — and by that the Muslims were strengthened, and
> al-Muṣabbaḥ's nerve weakened."

**عبرت:** He wrote before he marched, and half the enemy stopped being the enemy.
**Hands-up?** no

---

### RCT/E-RC31 · صنعاء — the two women in the street
**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** ۱۱ھ, اليمن `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** صنعاء goes red. A thin blue line escapes
it to **جبل خَوْلان**. Two convoys leave — one overland, one by sea from **عَدَن** — and both are
intercepted and turned back.
**What happened:** Qays b. Makshūḥ had been one of the three men who killed al-Aswad al-ʿAnsī. After
the Prophet ﷺ died he wanted Yemen for himself, and turned. He made a meal and invited the other two
separately. Dādhawayh came first and was killed. Fayrūz al-Daylamī ؓ was walking to the same house
when he passed two women talking. Then Qays took the families of the Abnāʾ and deported them — one
party overland, one by ship from Aden — and Fayrūz ؓ sent to ʿUqayl and to ʿAkk for help, and both of
them rode out and brought the families back.
**The statement:**
> فَلَمَّا دَنَا مِنْهُ سَمِعَ امْرَأَتَيْنِ تَتَحَدَّثَانِ، فَقَالَتْ إِحْدَاهُمَا: هَذَا مَقْتُولٌ كَمَا قُتِلَ دَاذَوَيْهِ، فَخَرَجَ.
> — الکامل ج۲ ص۲۲۸ · https://shamela.ws/book/21712/919
> *English:* "When he came near, he heard two women talking, and one of them said: this one will be
> killed just as Dādhawayh was killed — so he got out."

**عبرت:** He was saved by a sentence spoken by someone who was not talking to him.
**Hands-up?** no
**Also on the page:** Abū Bakr ؓ wrote to five Yemeni chiefs by name asking for support; Qays wrote to
the same men asking them to kill the Abnāʾ. «**فَلَمْ يُجِيبُوهُ وَلَمْ يَنْصُرُوا الْأَبْنَاءَ**» — *they
answered neither.* **Draw that neutral middle on the map.**

---

### RCT/E-RC32 · Abū Bakr ؓ judges two men who fought against him
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** ۱۱ھ→۱۲ھ `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** Two prisoner markers move from صنعاء to
المدينة — and then **back again** to their own tribes.
**What happened:** Qays b. Makshūḥ and ʿAmr b. Maʿdī Karib were taken and sent to Medina as prisoners.
One had killed a Muslim officer and led a revolt; the other had followed a false prophet and been
beaten and captured more than once. Abū Bakr ؓ rebuked them both. They made their excuses. And then he
accepted what they said openly, left what was inside them to Allah, released them, and sent them home
to their own people.
**The statement:**
> فعنَّفَهما وأنّبهما، فاعتذرا إليه فقبل منهما علانيتهما، ووكل سرائرهما إلى الله ﷿، وأطلقَ سراحهما وردّهما إلى قومهما.
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۴۳ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3189
> *English:* "He rebuked them and reproved them; they excused themselves to him, and he accepted
> their outward profession from them, entrusted their inward states to Allah, released them, and sent
> them back to their people."

**عبرت:** The state judged what it could see and left what it could not see to the One who sees it.
**Hands-up?** Before telling it: *"The men who fought you are standing in front of you and they say
they have come back. What can you actually check?"*
**The words to ʿAmr, from الکامل ج۲ ص۲۲۹:** «**أَمَا تَسْتَحِي أَنَّكَ كُلَّ يَوْمٍ مَهْزُومٌ أَوْ مَأْسُورٌ؟ لَوْ
نَصَرْتَ هَذَا الدِّينَ لَرَفَعَكَ اللَّهُ**» — *"Are you not ashamed that you are every day either routed or
taken prisoner? Had you helped this dīn, Allah would have raised you."*
**This is the settlement policy of the whole war in one card. Leaders were reintegrated; killers were
not.**

---

### RCT/E-RC33 · The she-camel called Shadhra
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** ۱۱ھ→۱۲ھ, حضرموت `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** حضرموت, which has been quiet all
evening, goes red — **and the trigger is one animal.**
**What happened:** Ziyād b. Labīd ؓ was collecting the zakāt in Kinda. A man handed over a young
she-camel and Ziyād ؓ branded her. Then it turned out the animal was not his to give — she belonged
to his brother, her name was Shadhra, and he had brought her by mistake. The brother said so. The
first man agreed and asked for her back. Ziyād ؓ refused: she had already passed into the right of
Allah. Then a third man rode up, told him to release her and take another, and when Ziyād ؓ said he
had no way to do that, said one sentence — and untied her hobble himself and stood in front of her.
**The statement:**
> فَقَالَ حَارِثَةُ: ذَاكَ إِذَا كُنْتَ يَهُودِيًّا، وَأَطْلَقَ عِقَالَهَا وَبَعَثَهَا وَقَامَ دُونَهَا.
> — الکامل فی التاریخ ج۲ ص۲۳۰ · https://shamela.ws/book/21712/921
> *English:* "Ḥāritha said: That would be so if you were a Jew — and he untied her hobble and drove
> her off and stood in front of her."

**عبرت:** A province went to war over one animal, a naming mistake and a public insult, and every
part of that is on one page.
**Hands-up?** Before telling it: *"How big does a quarrel have to be to start a war?"*
⚠ **البدایہ carries no Ḥaḍramawt campaign at all. Cite الکامل ج۲ ص۲۲۹–۲۳۲, never البدایہ, for
everything in this card and the next.** The underlying grievance was administrative: the Prophet ﷺ
had ordered some of Ḥaḍramawt's zakāt placed in Kinda and some of Kinda's in Ḥaḍramawt, and after his
death nobody would carry it (الکامل ج۲ ص۲۳۰).

---

### RCT/E-RC34 · النُّجَير — the name he forgot to write
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** **the last siege of the war** `[SOURCED as sequence]` — الکامل prints it
immediately **before** «ثم دخلت سنة اثنتي عشرة» (ج۲ ص۲۳۳), i.e. still inside ۱۱ھ by its own
arrangement; **no page fixes the month, and no page puts it in ۱۲ھ** (§12.2, §X item 2) · **Map:** Ziyād ؓ and
al-Muhājir ؓ converge and break Kinda at **مَحْجَر الزُّرْقان**; the survivors run to **النُّجَير**, a
strong hill fort. ʿIkrima's ؓ long line — the one that started at اليمامة in E-RC15 — **arrives here
and closes the last road.** The map's final arrow.
**What happened:** Kinda was broken in the field and shut itself into the fort of al-Nujayr. The
Muslims closed every road. When the siege bit, al-Ashʿath b. Qays came out with nine men and offered
to open the gate in exchange for a written safe-conduct for himself, his household and them. He was
told to write whatever he wanted and bring it to be sealed. He wrote it. He opened the gate. And when
it was over and the document was produced, every man named in it was spared — and his own name was
not in it.
**The statement:**
> فَأَجَارَ مَنْ فِي الْكِتَابِ، فَإِذَا الْأَشْعَثُ لَيْسَ مِنْهُمْ، فَقَالَ الْمُهَاجِرُ: الْحَمْدُ لِلَّهِ الَّذِي خَطَّأَ فَاكَ يَا أَشْعَثُ.
> — الکامل فی التاریخ ج۲ ص۲۳۲ · https://shamela.ws/book/21712/923
> *English:* "Every man in the document was spared — and al-Ashʿath was not among them. Al-Muhājir
> said: Praise be to Allah, who made your mouth err, Ashʿath."

**عبرت:** He wrote a safe-conduct for everyone he was bargaining with and left himself off it.
**Hands-up?** Before telling it: *"He is allowed to write his own terms. What is the one thing he
must not forget?"*
**And then Abū Bakr ؓ pardoned him anyway** and restored his wife (الکامل ج۲ ص۲۳۲; سیر ج۲ ص۳۹).
⚠ **الکامل gives a second version on the same page in which he betrayed his companions deliberately,
and his own tribeswomen named him for it. Record both. Adjudicate neither.**

---

### RCT/E-RC35 · The captives bought back
**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** in ʿUmar's ؓ caliphate `[SOURCED]` — «لَمَّا وَلِيَ عُمَرُ»; **the year is
not given on the page, so do not put one on the slide** · **Map:** Nothing moves.
Show the peninsula in one colour for the first time, with نجران marked separately as a covenanted
community.
**What happened:** When ʿUmar ؓ became caliph he said that it was a shameful thing for Arabs to own
one another. He consulted about ransoming the captives taken in these wars and set a price — six or
seven camels a head — and made it lighter for Ḥanīfa and Kinda, because so many of their men had been
killed. Then the women were sought out wherever they had been taken.
**The statement:**
> إِنَّهُ لَقَبِيحٌ بِالْعَرَبِ أَنْ يَمْلِكَ بَعْضُهُمْ بَعْضًا
> — الکامل فی التاریخ ج۲ ص۲۳۲ · https://shamela.ws/book/21712/923 (the ransom terms run on to
> ج۲ ص۲۳۳ · https://shamela.ws/book/21712/924)
> *English:* "It is a shameful thing for the Arabs that some of them should own others."

**عبرت:** The war's last act was the state buying back the people its own victory had taken.
**Hands-up?** no

---

### RCT/E-RC36 · What Ibn Kathīr says the whole war was
**Tier:** CORE — **this is the closing slide** · **When:** «أواخر سنة إحدى عشرة وأوائل سنة ثنتي عشرة»
`[SOURCED]` · **Map:** The completed map. All eleven arrows filled in, every front resolved, نجران in
its own colour as a covenanted community, and the date bracket across the bottom: **۱۱ھ → ۱۲ھ**.
**What happened:** When Ibn Kathīr finishes the whole account, he stops and sums it up himself. He
does not describe a conquest. He says there was no region of Arabia in which some people did not turn
— and that Abū Bakr ؓ sent armies and commanders to be a support to whichever believers were already
in that region. The fifth of the spoils came back to Medina and was spent among the people. And it
went on that way until there was nobody left in the peninsula except people in obedience, and people
under a covenant of protection — like the people of Najrān, who had sent a delegation to renew theirs
while Muslim tribes were breaking theirs.
**The statement:**
> ما من ناحية من جزيرة العرب إلا وحصلَ في أهلها ردَّةٌ لبعض الناس، فبعثَ الصدِّيقُ إليهم جيوشًا وأمراء يكونون عونًا لمن في تلك الناحية من المؤمنين … ولم يزل الأمر كذلك حتى لم يبق بجزيرة العرب إلَّا أهل طاعة للّه ولرسوله، وأهل ذمة من الصديق، كأهل نجران وما جرى مجراهم ولله الحمد.
> وعامة ما وقع من هذه الحروب كان في أواخر سنة إحدى عشرة وأوائل سنة ثنتي عشرة.
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۴۳ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3189
> *English:* "There was no region of the Arabian peninsula in which apostasy did not occur among some
> of its people; so al-Ṣiddīq sent them armies and commanders to be a support to whichever believers
> were in that region … And matters continued so until there remained in the peninsula none but
> people obedient to Allah and His Messenger, and people under al-Ṣiddīq's protection, such as the
> people of Najrān. The general run of these wars was in the last part of the year 11 and the first
> part of the year 12."

**عبرت:** The armies were sent to hold up people who were already standing, and the whole thing was
over in a little more than a year.
**Hands-up?** no

---

### RCT/E-RC37 · The Qurʾān was collected because of this
**Tier:** CORE — **the true ending** · **When:** **۱۲ھ** `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** The Line, not the map.
Put a single mark on ۱۲ھ.
**What happened:** So many of the reciters were killed at al-Yamāma that Abū Bakr ؓ ordered the Qurʾān
gathered — from the flat stones and the palm-stalks and the memories of men — so that it would not go
with them. Two of the four books say it in the same place, and both of them point at al-Bukhārī for
it.
**The statement:**
> فيها أمرَ الصديق زيدَ بن ثابتٍ أن يجمعَ القرآن من اللِّخاف والعُسُب وصدور الرجال، وذلك بعد ما استَحَرّ القتلُ في القُرّاء يوم اليمامة كما ثبت به الحديث في صحيح البخاري.
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۷۹ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3225 — **صحيح البخاري ٤٩٨٦**
> *English:* "In it, al-Ṣiddīq ordered Zayd b. Thābit to gather the Qurʾān from the flat stones and
> the palm-stalks and the breasts of men — and that was after killing had grown severe among the
> reciters on the day of al-Yamāma, as the ḥadīth in Ṣaḥīḥ al-Bukhārī establishes."

**عبرت:** The mushaf the room reads from was gathered because of the men who were killed in the
garden.
**Hands-up?** no — end here and go straight to the closing bookend.
**الکامل ج۲ ص۲۱۹, in the same place:** «أَمَرَ أَبُو بَكْرٍ بِجَمْعِ الْقُرْآنِ لِمَا رَأَى مِنْ كَثْرَةِ مَنْ
قُتِلَ مِنَ الصَّحَابَةِ؛ **لِئَلَّا يَذْهَبَ الْقُرْآنُ**».

---

### RCT/E-RC38 · ʿUmar ؓ to Quraysh — the framing card
**Tier:** CORE (**use at the START of the evening, not the end**) · **When:** ۱۱ھ, at المدينة
`[SOURCED]` · **Map:** The whole peninsula, before any arrow is drawn.
**What happened:** ʿAmr b. al-ʿĀṣ ؓ came back from Oman and reported that there were armed camps all
the way from Dabā to Medina. The senior Companions broke into knots, talking. ʿUmar ؓ walked past one
of them and they went quiet. He asked what they were discussing, and they would not answer, so he
said it for them: you are saying, how afraid we are for Quraysh, from the Arabs. They said: that is
true. And then he turned it round.
**The statement:**
> أَنَا وَاللَّهِ مِنْكُمْ عَلَى الْعَرَبِ أَخْوَفُ مِنِّي مِنَ الْعَرَبِ عَلَيْكُمْ، وَاللَّهِ لَوْ تَدْخُلُونَ، مَعَاشِرَ قُرَيْشٍ، جُحْرًا لَدَخَلَتْهُ الْعَرَبُ فِي آثَارِكُمْ، فَاتَّقُوا اللَّهَ فِيهِمْ.
> — الکامل فی التاریخ ج۲ ص۲۰۹ · https://shamela.ws/book/21712/900
> *English:* "By Allah, I fear you for the Arabs more than I fear the Arabs for you. By Allah, if you
> — company of Quraysh — went into a hole, the Arabs would go into it after you. So fear Allah
> concerning them."

**عبرت:** He turned the question round: the danger was not what would be done to them, but what would
be followed because of them.
**Hands-up?** Before telling it: *"The whole country is in arms against you. Who are you most worried
about?"*
**Pair it with the demand, said plainly by a chief who had not yet chosen a side** — قُرّة b. Hubayra
to ʿAmr ؓ over dinner: «**إِنَّ الْعَرَبَ لَا تَطِيبُ لَكُمْ نَفْسًا بِالْإِتَاوَةِ**» (الکامل ج۲ ص۲۰۸), *"the
Arabs will not give you their property willingly as tribute."* **That is what E-RC11's three-item
pledge answers.**

---

### RCT/E-RC39 · The men of Badr who were kept out of office
**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** ۱۱ھ `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** None.
**What happened:** Abū Bakr ؓ had a policy about who did not get commands. He would not put the men of
Badr into office. He said he left them so they would meet Allah with their good deeds, and that Allah
wards off harm through such men more than He gives victory through them. And the book records, in the
same breath and without embarrassment, that ʿUmar ؓ took the opposite view and held that they should
be employed.
**The statement:**
> لَا أَسْتَعْمِلُ أَهْلَ بَدْرٍ، أَدَعُهُمْ حَتَّى يَلْقَوُا اللَّهَ بِصَالِحِ أَعْمَالِهِمْ، فَإِنَّ اللَّهَ يَدْفَعُ بِهِمْ وَبِالصَّالِحِينَ أَكْثَرَ مِمَّا يَنْتَصِرُ بِهِمْ. وَكَانَ عُمَرُ يَرَى اسْتِعْمَالَهُمْ عَلَى الْجُنْدِ وَغَيْرِهِ.
> — الکامل فی التاریخ ج۲ ص۲۱۵ · https://shamela.ws/book/21712/906
> *English:* "I do not put the men of Badr into office. I leave them so they may meet Allah with
> their good deeds — for Allah wards off by them and by the righteous more than He gives victory
> through them. And ʿUmar held that they should be employed over the troops and otherwise."

**عبرت:** Two men who agreed about the war disagreed about who should run it, and the book records
both without making a quarrel of it.
**Hands-up?** no
**This is the safest possible demonstration of how the sources handle disagreement** — a real
difference of judgement between the first two caliphs, recorded plainly, with nothing at stake.

---

### RCT/E-RC40 · The three-colour tribe — CUT, recorded so it is never re-researched
**Tier:** CUT · **When:** ۱۱ھ · **Map:** Banū Tamīm in three shades.
**What happened:** Already fully worked in `arabian-tribes-and-the-ridda-setup.md` §3.5, from
البدایہ ج۷ ص۲۸: some of Banū Tamīm apostatised and withheld the zakāt; some sent their ṣadaqāt in to
Medina; and some waited to see. **This note adds only the collectors' names and the mechanism** —
الکامل ج۲ ص۲۰۹ gives the Prophet's ﷺ seven collectors among Tamīm by name and shows Qays b. ʿĀṣim
holding his back purely to spite al-Zibriqān, then regretting it and bringing it out when al-ʿAlāʾ ؓ
came past. **Use the setup note's card, not a new one.**
**The statement:** none additional in the sources we use.
**عبرت:** —
**Hands-up?** no

---

### RCT/E-RC41 · The reprisals, and the objection that was spoken aloud
**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** ۱۱ھ, after بُزاخة `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** n/a
**What happened:** Khālid ؓ accepted no submission from Asad, Ghaṭafān, Ṭayyiʾ, Sulaym and ʿĀmir until they
handed over the men who had burned and mutilated Muslims during the rising. When those men were handed
over, he put them to death by fire, by stoning, and by throwing them from heights — and he wrote to
Abū Bakr ؓ to tell him what he had done. Ibn Kathīr gives the reason in one clause: so that the
apostate Arabs who heard of it would take warning. A separate report in al-Dhahabī, about Banū Sulaym,
records that ʿUmar ؓ objected to Abū Bakr ؓ — and records Abū Bakr's ؓ answer.
**The statement:**
> … فَقَالَ عُمَرُ لأَبِي بَكْرٍ: أَتَدَعُ رَجُلاً يُعَذِّبُ بِعَذَابِ اللهِ؟ قَالَ: وَاللهِ لاَ أَشِيْمُ سَيْفاً سَلَّهُ اللهُ عَلَى عَدُوِّهِ
> — سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۱ ص۳۷۲ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/1798
> *English:* "ʿUmar said to Abū Bakr: Will you leave a man who punishes with the punishment of Allah? He
> said: By Allah, I will not sheathe a sword Allah has drawn against His enemy."
**عبرت:** The first objection to the severity came from inside the leadership, and it was said aloud and answered.
**Hands-up?** no

⚠ Speaker's discretion. Handle exactly as ridda-campaign note §5.4 prescribes: the terms first (the
men handed over were those who had burned and mutilated Muslims), then the reprisals in one plain
sentence, then ʿUmar's ؓ objection and Abū Bakr's ؓ answer — and stop. Do not defend, excuse or
adjudicate. al-Dhahabī's editor marks the Sulaym report **mursal**. The same answer, «لا أشيم
سيفًا», is also given about مالك بن نويرة (card E-RC14) — two incidents; do not let the room hear
them as one. Full answer for a written slip: docs/catalogue/QA_BANK.md §6.3.
Sources: الکامل ج۲ ص۲۰۶–۲۰۷ (21712/897–898) · البدایہ ج۷ ص۲۷ · سیر ج۱ ص۳۷۲ (10906/1798)

---

## جيش أسامة ؓ, and the Qur'an collected

*Source note: [`abu-bakr-usama-and-the-jam-of-the-quran.md`](../docs/research/abu-bakr-usama-and-the-jam-of-the-quran.md) — read it for the pages behind these cards.*

### ABU/E-U1 · The appointment nobody liked
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** in the Prophet's ﷺ lifetime, ۱۱ھ `[SOURCED]` (no page read gives the
month) · **Map:** a pin at
**المدینہ**, and an arrow drawn north-west toward **البلقاء / أُبنى**, well past تبوك — the first
time the map points at Byzantine ground.
**What happened:** In his last weeks the Prophet ﷺ raised an army for the Syrian frontier. He put
أسامة بن زيد ؓ over it. أسامة ؓ was eighteen. In the ranks under him stood عمر بن الخطاب ؓ and the
senior men of the مهاجرون and the أنصار. People said so out loud. The Prophet ﷺ heard it, stood up,
and answered them himself.
**The statement:**
> إن تَطْعُنوا في إمارَتِهِ فَقَدْ كُنْتُمْ تَطْعُنون في إمارةِ أبيه منْ قَبْلُ، وايْمُ اللَّهِ إن كان لخليقًا للإمارة، وإن كان لمنْ أحَبِّ النّاس إليَّ، وإنَّ هذا لمِنْ أحبِّ الناسِ إليَّ بَعْدَه
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۵ ص۳۱۱ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/2495
> *English:* "If you find fault with his command, you found fault with his father's command before
> him. And by Allah, he was fit for command, and he was among the dearest of people to me — and this
> one is among the dearest of people to me after him."
**عبرت:** A man is fit for a task because of what he can do, not because of how long he has been
alive.
**Hands-up?** Ask the room: *how old do you have to be before people will take an order from you?*
Then give the number.

---

### ABU/E-U2 · The crier, the morning after
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** the day after the Prophet ﷺ died, ۱۱ھ `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** المدینہ,
with **الجُرْف** marked just outside it — the mustering ground.
**What happened:** The Prophet ﷺ was buried. The bayʿah was taken. And the very next morning a
crier went through المدینہ. Not to announce mourning. To order every man of أسامة ؓ's army out of
the city and back to the camp at الجُرف. The first executive act of the caliphate was to carry out
an order the Prophet ﷺ had already given.
**The statement:**
> نادى منادي أبي بكر من الغد من مُتوفَّى رسول الله ﷺ ليتمّم بعثَ أسامةَ: ألا لا يبقين بالمدينة أحدٌ من جيش أسامة إلَّا خرج إلى عسكره بالجُرف
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۸ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3154
> *English:* "أبو بكر's crier called out, the day after the death of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, to
> complete أسامة's expedition: 'Let no one of أسامة's army remain in المدینہ — let every man go out
> to his camp at الجُرف.'"
**عبرت:** The first day of an authority is what tells you what it thinks it is for.
**Hands-up?** no

---

### ABU/E-U3 · "I will not untie a knot"
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** ربيع الأول ۱۱ھ `[SOURCED]` (the connected narrative runs through
سيف بن عمر; the wording at ج۷ ص۱۱ comes by a separate route) · **Map:** المدینہ ringed — the
apostatising clans around it — and the army's arrow still pointing away, northward.
**What happened:** The advice came from the best men he had. Tribes around المدینہ had broken away.
Others were refusing the زکاۃ. The state's only field army was pointed at the Roman frontier, and
it was outside the walls. Keep it here, they said. Among those who said it was عمر بن الخطاب ؓ. He
refused, and he refused hard.
**The statement:**
> والذي نفس أبي بكر بيده لو ظننت أن السباعَ تَخْطفني لأنفذتُ بعثَ أسامةَ كما أمرَ به رسولُ الله ﷺ؛ ولو لم يبقَ في القرى غيري لأنفذته
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۱۰ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3156
> *English:* "By Him in whose hand is أبو بكر's soul — if I thought the wild beasts would snatch me
> away, I would still send out أسامة's expedition as the Messenger of Allah ﷺ ordered it. And if
> there were no one left in the settlements but me, I would still send it."
**عبرت:** There is a kind of steadiness that is not stubbornness: it is refusing to treat an
emergency as permission.
**Hands-up?** Ask before telling it: *the capital is surrounded and your only army is marching away.
Do you call it back?* Take the show of hands. Then read the line.

---

### ABU/E-U4 · The beard, and the man who would not be deposed
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** ربيع الأول ۱۱ھ `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** الجُرْف, one march outside
المدینہ.
**What happened:** The أنصار in the column sent عمر ؓ back with a second request. Not "cancel the
march" — "give us an older commander." عمر ؓ carried the message. أبو بكر ؓ had been sitting. He
came up off the ground, took عمر ؓ by the beard, and gave his answer in one sentence. Then he walked
out to الجُرف himself, reviewed the army and sent it off.
**The statement:**
> ثَكِلَتْكَ أُمُّكَ يَا ابْنَ الْخَطَّابِ! اسْتَعْمَلَهُ رَسُولُ اللَّهِ - صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ - وَتَأْمُرُنِي أَنْ أَعْزِلَهُ؟!
> — الکامل فی التاریخ ج۲ ص۱۹۵ · https://shamela.ws/book/21712/886
> *English:* "May your mother lose you, son of الخطاب! The Messenger of Allah ﷺ appointed him — and
> you order me to depose him?"
**عبرت:** Authority that can be renegotiated the moment its author is gone was never authority.
**Hands-up?** no

---

### ABU/E-U5 · The caliph on foot
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** آخر ربيع الأول ۱۱ھ `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** the arrow finally leaves
المدینہ — الجُرْف → ذو خُشُب → the marches of البلقاء.
**What happened:** He walked the army out. The caliph of the Muslims on foot in the dust; the
eighteen-year-old commander in the saddle; عبد الرحمن بن عوف ؓ leading the caliph's riding-beast
behind them, empty. أسامة ؓ could not bear it and offered to dismount. He was refused. Only at the
end did أبو بكر ؓ ask a favour — and he asked it of أسامة ؓ, because أسامة ؓ was the commander and
عمر ؓ was in his army: *would you release عمر to me?*
**The statement:**
> فقال أسامةُ: يا خليفةَ رسولِ الله: إما أن تركبَ وإما أن أنزلَ، فقال: والله لستَ بنازلٍ ولستُ براكب
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۱۱ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3157
> *English:* "أسامة said: 'O successor of the Messenger of Allah, either you ride or I dismount.' He
> said: 'By Allah, you shall not dismount, and I shall not ride.'"
**عبرت:** Rank is a place in an order, not a claim about who is the better man.
**Hands-up?** no

---

### ABU/E-U6 · Forty days, and what the tribes concluded
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** marched **آخر ربيع الأول ۱۱ھ** `[SOURCED]`, away forty days — said
seventy — `[SOURCED]`. (That puts the return in جمادى; the month of the return is **our arithmetic**,
`[CONVENTIONAL-ESTIMATE]`, not a dated report on any page read.) · **Map:** the arrow goes out, strikes,
and comes back — and the ring of hostile clans around المدینہ **thins** rather than closes.
**What happened:** The column marched north through country that was ready to rise. It did not
fight its way through. It simply passed. And the men watching it pass drew the obvious conclusion:
nobody sends an army *away* unless he has more behind him. Forty days later — some say seventy — it
came back whole and carrying spoil. The blow it struck is described differently in different books:
a raid on أُبنى in the البلقاء, or on قُضاعة clans that had broken away, or a clash with Roman
forces. The two books that comment on the *effect* — البدایہ ج۷ ص۱۱ and الکامل ج۲ ص۱۹۶ — agree on it.
(سیر ج۲ ص۴۹۷ names the target and says nothing about the effect; do not enlist it for that.)
**The statement:**
> فجعل لا يمرُّ بقبيل يريدون الارتداد إلَّا قالوا: لولا أن لهؤلاء قوةً ما خرجَ مثلُ هؤلاء من عندهم
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۱۱ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3157
> *English:* "He passed no tribe intending to break away but they said: 'Were it not that these
> people have strength, men like these would not have gone out from among them.'"
**عبرت:** What people believe about your strength does as much work as your strength does.
**Hands-up?** no

---

### ABU/E-U7 · "Peace be upon you, commander"
**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** ۱۱ھ and after `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** المدینہ — أسامة ؓ, aged eighteen,
left in charge of it.
**What happened:** The army came home. أبو بكر ؓ made أسامة ؓ his deputy over المدینہ, told the men
to rest their animals, and only then rode out himself against the breakaway clans. And عمر ؓ — who
would himself be caliph after him — never again met أسامة ؓ without greeting him by his rank. He gave
his reason: the Prophet ﷺ died while you were my commander.
**The statement:**
> السَّلاَمُ عَلَيْكَ أَيُّهَا الأَمِيْرُ وَرَحْمَةُ اللهِ! تُوُفِّيَ رَسُوْلُ اللهِ -صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ- وَأَنْتَ عَلَيَّ أَمِيْرٌ.
> — سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۲ ص۵۰۱ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/2481
> *English:* "Peace be upon you, commander, and the mercy of Allah! The Messenger of Allah ﷺ died
> while you were my commander."
**عبرت:** Honour given for the right reason costs the giver nothing and is remembered for centuries.
**Hands-up?** no

---

### ABU/E-U8 · The oath of أبو ہریرہ ؓ
**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** told later, about ۱۱ھ `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** none; this is a card the
speaker delivers standing still.
**What happened:** Years afterwards أبو ہریرہ ؓ was talking about that year. He swore an oath, and
then he swore it again, and then a third time, until the men around him told him to stop. Then he
explained what he meant: the army was pointed at Rome, the Arabs around المدینہ had broken away,
and the Companions came and asked for it back — and one man said no.
**The statement:**
> والله الذي لا إله إلَّا هو، لولا أن أبا بكر استُخلف ما عُبد الله
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۱۱ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3157
> *English:* "By Allah, besides whom there is no god — had أبو بكر not been made successor, Allah
> would not have been worshipped."
**عبرت:** A hinge is a small thing, and the whole door turns on it.
**Hands-up?** no
*(Note for the speaker: Ibn Kathīr examines the chain of this report on the very same page and
concludes the narrator is the acceptable عبّاد بن كثير. Saying that out loud is itself a
«کیسے پتا چلا؟» moment.)*

---

### ABU/E-Q1 · The killing ran hot among the reciters
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** اليمامة — ابن كثير: begun ۱۱ھ, finished ۱۲ھ; «المشهور» ربيع ۱۲ھ;
ابن الاثیر places it in ۱۱ھ `[SOURCED]` for the dispute · **Map:** **اليمامة** in central نجد, and
then the map goes quiet and the scene returns to المدینہ.
**What happened:** At اليمامة the ردة was broken and مسيلمة was killed, and the cost is the one
Ibn Kathīr stops to count: the dead in the garden and in the battle at close to ten thousand —
though he adds that some say twenty-one thousand — and the Muslim dead at six hundred, or five
hundred, and then he writes *and Allah knows best*. Among the
fallen were men who carried the Qur'an in their memories. Ibn Kathīr's phrase for it is
**استحرَّ القتل في القرّاء** — the killing *ran hot* among the reciters (the editor glosses
استحرَّ as اشتدَّ, grew fierce).
**The statement:**
> وقتل من المسلمين ستمئة، وقيل: خمسمئة، فالله أعلم، وفيهم من سادات الصحابة، وأعيان الناس من يُذكر بعد
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۳۵ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3181
> *English:* "Six hundred of the Muslims were killed — and it is said five hundred — and Allah knows
> best. Among them were leaders of the Companions and men of standing, who will be named later."
**عبرت:** A generation can carry something priceless and never notice how thin the carrying is until
some of it is dropped.
**Hands-up?** Ask: *if every copy of a book you loved lived only in people's memories, how many
funerals would it take to lose it?*
*(Never put a number of قرّاء on the slide — none of our sources gives one.)*

---

### ABU/E-Q2 · "How can we do a thing the Messenger of Allah ﷺ did not do?"
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** سنة ۱۲ھ `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** المدینہ; no movement — this is the
evening's still point.
**What happened:** After اليمامة, أبو بكر ؓ sent for زيد بن ثابت ؓ — a young man of بنو النجار who
had been eleven years old when the Prophet ﷺ arrived in المدینہ, who had memorised seventeen سور
before he was introduced, who had learned the Jews' script in a fortnight because the Prophet ﷺ
asked him to, and who had written the revelation down as it came. أبو بكر ؓ told him to go and find
the whole Qur'an and gather it. زيد ؓ balked — and his objection was the most serious objection
there is.
**The statement:**
> فَقُلْتُ: كَيْفَ تَفْعَلُوْنَ شَيْئاً لَمْ يَفْعَلْهُ رَسُوْلُ اللهِ -صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ-. قَالَ: هُوَ -وَاللهِ- خَيْرٌ. فَلَمْ يَزَلْ أَبُو بَكْرٍ يُرَاجِعُنِي حَتَّى شَرَحَ اللهُ صَدْرِي لِلَّذِي شَرَحَ لَهُ صَدْرَ أَبِي بَكْرٍ وَعُمَر
> — سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۲ ص۴۳۱ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/2411
> *English:* "I said: 'How will you do a thing the Messenger of Allah ﷺ did not do?' He said: 'By
> Allah, it is good.' And أبو بكر did not stop coming back to me until Allah opened my breast to
> that to which He had opened the breasts of أبو بكر and عمر."
**عبرت:** The men closest to the Prophet ﷺ were the most afraid of adding to what he ﷺ left — which
is exactly why what they did add can be trusted.
**Hands-up?** no

---

### ABU/E-Q3 · Parchment, shoulder-blades, palm-stalks, and the breasts of men
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** سنة ۱۲ھ `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** none. Show the four materials instead —
they *are* the picture.
**What happened:** There was no volume to copy from. زيد ؓ had to go and track it down — the verb in
his own account is تتبَّع, to follow a thing up piece by piece — and gather it off whatever the
Companions had written it on: strips of parchment, the
flat shoulder-blades of animals, the bare stalks of palm branches, thin white stones — and out of
the memories of the men who had it by heart. Then it was one set of صحف, in one place, for the
first time.
**The statement:**
> فَكُنْتُ أَتَتَبَّعُ القُرْآنَ، أَجْمَعُهُ مِنَ الرِّقَاعِ، وَالأَكْتَافِ، وَالعُسُبِ، وَصُدُوْرِ الرِّجَالِ.
> — سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۲ ص۴۳۱ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/2411
> *English:* "So I began to track down the Qur'an, gathering it from the parchments, the
> shoulder-blades, the palm-stalks, and the breasts of men."
**عبرت:** Preservation is not one heroic act; it is somebody doing a careful, unglamorous job
properly.
**Hands-up?** Ask the room to name the four things after you have said them once. It is the one line
in the evening everybody will remember.
*(Ibn Kathīr's own wording at البدایہ ج۷ ص۷۹ names اللِّخاف — the thin white stones — where الذہبی
names الرقاع. If both are wanted, say so and cite both.)*

---

### ABU/E-Q4 · علی ؓ on who deserves the credit
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** **undated** — علی ؓ is speaking about the جمع of ۱۲ھ, but no page read
says when he said it. The chain is graded **حسن** by الذہبی `[SOURCED]`; the date is not. ·
**Map:** none.
**What happened:** The judgement on this decision does not have to come from us, and it does not
have to come from a later historian. It comes from علی بن أبی طالب ؓ, and الذہبی records his chain
and grades it good.
**The statement:**
> أعظم الناس أجرا في المصاحف أبو بكر، كان أول من جمع القرآن بين اللوحين
> — سیر أعلام النبلاء، سیر الخلفاء الراشدين ص۱۵ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/1163
> *English:* "The greatest of men in reward in the matter of the مصاحف is أبو بكر — he was the first
> to gather the Qur'an between two boards."
**عبرت:** When a man's rival in nothing and his equal in much says the thing for you, you do not
need to say it yourself.
**Hands-up?** no

---

### ABU/E-Q5 · Where the صحف went
**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** ۱۲ھ, and then across two more caliphates `[SOURCED]` for the endpoint ·
**Map:** a single arrow: المدینہ → المدینہ. Nothing moves. That is the point.
**What happened:** The صحف زيد ؓ gathered did not go into a library or a treasury. They stayed in
the household. Twenty-odd years later, when حذيفة ؓ came back from the Armenian front alarmed at how
differently the men of الشام and the men of العراق were reciting, عثمان ؓ sent for them — and he
sent to حفصة بنت عمر ؓ, a wife of the Prophet ﷺ and the daughter of the second caliph. She sent
them. They were copied. Then he sent them back to her.
**The statement:**
> فأرسل إلى حفصة أم المؤمنين: أن أرسلي بالصحف التي جمع فيها القرآن، فأرسلت إليه بها … ثم رد عثمان الصحف إلى حفصة
> — سیر أعلام النبلاء، سیر الخلفاء الراشدين ص۱۵۷ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/1299
> *English:* "So he sent to حفصة, mother of the believers: 'Send us the صحف in which the Qur'an was
> gathered.' And she sent them to him … then عثمان returned the صحف to حفصة."
**عبرت:** What is entrusted is returned. The whole transmission of this book is a chain of things
handed back intact.
**Hands-up?** no
*(Do **not** recite "أبو بكر → عمر → حفصة" as a three-step chain on the slide. The middle step is
البخاري's and is not on any page we hold. What is sourced is: they were with حفصة ؓ, borrowed, and
returned.)*

---

### ABU/E-Q6 · Four men had it by heart
**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** in the Prophet's ﷺ lifetime `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** none.
**What happened:** أنس بن مالك ؓ named the men who had gathered the Qur'an in the Prophet's ﷺ own
lifetime, and both of our books carry the list. All four were أنصار. زيد بن ثابت ؓ is one of them.
This is the reason he was the man asked — not because he was pious, but because he was one of the
few who already had the whole of it and had also physically written it down.
**The statement:**
> جَمَعَ القُرْآنَ عَلَى عَهْدِ رَسُوْلِ اللهِ أَرْبَعَةٌ، كُلُّهُمْ مِنَ الأَنْصَارِ: أُبَيٌّ، وَمُعَاذٌ، وَزَيْدُ بنُ ثَابِتٍ، وَأَبُو زَيْد
> — سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۲ ص۴۳۱ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/2411 (also البدایہ ج۵ ص۴۸۸ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/2672)
> *English:* "Four gathered the Qur'an in the time of the Messenger of Allah, all of them from the
> أنصار: أُبَيّ, and مُعاذ, and زيد بن ثابت, and أبو زيد."
**عبرت:** Being ready is not something you do on the day you are needed.
**Hands-up?** no

---

### ABU/E-Q7 · The boy who learned the Jews' script in a fortnight
**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** ۱ھ onward `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** المدینہ.
**What happened:** زيد بن ثابت ؓ was eleven when the Prophet ﷺ came to المدینہ. His father had
already been killed at بُعاث, so he grew up an orphan. They brought him forward and said: this boy
of بنو النجار has already memorised seventeen of the سور that came down to you. He recited, and the
Prophet ﷺ was pleased, and gave him a task on the spot.
**The statement:**
> يَا زَيْدُ! تَعَلَّمْ لِي كِتَابَ يَهُوْدٍ، فَإِنِّي -وَاللهِ- مَا آمَنُهُمْ عَلَى كِتَابِي
> — سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۲ ص۴۲۸–۴۲۹ (the last word falls on ص۴۲۹) · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/2408
> *English:* "زيد! Learn for me the writing of the Jews, for by Allah I do not trust them with my
> correspondence."
**عبرت:** The people who end up carrying something were given small jobs first, and did them.
**Hands-up?** no
*(He adds on the next page, سیر ج۲ ص۴۲۹: «فَتَعَلَّمْتُهُ، فَمَا مَضَى لِي نِصْفُ شَهْرٍ حَتَّى
حَذَقْتُهُ» — "I learned it, and half a month had not passed before I had mastered it." His age at
the جمع is not stated in any source we hold; the arithmetic gives about twenty-one, and must be
labelled `[CONVENTIONAL-ESTIMATE]` if used.)*

---

### ABU/E-U8b · What المدینہ felt like, in عائشة ؓ's words
**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** ۱۱ھ `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** المدینہ, alone on the board.
**What happened:** The daughter of the caliph described what fell on her father in that year. Not
what he did — what landed on him. She reached for two images: mountains, and livestock.
**The statement:**
> والله لقد نزل بأبي ما لو نزل بالجبال الراسيات لهاضها … وصار أصحاب محمد ﷺ كأنهم مِعْزًى مطيرة في حُشٍّ في ليلة مطيرة بأرض مَسْبَعَة
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۱۰ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3156
> *English:* "By Allah, there descended upon my father what, had it descended upon the firm-set
> mountains, would have broken them … and the Companions of محمد ﷺ became like rain-soaked goats in
> a walled garden on a rainy night, in a land full of predators."
**عبرت:** Steadiness is not the absence of fear; it is what a frightened man does next.
**Hands-up?** no
*(Her opening clause in the same report — «ارتدتِ العربُ قاطبةً» — is rhetorical compression. Do not
let a slide turn it into a headcount; see `arabian-tribes-and-the-ridda-setup.md` §5.)*

---

### ABU/E-U9 · The first victory he never fought
**Tier:** CUT (recorded so it is not re-researched; it belongs to the الأسود العنسي strand) ·
**When:** آخر ربيع الأول ۱۱ھ `[SOURCED]`, with a competing report Ibn Kathīr records and does not
adopt · **Map:** صنعاء lights up while المدینہ is still counting its dead.
**What happened:** Days after the column marched, word reached المدینہ from اليمن that الأسود
العنسي — the false prophet who had taken صنعاء in the Prophet's ﷺ own last months — had been killed
inside the city. Ibn Kathīr calls it the first فتح of أبو بكر ؓ's caliphate, and notes that he won
it sitting in المدینہ.
**The statement:**
> أمضى أبو بكر جيشَ أسامةَ بن زيد في آخر ربيع الأول، وأتى مقتل الأسود في آخر ربيع الأول بعد مخرج أسامة، فكان ذلك أولَ فَتْحٍ فَتَحَ أبو بكر وهو بالمدينة
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۱۲ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3158
> *English:* "أبو بكر sent out أسامة بن زيد's army at the end of ربيع الأول, and news of الأسود's
> killing came at the end of ربيع الأول, after أسامة's departure — and that was the first victory
> أبو بكر won, while he was in المدینہ."
**عبرت:** Some of what a man is credited with was decided before he sat down, by people he never
met.
**Hands-up?** no
*(A second report — «بل جاءت البشارةُ إلى المدينة صبيحةَ توفي رسول الله ﷺ» — is recorded at
البدایہ ج۷ ص۱۷, where Ibn Kathīr says «والأول أشهر». Do not adjudicate on a slide.)*

---

---

## The death of أبو بكر ؓ, and the succession

*Source note: [`abu-bakr-death-and-the-succession-of-umar.md`](../docs/research/abu-bakr-death-and-the-succession-of-umar.md) — read it for the pages behind these cards.*

### ABD/E-B1 · The bath on a cold day
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** جمادى الآخرة 13 AH `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** nothing moves. The pin stays on
المدینہ while the two arrows — الشام and العراق — keep running off the edge of the frame. That
contrast is the point: the man is in a room; the state is at two horizons.
**What happened:** He washed on a cold day. The fever came, and it did not leave. For fifteen days he
did not walk out to the mosque. He sent عمر ؓ to lead the prayer in his place. People came in to sit
with him, and عثمان ؓ came more than anyone. On the fifteenth day the state that had held Arabia
together was being run from a bed.
**The statement:**
> وعن عائشة قالت: أول ما بدئ مرض أبي بكر أنه اغتسل، وكان يوما باردًا فحم خمسة عشر يوما لا يخرج إلى صلاة، وكان يأمر عمر بالصلاة، وكانوا يعودونه، وكان عثمان ألزمهم له في مرضه.
> — سیر أعلام النبلاء، ج الراشدون ص۱۶ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/1164
> *English:* "ʿĀʾisha ؓ said: the beginning of Abū Bakr's illness was that he bathed on a cold day
> and was feverish for fifteen days, not going out to the prayer. He would order ʿUmar to lead the
> prayer. People visited him, and ʿUthmān was the most constant of them at his side."
**عبرت:** A man may be carrying a whole community and still be, that week, only a sick man in a room.
**Hands-up?** no

### ABD/E-B2 · "The doctor has already been"
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** during the fifteen days `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** nothing moves.
**What happened:** They asked whether they should call a physician for him. He told them the
physician had already come. They asked what he had said. He said: *I do what I will.* They understood
who he meant. Nobody said anything more about doctors.
**The statement:**
> وَلَمَّا مَرِضَ قَالَ لَهُ النَّاسُ: أَلَا نَدْعُوَ الطَّبِيبَ؟ قَالَ: قَدْ أَتَانِي وَقَالَ لِي: أَنَا فَاعِلٌ مَا أُرِيدُ. فَعَلِمُوا مُرَادَهُ وَسَكَتُوا عَنْهُ، ثُمَّ مَاتَ.
> — الکامل فی التاریخ ج۲ ص۲۶۲ · https://shamela.ws/book/21712/953
> *English:* "When he fell ill the people said to him: shall we not call the physician? He said: he
> has already come to me, and he said to me — I do what I will. They understood what he meant, fell
> silent, and then he died."
**عبرت:** A man who has spent his life in submission does not change his vocabulary when he is dying.
**Hands-up?** no

### ABD/E-B3 · He asks عبد الرحمن بن عوف ؓ about عمر ؓ
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** the last days of the illness `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** nothing moves.
**What happened:** He called عبد الرحمن بن عوف ؓ in and asked him to tell him about عمر ؓ. عبد الرحمن
ؓ said what everyone in المدینہ already thought: he is better than you think — except there is
harshness in him. أبو بكر ؓ did not argue with the description. He explained it. *He is hard because
he sees me being soft. When I was angry with a man, عمر showed me leniency; when I was gentle with a
man, عمر showed me severity. Put the office in his hands and much of that will go.*
**The statement:**
> فَقَالَ: إِنَّهُ أَفْضَلُ مِنْ رَأْيِكَ إِلَّا أَنَّهُ فِيهِ غِلْظَةٌ. فَقَالَ أَبُو بَكْرٍ: ذَلِكَ لِأَنَّهُ يَرَانِي رَقِيقًا، وَلَوْ أَفْضَى الْأَمْرُ إِلَيْهِ لَتَرَكَ كَثِيرًا مِمَّا هُوَ عَلَيْهِ
> — الکامل فی التاریخ ج۲ ص۲۶۶ · https://shamela.ws/book/21712/957
> *English:* "He said: he is better than your opinion of him — except that there is harshness in
> him. Abū Bakr said: that is because he sees me being gentle. If the affair passed to him he would
> leave much of what he is now."
**عبرت:** A man's edge is often the shape of the gap he thinks he is filling.
**Hands-up?** Before telling it: *"Hands up — who here has worked with someone whose hardness was a
response to somebody else's softness?"*

### ABD/E-B4 · He asks عثمان ؓ, and hears about himself in return
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** immediately after `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** nothing moves.
**What happened:** Then he called عثمان ؓ and asked him the same question. عثمان ؓ answered in one
line: *what I know of him is that his inside is better than his outside, and there is nobody among us
like him.* أبو بكر ؓ told both men to repeat nothing. And then he said something about عثمان ؓ
himself: had I passed عمر over, I would not have passed you over.
**The statement:**
> علمي فيه أن سريرته خير من علانيته وأنه ليس فينا مثله فقال: يرحمك الله، والله لو تركته ما عدوتك
> — سیر أعلام النبلاء، ج الراشدون ص۱۶ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/1164
> *English:* "What I know of him is that his inward is better than his outward, and that there is
> none among us like him. He said: God have mercy on you — by God, had I left him, I would not have
> passed you over."
**عبرت:** He asked the men most likely to tell him something he did not want to hear.
**Hands-up?** no

### ABD/E-B5 · The objection, and "sit me up"
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** the last days of the illness `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** nothing moves.
**What happened:** Somebody came in and said it to his face. *You have put عمر over the people. You
have seen what they get from him while you are still standing beside him — what will it be when he is
alone with them? And you are going to meet your Lord, and He will ask you about your flock.* أبو بكر ؓ
was lying down. He said: sit me up. They sat him up. Then he answered: *Do you frighten me with God?
When I meet Him and He asks me, I will say — I appointed over Your people the best of Your people.*
**The statement:**
> فَقَالَ أَبُو بَكْرٍ: أَجْلِسُونِي، فَأَجْلَسُوهُ، فَقَالَ: أَبِاللَّهِ تُخَوِّفُنِي! إِذَا لَقِيتُ رَبِّي فَسَأَلَنِي قُلْتُ: اسْتَخْلَفْتُ عَلَى أَهْلِكَ خَيْرَ أَهْلِكَ.
> — الکامل فی التاریخ ج۲ ص۲۶۷ · https://shamela.ws/book/21712/958
> *English:* "Abū Bakr said: sit me up. They sat him up. He said: do you frighten me with God? When I
> meet my Lord and He asks me, I shall say: I appointed over Your people the best of Your people."
**عبرت:** A decision worth making is a decision you are willing to be sat upright to defend.
**Hands-up?** no
> ⚠ **Delivery note:** say *"one of them came in and said"*. الکامل names طلحة بن عبيد الله ؓ
> (ج۲ ص۲۶۷); سیر leaves him unnamed (الراشدون ص۱۶). Only name him if asked, and only as *"الکامل
> names him"*.

### ABD/E-B6 · The pen keeps writing while he is unconscious
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** the last days `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** nothing moves.
**What happened:** He brought عثمان ؓ in privately and began dictating. *In the name of God, the
Merciful, the Compassionate. This is what Abū Bakr ibn Abī Quḥāfa covenants to the Muslims. To
proceed —* and there he lost consciousness. عثمان ؓ did not put the pen down and did not wait. He
wrote عمر ؓ's name himself. When أبو بكر ؓ came round he asked for it to be read back. When he heard
the name he said "Allāhu akbar", and then: *I see you were afraid the people would split if I died in
that faint.* عثمان ؓ said yes. He said: may God reward you well on behalf of Islam.
**The statement:**
> ثُمَّ أَفَاقَ أَبُو بَكْرٍ فَقَالَ: اقْرَأْ عَلَيَّ. فَقَرَأَ عَلَيْهِ، فَكَبَّرَ أَبُو بَكْرٍ وَقَالَ: أَرَاكَ خِفْتَ أَنْ يَخْتَلِفَ النَّاسُ إِنْ مُتُّ فِي غَشْيَتِي. قَالَ: نَعَمْ. قَالَ: جَزَاكَ اللَّهُ خَيْرًا عَنِ الْإِسْلَامِ وَأَهْلِهِ.
> — الکامل فی التاریخ ج۲ ص۲۶۷ · https://shamela.ws/book/21712/958
> *English:* "Then Abū Bakr came round and said: read it to me. He read it to him, and Abū Bakr said
> takbīr and said: I see you feared the people would fall into disagreement if I died in my swoon. He
> said: yes. He said: may God reward you well on behalf of Islam and its people."
**عبرت:** He was not angry that a man had acted without instruction — he thanked him, because the man
had been thinking about the community and not about himself.
**Hands-up?** Before telling it: *"Hands up — if your chief fell unconscious mid-sentence, would you
finish the sentence for him?"*
> Corroboration in the second source, same incident, different wording:
> سیر الراشدون ص۱۷ (https://shamela.ws/book/10906/1165) — «فكتب عثمان من عنده اسم عمر … والله إن كنت
> لها أهلا».

### ABD/E-B7 · "I have not appointed a relative over you"
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** the last days `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** nothing moves; المدینہ's mosque fills.
**What happened:** The document was finished and he ordered it read out. The people were gathered.
The letter went with a freedman of his, and عمر ؓ walked beside him telling the crowd to be quiet and
listen. It was read. They said: we hear and we obey. Then أبو بكر ؓ looked out over them himself and
asked them a question — are you content with the man I have appointed? And then he told them the
thing nobody had asked him: *I have not appointed a relative over you.*
**The statement:**
> أَتَرْضُونَ بِمَنِ اسْتَخْلَفْتُ عَلَيْكُمْ؟ فَإِنِّي مَا اسْتَخْلَفْتُ عَلَيْكُمْ ذَا قَرَابَةٍ، وَإِنِّي قَدِ اسْتَخْلَفْتُ عَلَيْكُمْ عُمَرَ فَاسْمَعُوا لَهُ وَأَطِيعُوا، فَإِنِّي وَاللَّهِ مَا أَلَوْتُ مِنْ جُهْدِ الرَّأْيِ.
> — الکامل فی التاریخ ج۲ ص۲۶۷ · https://shamela.ws/book/21712/958
> *English:* "Are you content with the one I have appointed over you? For I have not appointed over
> you a relative of mine. I have appointed ʿUmar over you, so hear him and obey — for by God, I have
> not fallen short of the utmost of my judgement."
**عبرت:** He had a son and a family in that city, and he named the hardest man in it instead.
**Hands-up?** no

### ABD/E-B8 · The covenant itself
**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** the last days `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** nothing moves. Full-bleed slide: the
Arabic, large, and nothing else.
**What happened:** The wording he dictated is preserved. It opens by placing itself at a specific
moment — the last hour a man has of this world and the first hour he has of the next, the hour when
the disbeliever believes and the liar tells the truth. Then, in that frame, one sentence of business:
I have appointed ʿUmar; hear him and obey. And then a clause that most rulers would have left out —
*if he changes, every man has what he has earned. Good is what I intended. I do not know the unseen.*
**The statement:**
> هذا ما عهد أبو بكر بن أبي قحافة في آخر عهده بالدنيا خارجا منها، وعند أول عهده بالآخرة داخلا فيها، حيث يؤمن الكافر، ويوقن الفاجر، ويصدق الكاذب
> — سیر أعلام النبلاء، ج الراشدون ص۱۶–۱۷ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/1164 و https://shamela.ws/book/10906/1165
> *English:* "This is what Abū Bakr ibn Abī Quḥāfa covenants at the last of his time with this world,
> going out of it, and at the first of his time with the next, entering it — where the disbeliever
> believes, the corrupt man attains certainty, and the liar tells the truth."
**عبرت:** He wrote his own limits into the appointment: I chose, I meant well, and I do not know what
happens next.
**Hands-up?** no
> ⚠ **Read this off a printed سیر before it reaches a slide.** The Shamela text of this passage
> carries at least one plain typing slip («وعلمي فه» for «وعلمي فيه»).
> ⚠ **This wording spans the printed page break**: it begins on ص۱۶ and finishes on ص۱۷. The
> break falls after «وعند أول».

### ABD/E-B9 · The list of what he owned
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** the last day `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** nothing moves.
**What happened:** He called عائشة ؓ. He told her that since the day he took charge of the Muslims'
affair he had not eaten a dinar or a dirham of theirs — only the coarsest of their food, only the
roughest of their clothing. Then he listed what was in the house that belonged to the Muslims and not
to him. An Abyssinian servant. A camel used for carrying water. The worn nap of one blanket. *When I
die, send them to ʿUmar.* She did.
**The statement:**
> وليس عندنا من فيء المسلمين شيء إلا هذا العبد الحبشي وهذا البعير الناضح وجرد هذه القطيفة، فإذا مت فابعثي بهن إلى عمر، ففعلت.
> — سیر أعلام النبلاء، ج الراشدون ص۱۹ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/1167
> *English:* "We have nothing of the Muslims' fayʾ except this Abyssinian servant, this water-carrying
> camel, and the worn nap of this blanket. When I die, send them to ʿUmar. And she did."
**عبرت:** He kept an account of what was not his, and he closed it before he died.
**Hands-up?** Before telling it: *"Hands up — could you list, right now, everything in your house that
is not yours?"*

### ABD/E-B10 · عمر ؓ weeps, and refuses to give it back
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** after his death `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** nothing moves.
**What happened:** The three things arrived at عمر ؓ. He wept until his tears ran onto the ground,
repeating one sentence: *God have mercy on Abū Bakr — he has exhausted whoever comes after him.* Then
he ordered them taken into the treasury. عبد الرحمن بن عوف ؓ objected: you are stripping his family
of a servant, a water-camel and a threadbare blanket worth five dirhams — send them back. عمر ؓ
refused. *Abū Bakr did not get clear of this for me to hang it round my own neck.*
**The statement:**
> فَقَالَ: لَا وَالَّذِي بَعَثَ مُحَمَّدًا - صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ - لَا يَكُونُ هَذَا فِي وِلَايَتِي، وَلَا خَرَجَ أَبُو بَكْرٍ مِنْهُ وَأَتَقَلَّدُهُ أَنَا.
> — الکامل فی التاریخ ج۲ ص۲۶۵ · https://shamela.ws/book/21712/956
> *English:* "He said: no, by the One who sent Muḥammad ﷺ — this shall not happen under my authority.
> Abū Bakr did not get clear of it for me to hang it round my own neck."
**عبرت:** A standard is only a standard once the next man refuses to lower it.
**Hands-up?** no

### ABD/E-B11 · Sell my land and pay them back
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** the last days `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** nothing moves.
**What happened:** He had been given a maintenance out of the treasury — the community had voted it
to him when he gave up trading, because a man cannot run a market stall and a state at once. On his
deathbed he gave an instruction about it. Sell a piece of land of mine. Take the price. Pay it in
against every dirham I have drawn.
**The statement:**
> فَلَمَّا حَضَرَتْهُ الْوَفَاةُ أَوْصَى أَنْ تُبَاعَ أَرْضٌ لَهُ وَيُصْرَفَ ثَمَنُهَا عِوَضَ مَا أَخَذَهُ مِنْ مَالِ الْمُسْلِمِينَ.
> — الکامل فی التاریخ ج۲ ص۲۶۶ · https://shamela.ws/book/21712/957
> *English:* "When death came to him he directed that land of his be sold and its price paid over in
> compensation for what he had taken of the Muslims' property."
**عبرت:** He treated a lawful salary as a debt.
**Hands-up?** no
> **Number caution:** the sources give the salary as 6,000 dirhams a year (الکامل ج۲ ص۲۶۶), as half a
> sheep a day (سیر الراشدون ص۱۴), and as 2,500 (سیر الراشدون ص۱۴). **Do not put a figure on the
> slide.** The instruction is the point, not the amount.

### ABD/E-B12 · One dinar
**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** after his death `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** nothing moves.
