him; he refused the money he was offered; and he handed over the letter. Saʿd swore he had never said
the words people were reporting — and ʿUmar believed him. He governed Kufa three and a half more
years, and when ʿUmar removed him, Ibn Kathīr says plainly it was for no incapacity and no breach of
trust.
**The statement:**
> بَلَغَنِي أَنَّكَ اتَّخَذْتَ قَصْرًا جَعَلْتَهُ حِصْنًا، وَيُسَمَّى قَصْرَ سَعْدٍ، بَيْنَكَ وَبَيْنَ النَّاسِ بَابٌ، فَلَيْسَ بِقَصْرِكَ وَلَكِنَّهُ قَصْرُ الْخَبَالِ … وَلَا تَجْعَلْ عَلَى الْقَصْرِ بَابًا يَمْنَعُ النَّاسَ مِنْ دُخُولِهِ
> — الکامل فی التاریخ ج۲ ص۳۵۴ · https://shamela.ws/book/21712/1045
> *English:* "It has reached me that you have taken a palace and made it a fortress, and that it is
> called Saʿd's palace, and that between you and the people there is a door. It is not your palace —
> it is the palace of ruin … and set no door on the palace that keeps people from entering it."
**عبرت:** A door between a governor and the people is the first thing that has to be burnt, even
when the governor is innocent.
**Hands-up?** no

---

### QMA/E-Q24 · «أنا أشرفهم» — the load of earth
**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** before the battle, at المدائن `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** the delegation's line
running back from **المدائن** to **القادسية**, carrying something.
**What happened:** When the king had finished with the delegation he called for a load of earth, to
be put on the neck of their noblest man and driven out of the city as an insult. He asked: who is
the noblest of you? They were silent. ʿĀṣim ibn ʿAmr claimed — falsely — that he was, so that he
could be the one to carry it, and they let him. He carried it out on his neck, loaded it on his
mount, and rode. Rustam, when the king told him, understood at once what had been done and sent a
rider to get the earth back. He was too late. ʿĀṣim reached Saʿd first, and Saʿd read it the same
way.
**The statement:**
> فقال له رستم: إنه ليس أحمق، وليس هو بأشرفهم، إنما أراد أن يفتدي قومه بنفسه، ولكن والله ذهبوا بمفاتيح أرضنا
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۱۳۸ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3284
> *English:* "Rustam said to him: he is not a fool, and he is not their noblest. He only wanted to
> ransom his people with himself. But by God, they have gone off with the keys of our land."
**عبرت:** A humiliation only lands where somebody agrees to be humiliated.
**Hands-up?** no

---

### QMA/E-Q25 · «كانوا يُدَوُّون بالقرآن … كدويّ النَّحْل»
**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** after القادسية `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** none — statement slide, pairs with
E-Q16.
**What happened:** Saʿd wrote his dispatch to Medina and had it carried by hand. He reported the
victory, the length of the fighting, the size of the force that had come against them. Then he
listed the dead he could name — and stopped, because he could not name the rest. What he wrote about
them instead is the reason the letter survives. ʿUmar read it out to the people from the pulpit, and
then spoke about himself: he was not a king, that he should own them; he was a servant of God who
had been offered a trust.
**The statement:**
> ورجالٌ من المسلمين لا يعلمهم إلا الله، فإنّه بهم عالمٌ كانوا يُدَوُّون بالقرآن إذا جنَّ عليهم الليلُ كدويّ النَّحْل، وهم آساد في النهار لا تشبههم الأسود
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۱۴۳ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3289
> *English:* "…and men of the Muslims whom none knows but God, for He knows them: they used to hum
> with the Qurʾān when night fell over them like the humming of bees, and by day they were lions
> that lions do not resemble."
**عبرت:** The ones who decided it are almost always the ones nobody wrote down.
**Hands-up?** no

---

## بيت المقدس and the العهدة العمرية

*Source note: [`bayt-al-maqdis-and-the-umari-covenant.md`](../docs/research/bayt-al-maqdis-and-the-umari-covenant.md) — read it for the pages behind these cards.*

### BAM/E-Q1 · The condition the city set
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** 15 or 16 AH `[SOURCED]` — البدایہ ج۷ ص۱۵۷–۱۵۸ and الکامل ج۲ ص۳۲۹ for 15,
البدایہ ج۷ ص۱۵۹–۱۶۰ and سیر جراشدون ص۱۱۷ for 16, سیر جراشدون ص۸۸ for 17; the full table is §7 ·
**Map:** دمشق already
Muslim; a siege ring is drawn round بيت المقدس; a long arrow is *not* yet drawn from المدینہ.
**What happened:** أبو عبيدة بن الجراح ؓ finished at دمشق, left سعيد بن زيد ؓ in charge there, and
rode south. He wrote to the people of إيلياء first — Islam, or the جزية, or notice of war. They
refused all three. So he besieged the city and pressed it hard. In the end they agreed to terms. But
they attached one condition. They would surrender, and they would pay — to the Commander of the
Believers, in person, and to nobody else. أبو عبيدة ؓ wrote to المدینہ.
**The statement:**
> ثم حاصرَ بيت المَقْدس وضيَّقَ عليهم حتى أجابوا إلى الصلح بشرطِ أن يقدم إليهم أمير المؤمنين عمر بن الخطاب
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۱۵۷ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3303
> *English:* "Then he besieged بيت المقدس and pressed them hard until they agreed to terms, on the
> condition that the Commander of the Believers, عمر بن الخطاب, come to them in person."

**عبرت:** A city that had refused an army's terms would accept one man's word — which tells you what
the word of that office was still worth.
**Hands-up?** Ask before telling: *"They were losing. They could name one condition. What would you
have asked for?"*

---

### BAM/E-Q2 · Two Companions, two opinions, one decision
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** immediately after E-Q1 `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** المدینہ lights up; a line is
drawn from المدینہ north.
**What happened:** عمر ؓ did not decide alone. He put the letter to the people of المدینہ. عثمان بن
عفان ؓ said: do not go. Let them come to us. It humbles them more. علي بن أبي طالب ؓ said: go. Our
men are tied down outside those walls, and going will lift the siege off them sooner. عمر ؓ liked
علي ؓ's answer and did not like عثمان ؓ's. He left علي ؓ himself in charge of المدینہ, put العباس بن
عبد المطلب ؓ at the head of the column, and rode out.
**The statement:**
> فأشار عثمان بن عفان بأن لا يركبَ إليهم ليكونَ أحقرَ لهم وأرغمَ لأنوفهم، وأشار علي بن أبي طالب بالمسير إليهم ليكونَ أخفَّ وطأةً على المسلمين في حصارهم بينهم، فهوي ما قالَ عليّ، ولم يهو ما قال عثمان
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۱۵۷ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3303
> *English:* "عثمان بن عفان advised that he not ride out to them, so that it would be more belittling
> to them and harder on their pride. علي بن أبي طالب advised marching to them, so that it would be
> lighter on the Muslims tied down in the siege of them. He liked what علي said and did not like what
> عثمان said."

**عبرت:** Two of the best of them read the same letter and reached opposite conclusions — and
nothing at all was wrong with that; it is what consultation is for.
**Hands-up?** no. *(Delivery note: this is the model of a Companion disagreement being ordinary
rather than fraught. Tell it plainly and move on — do not point at it.)*

---

### BAM/E-Q3 · The stones at الجابية
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** on arrival in الشام `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** the pin moves to الجابية in
حوران — a plain, not a city, and not Jerusalem.
**What happened:** The commanders rode out to meet the caliph. Two years earlier these had been
hungry men. They came in brocade and silk, on horses — يزيد بن أبي سفيان ؓ first, then أبو عبيدة ؓ,
then خالد بن الوليد ؓ. عمر ؓ got down off his mount, picked up stones off the ground, and threw them
at them. Then they explained: the silk is only the outer coat; the armour is underneath, and we need
it. He accepted that at once and said nothing more about it.
**The statement:**
> مَا أَسْرَعَ مَا رَجَعْتُمْ عَنْ رَأْيِكُمْ! إِيَّايَ تَسْتَقْبِلُونَ فِي هَذَا الزِّيِّ وَإِنَّمَا شَبِعْتُمْ مُذْ سَنَتَيْنِ! وَبِاللَّهِ لَوْ فَعَلْتُمْ هَذَا عَلَى رَأْسِ الْمِائَتَيْنِ لَاسْتَبْدَلْتُ بِكُمْ غَيْرَكُمْ
> — الکامل فی التاریخ ج۲ ص۳۳۰ · https://shamela.ws/book/21712/1021
> *English:* "How quickly you have turned back from your own view! Me you come out to meet in this
> dress — and you have only been eating your fill for two years! By Allah, had you done this at the
> turn of two hundred years I would have replaced you with others."

**عبرت:** He was not angry at silk; he was angry at how fast comfort had arrived after hunger — and
when they gave him the reason he had not known, he dropped it.
**Hands-up?** Ask: *"Two years earlier these men were hungry. What is the danger he saw?"*

---

### BAM/E-Q4 · The ford
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** on the road in الشام `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** no change — the pin is on the
road, between الجابية and the coast.
**What happened:** A water crossing lay in his path. He got down off the camel. He pulled off his
shoes and held them in one hand, and walked into the water leading the animal behind him. أبو عبيدة
بن الجراح ؓ — his own commander in الشام — was
watching, and it embarrassed him. *You have done a great thing today in front of the people of this
country*, he said. عمر ؓ struck him on the chest.
**The statement:**
> إنكم كنتم أذل الناس وأحقر الناس وأقل الناس، فأعزَّكم الله بالإسلام، فمهما تطلبوا العزَّ بغيره يذلكم الله
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۱۶۳ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3309
> *English:* "You were the lowest of people, and the most contemptible of people, and the fewest of
> people, and Allah honoured you by Islam. However much you seek honour by anything other than it,
> Allah will humble you."

**عبرت:** He was not being modest in front of the Romans; he was refusing to let his own men start
measuring themselves by what the Romans would think.
**Hands-up?** no. *(This is the still point of the evening. Let the sentence land and stop talking.)*

---

### BAM/E-Q5 · The linen shirt, and the horse he called the devil
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** at الجابية `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** no change.
**What happened:** His shirt was worn through and split at the side. He sent for the local headman
and asked for it to be washed and stitched, and for something to wear meanwhile. They brought a linen
shirt. He asked what the cloth was. They told him it was linen. He asked what linen was. When his own
shirt came back washed and patched he took theirs off and put his back on. Then the headman made a
suggestion: better clothes and a better horse, *because it would look greater in the eyes of the
Romans*. They brought a برذون with a blanket over it, no saddle. He rode a few paces and stopped it.
**The statement:**
> نحن قوم أعزنا الله بالإسلام فلا نطلب بغير الله بديلًا
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۱۶۳ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3309
> *English:* "We are a people whom Allah honoured by Islam, so we seek no substitute for what Allah
> gave."

**عبرت:** The advice was not malicious — it was practical, and it was about appearances; that is
exactly why it had to be refused.
**Hands-up?** Ask: *"What did the headman actually want him to do? And was it bad advice?"*
*(Second half of the scene, if there is time: «احبسوا احبسوا، ما كنتُ أرى الناسَ يركبونَ الشيطان قبل
هذا» — البدایہ ج۷ ص۱۶۳ — "Hold it, hold it! I never saw people ride the devil before this." And he
called for his camel. سیر جراشدون ص۸۳ ends it with «هاتوا جملي».)*

---

### BAM/E-Q6 · What he arrived on
**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** at الجابية `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** no change; this is the portrait slide.
**What happened:** An eyewitness described him coming in. An ash-grey camel. No turban, no cap — his
bald head bare in the sun. His legs swinging loose between the forks of the saddle, no stirrups. A
rough woollen wrap that served as his seat when he rode and his bed when he stopped. A saddlebag
stuffed with palm fibre that was his pillow when he stopped. And a coarse cotton shirt worn to
nothing and torn at the side.
**The statement:**
> قدمَ عمرُ بن الخطاب الجابية على طريق إيلياء على جملٍ أوْرَق، تلوحُ صلعتُه للشمس، ليس عليه قلنسوةٌ ولا عمامةٌ، تصطفقُ رجلاه بين شعبتي الرَّحل بلا ركاب
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۱۶۲ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3308
> *English:* "عمر بن الخطاب came to الجابية on the إيلياء road on an ash-grey camel, his bald head
> shining in the sun, with no cap and no turban on it, his legs swinging between the two forks of the
> saddle with no stirrups."

**عبرت:** Nothing about this was arranged for the occasion — الذہبی records that he dressed this way
in المدینہ too, with four patches in the back of his shirt.
**Hands-up?** no.
*(Second source for the deck: سیر جراشدون ص۱۱۸ carries the same report with the same narrator; سیر
جراشدون ص۸۲ carries أنس ؓ: «رأيت بين كتفي عمر أربع رقاع في قميصه».)*

---

### BAM/E-Q7 · The covenant of إيلياء
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** written at الجابية; the document says 15 AH `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** the
siege ring round بيت المقدس is replaced by a Muslim marker; a line runs back to الجابية where the
document was actually signed.
**What happened:** While عمر ؓ was still at الجابية, a squadron of Roman horsemen appeared with drawn
swords. The Muslims reached for their weapons. عمر ؓ stopped them: these men are coming for terms,
not for a fight. They were the delegation from بيت المقدس. He granted what they asked, fixed the
جزية, and had the document written out. معاوية بن أبي سفيان ؓ held the pen. خالد بن الوليد ؓ, عمرو
بن العاص ؓ and عبد الرحمن بن عوف ؓ signed as witnesses. The Romans in the city had three days to
leave.
**The statement:**
> وشهد في الكتاب خالد بن الوليد، وعمرو بن العاص، وعبد الرحمن بن عوف، ومعاوية بن أبي سفيان، وهو كاتب الكتاب، وذلك في سنة خمسة عشر
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۱۵۸ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3304
> *English:* "Witnesses to the document were خالد بن الوليد, عمرو بن العاص, عبد الرحمن بن عوف and
> معاوية بن أبي سفيان — and it was he who wrote the document. That was in the year fifteen."

**عبرت:** The four names at the bottom of that page are the reason it is a public act of the state
and not a general's private promise — which is exactly what the city had asked for.
**Hands-up?** no.
> ⚠ **Deck note.** ابن کثیر does **not** print the text; he sends the reader to ابن جریر. If the deck
> reproduces the covenant it must be captioned **تاریخ الطبری ج۳ ص۶۰۹**, never البدایہ. Full text and
> the delivery caution: §4.2. Read the document; do not comment on it.

---

### BAM/E-Q8 · "Where do you think I should pray?"
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** on entering the sanctuary `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** the pin moves inside the
walls; mark the Rock, and mark the southern end where the mosque went.
**What happened:** Inside, عمر ؓ turned to كعب الأحبار — the man who had shown him where the Rock
lay in the first place. *Where do you think I should pray?* كعب's
answer was reasonable: pray behind the Rock, and the whole of القدس lies in front of you. عمر ؓ
refused it in one sentence, and then went forward to the qiblah and prayed. The mosque was built at
the southern end, in front — not behind the Rock. ابن کثیر, who is careful about such things,
examined the chain of this report and called it good.
**The statement:**
> سمعت عمر يقول لكعب: أين ترى أن أصلي؟ قال: إن أخذت عني صليت خلفَ الصخرة وكانت القدسُ كلُّها بين يديك. فقال عمر: ضاهيتَ اليهوديةَ، لا، ولكن أصلِّي حيثُ صلَّى رسولُ اللّه ﷺ
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۱۶۱ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3307
> *English:* "I heard عمر say to كعب: where do you think I should pray? He said: if you take it from
> me, you would pray behind the Rock, and the whole of القدس would be before you. عمر said: you have
> imitated Jewry. No — I pray where the Messenger of Allah ﷺ prayed."

**عبرت:** The city was his that morning and the advice came from an expert — and it still did not
outrank the question *where did the Prophet ﷺ pray?*
**Hands-up?** Ask: *"He has just taken the city. Whose practice does he look to?"*
*(Briefing note, not for the room: ابن کثیر grades the isnad **جيد**; the دار ابن كثير editor thinks
that is too generous, since عيسى بن سنان القسملي is in the chain. Say "ابن کثیر called the chain
good" — do not go further.)*

---

### BAM/E-Q9 · Sweeping the Rock
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** the same day `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** the Rock is marked and cleared.
**What happened:** The Rock was under a rubbish tip. ابن کثیر explains why: the Byzantines had
dumped refuse on it because it was the Jews' qiblah, in requital for something older still. هرقل had
seen it himself — he was at إيلياء when the Prophet's ﷺ letter reached him — and he told the
Christians they deserved to be killed for degrading a sanctuary that way, and ordered it cleared.
But less than a third had been moved when
the Muslims arrived. عمر ؓ finished the job. He spread out his own cloak, swept the rubbish into it
with his hands, and carried it off. The people saw the caliph doing it and swept with him. The rest
was carried away by the people of الأردن.
**The statement:**
> ثم جاء فبسط رداءَهُ وكَنَس الكُناسة في ردائه وكنسَ الناسُ
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۱۶۱ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3307
> *English:* "Then he came and spread out his cloak and swept the rubbish into his cloak, and the
> people swept."

**عبرت:** He did not order the place cleaned; he cleaned it, and then there was no one left who
could think the work beneath him.
**Hands-up?** no.
*(البدایہ ج۷ ص۱۵۷ has the parallel wording: «ثم نقل التراب عن الصخرة في طرف ردائه وقبائه، ونقل
المسلمون معه في ذلك».)*

---

### BAM/E-Q10 · The gate, and the two prayers
**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** the entry and the following dawn `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** mark the gate and
مِحراب داود.
**What happened:** He entered the sanctuary by the same gate the Prophet ﷺ had entered on the night
of الإسراء. It is said he made the talbiyah as he came in. He prayed the greeting of the mosque at
the miḥrāb of داود. And the next morning he led the dawn prayer there: سورة ص in the first rakʿah,
with the prostration in it, and the whole congregation went down with him; and سورة بني إسرائيل — the
sūrah that opens with الإسراء — in the second.
**The statement:**
> ثم دخلها إذ دخل المسجد من الباب الذي دخل منه رسول الله ليلةَ الإسراء … وصلَّى بالمسلمين فيه صلاةَ الغداة من الغد فقرأ في الأولى بسورة ص وسجد فيها والمسلمون معه، وفي الثانية بسورة بني إسرائيل
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۱۵۷ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3303
> *English:* "Then he entered it, and he entered the mosque by the gate through which the Messenger
> of Allah ﷺ entered on the night of الإسراء … and he led the Muslims in the dawn prayer there the
> next morning, reciting in the first rakʿah سورة ص and prostrating in it with the Muslims, and in
> the second سورة بني إسرائيل."

**عبرت:** He chose the two sūrahs the place itself would have chosen — and that is the whole of what
he did to mark the day.
**Hands-up?** Ask: *"Of all the sūrahs — why that one, in that mosque?"*
*(ابن کثیر hedges the talbiyah with «ويقال». Keep the hedge.)*

---

### BAM/E-Q11 · The sermon at الجابية
**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** at الجابية, before the surrender `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** no change.
**What happened:** Before any of it — before the delegation, before the document — he stood up at
الجابية and preached. ابن کثیر gives an abridgement and says openly that he has abridged it; the full
chains, he says, are in his own مسند عمر. الذہبی calls the sermon famous and mass-transmitted.
**The statement:**
> أيها الناسُ أصْلحُوا سَرائرَكُم تَصْلُحْ علانيتكُمْ، واعملُوا لآخرتِكُم تُكْفَوا أمرَ دنياكم، واعلموا أن رجلًا ليس بينه وبين آدم أبٌ حيٌّ، ولا بينه وبين الله هوادةٌ
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۱۵۸ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3304
> *English:* "O people: set right what is hidden in you and what is open in you will be set right.
> Work for your hereafter and you will be sufficed in the matter of your world. And know that between
> a man and آدم there is no living father, and between him and Allah there is no privileged kinship."

**عبرت:** The first thing he said on that ground was that no one there had a claim on Allah by
descent — which is a strange thing to say at a victory, and the reason he said it.
**Hands-up?** no.
*(Second citation for the deck: «فخطب بها خطبة مشهورة متواترة عنه» — سیر أعلام النبلاء، جراشدون ص۱۱۸.)*

---

### BAM/E-Q12 · أبو عبيدة ؓ and the caliph, each refusing to be honoured
**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** the meeting in الشام `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** no change.
**What happened:** When عمر ؓ reached الشام, أبو عبيدة ؓ came out to him with the senior commanders —
خالد بن الوليد ؓ, يزيد بن أبي سفيان ؓ. أبو عبيدة ؓ got down off his mount. عمر ؓ got down off his.
أبو عبيدة ؓ moved to kiss the caliph's hand; عمر ؓ moved to kiss أبو عبيدة ؓ's foot. Each stopped.
Later, when عمرو بن العاص ؓ and شرحبيل بن حسنة ؓ finally arrived from أجنادين, they found him mounted,
threw themselves on his knees and kissed them — and he took them both in his arms.
**The statement:**
> فترجَّل أبو عبيدة، وترجَّل عمر، فأشار أبو عبيدة ليقبّل يد عمر، فهم عمرُ بتقبيل رِجلِ أبي عبيدة، فكفَّ عمر
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۱۵۷ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3303
> *English:* "أبو عبيدة dismounted, and عمر dismounted. أبو عبيدة moved to kiss عمر's hand, and عمر
> made as if to kiss أبو عبيدة's foot — and عمر held back."

**عبرت:** Neither of them would be the one honoured, and both of them stopped short — which is what
respect between equals in a matter of faith actually looks like.
**Hands-up?** no.

---

### BAM/E-Q13 · The برذون that swaggered
**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** الجابية → بيت المقدس `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** the line from الجابية to
بيت المقدس is drawn as he rides it.
**What happened:** He set out from الجابية for بيت المقدس and his horse went lame. They brought him a
برذون. It began to amble under him with a showy, rolling gait. He got straight down off it and struck
its face. He never rode one again, before or after.
**The statement:**
> فنزل عنه وضرب وجهه، وقال: لا علّم الله من علَّمك، هذا من الخيلاء. ثم لم يركب برذونًا قبله ولا بعده
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۱۵۹ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3305
> *English:* "He got down off it and struck its face and said: may Allah teach nothing to whoever
> taught you — this is of vanity. And he never rode a برذون before it or after it."

**عبرت:** He would not be carried into that city by anything that looked as though it were showing
him off.
**Hands-up?** no.
*(الکامل ج۲ ص۳۳۰–۳۳۱ has the same with «لَا أَعْلَمُ مَنْ عَلَّمَكَ هَذِهِ الْخُيَلَاءَ».)*

---

### BAM/E-Q14 · The man at الجابية who knew what was coming
**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** at الجابية `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** mark لُدّ, west of the hills — a small
town on the coastal plain.
**What happened:** A Jewish scholar met him on the road, or at الجابية — the two accounts differ.
*Peace be upon you, Fārūq. Are you the one for إيلياء? By Allah, you will not go home until Allah
opens إيلياء to you.* He was at the signing when it happened. And عمر ؓ, who asked about الدجال more
than he asked about most things, asked him. The man answered with a place and a distance.
**The statement:**
> فَسَأَلَهُ عُمَرُ عَنِ الدَّجَّالِ، وَكَانَ كَثِيرَ السُّؤَالِ عَنْهُ. فَقَالَ لَهُ: وَمَا مَسْأَلَتُكَ عَنْهُ يَا أَمِيرَ الْمُؤْمِنِينَ؟ أَنْتُمْ وَاللَّهِ تَقْتُلُونَهُ دُونَ بَابِ لُدٍّ بِبِضْعَ عَشْرَةَ ذِرَاعًا
> — الکامل فی التاریخ ج۲ ص۳۳۰ · https://shamela.ws/book/21712/1021
> *English:* "عمر asked him about الدجال — he used to ask about him often. He said to him: and why do
> you ask about him, Commander of the Believers? By Allah, you will kill him short of the gate of
> لُدّ by some ten cubits."

**عبرت:** He was standing in the country the reports were about, and he still asked, and still wrote
down the answer with the name of the man who gave it.
**Hands-up?** no. *(Tell it as reported speech and do not build on it. It is a report at a signing,
not a doctrine.)*

---

### BAM/E-Q15 · The year nobody can fix
**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** — `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** the timeline strip, with a **band** rather than a
single mark.
**What happened:** Ask when بيت المقدس was taken and the books do not agree. ابن کثیر narrates the
whole chapter under the year 15 and ends the covenant paragraph "and that was in the year fifteen" —
then, one page later, tells you plainly that other leading authorities put it in 16. ابن الاثیر puts
it in 15 and adds "and it is said: 16, in ربيع الأول". الذہبی's own annals place it in 16 — but at
another page he relays الليث بن سعد, who puts إيلياء in 17. The document itself, as الطبری transmits
it, is dated 15.
**The statement:**
> هذا سياق سيف بن عمر، وقد خالفه غيره من أئمة السير، فذهبوا إلى أن فتح بيت المقدس كان في سنة ست عشرة
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۱۵۹ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3305
> *English:* "This is سيف بن عمر's account. Others among the leading authorities on the campaigns
> disagreed with him and held that the opening of بيت المقدس was in the year sixteen."

**عبرت:** The historian wrote down the disagreement instead of hiding it — which is why we can still
see, 700 years later, exactly what he did and did not know.
**Hands-up?** Ask: *"Two great books, and they give different years. Is that a problem?"*
*(This is the evening's "how do we know that?" moment, spoken inside the story — not a methodology
slot. `CLAUDE.md` §1.7.)*

---

### BAM/E-Q16 · The stories that are not in the books
**Tier:** CUT — recorded so it is never re-researched, and so the speaker is never ambushed ·
**When:** — · **Map:** none.
**What happened:** Three of the best-loved details of this event are not in ابن کثیر, not in ابن
الاثیر and not in الذہبی: the Patriarch صفرونيوس handing over the keys; عمر ؓ being invited to pray
inside the Church and refusing lest Muslims later turn it into a mosque; and عمر ؓ and his servant
taking turns on one camel all the way from المدینہ, so that he entered the city on foot leading it
while the servant rode. The first appears in no source we hold. The second traces to ابن خلدون,
seven centuries later and without a chain. The third is in none of the three, nor in الطبری. What the
books do carry instead — the ford, the patched shirt, the refused horse — is better, and it is
sourced.
**The statement:** none in the sources we use.
**عبرت:** A course that will not tell you a story it cannot show you in a book is a course you can
trust with the ones it does tell.
**Hands-up?** no. *(If a written question asks for any of the three, the answer is in `QA_BANK.md`:
"it is widely repeated, but it is not in the books this course is built on, so I am not going to
tell it to you as history.")*

---

## عمر ؓ builds a state — and the two trials

*Source note: [`umar-the-state-diwan-calendar-and-the-two-trials.md`](../docs/research/umar-the-state-diwan-calendar-and-the-two-trials.md) — read it for the pages behind these cards.*

### UTS/E-U1 · The name of the office
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** 13 AH, at his accession — البدایہ ج۷ ص۱۲۰ dates it «يوم الثلاثاء لثمان بقين من جمادى الآخرة» of that year (https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3266) `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** no change — this is a Medina interior. Put the Line up instead: the caliphate is two years old.
**What happened:** When أبو بكر ؓ died, the people had to find something to call the new man. They tried the obvious thing. They called him *khalīfat khalīfat rasūl Allāh* — the successor of the successor of the Messenger of God. عمر ؓ heard it and did the arithmetic. The next man would need four terms. The one after him, five. So he stopped it and proposed something shorter. He said: you are the believers; I am your commander. أمير المؤمنين. The title he coined outlived him by a very long way. The first man to greet him with it was المغيرة بن شعبة ؓ — and ابن کثیر adds that another name is reported, without giving it.
**The statement:**
> لما ولي عمر قالوا: يا خليفةَ خليفةِ رسولِ الله. فقال عمر: هذا أمرٌ يطولُ، بل أنتم المؤمنون وأنا أميركم. فسمي أمير المؤمنين.
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۲۶۸ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3414
> *English:* "When ʿUmar took office they said: O successor of the successor of the Messenger of God. ʿUmar said: this is a thing that will go on getting longer — rather, you are the believers and I am your commander. So he was named Commander of the Believers."
**عبرت:** A title is not decoration; a man who trims his own title is telling you what he thinks the office is for.
**Hands-up?** "Who here has heard the words أمير المؤمنين? Keep your hand up if you know where they come from."

---

### UTS/E-U2 · The bond that had no year on it
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** 16 AH per البدایہ; 17 or 18 per الکامل `[SOURCED — disputed]` · **Map:** no change. This is the moment to show the Line with no numbers on it yet.
**What happened:** A piece of paper was brought up to عمر ؓ. It was a bond — one man owed another money, and the debt fell due in شعبان. عمر ؓ looked at it and asked one question. Which شعبان? This year's? Last year's? Next year's? Nobody could answer, because there was no answer. The Muslims had months but no years. A second complaint had come from أبو موسى الأشعري ؓ in Basra, saying much the same thing about official letters: they arrive from you with no date on them. So عمر ؓ gathered the Companions and put the problem to them.
**The statement:**
> وذلك أنه رُفع إلى عمر صك مكتوبٌ لرجل على آخر بدَيْنٍ يحلّ عليه في شعبان، فقال: أي شعبان؟ أمن هذه السنة أم التي قبلها، أم التي بعدها؟ ثم جمع الناس فقال: ضعوا للناس شيئًا يعرفون فيه حلول ديونهم.
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۱۸۱ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3327
> *English:* "A bond was brought up to ʿUmar, written for one man against another for a debt falling due in Shaʿbān. He said: which Shaʿbān? This year's, or the one before it, or the one after? Then he gathered the people and said: set down for the people something by which they may know when their debts fall due."
**عبرت:** Big institutions start from small unbearable problems, and the man who notices the small problem is doing the work.
**Hands-up?** "What is today's date? Now — what is today's Hijri date?"

---

### UTS/E-U3 · Five ways to count a year, and the one they chose
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** same council `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** no change. Use a five-line diagram slide, four struck through.
**What happened:** Five proposals went on the table. Count the way the Persians count, restarting at every new king — they disliked it, and الکامل notes the obvious flaw: each king wipes out the era before him. Count from Alexander, the way the Romans do — they disliked that, and it was too long besides. Count from the Prophet's ﷺ birth. Count from the day he was sent as Prophet. And then علي ؓ, and others with him, said: count from the هجرة. The reason given is not sentimental. It is that the هجرة is the plainest of the four — it is public, it is dateable, everyone knows when it happened. عمر ؓ and the Companions approved it.
**The statement:**
> وأشار علي بن أبي طالب وآخرون أن يؤرخ من هجرته من مكة إلى المدينة لظهوره لكلّ أحد فإنه أظهر من المولد والمبعث. فاستحسن ذلك عمر والصحابة
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۱۸۲ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3328
> *English:* "ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib and others advised that it be dated from his emigration from Mecca to Medina, because it is plain to everyone — plainer than the birth and than the sending. ʿUmar and the Companions approved of that."
**عبرت:** They chose the date that could be checked, not the date that felt greatest — which is what taking a decision seriously looks like.
**Hands-up?** no

---

### UTS/E-U4 · Why the year begins in المحرم
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** same council `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** no change.
**What happened:** One more question remained. If the count starts from the هجرة, which month does the year begin in? Not ربيع الأول, when he ﷺ actually arrived — Imām Mālik رحمہ اللہ is reported to have held that view, but the majority did not. Not رمضان, which was proposed and dropped. They fixed on المحرم, and the two books give two different reasons. ابن کثیر: it is the more exact, so the months do not drift, because المحرم is where the Arab lunar year already began. ابن الاثیر: المحرم is the month people come home from Hajj, and it is a sacred month. Both reasons are on the page. This is why your calendar starts where it starts.
**The statement:**
> فَاتَّفَقُوا عَلَى الْهِجْرَةِ، ثُمَّ قَالُوا: مِنْ أَيِّ الشُّهُورِ؟ فَقَالُوا: مِنْ رَمَضَانَ، ثُمَّ قَالُوا: فَالْمُحَرَّمُ هُوَ مُنْصَرَفُ النَّاسِ مِنْ حَجِّهِمْ، وَهُوَ شَهْرٌ حَرَامٌ، فَأَجْمَعُوا عَلَيْهِ.
> — الکامل فی التاریخ ج۱ ص۱۳ · https://shamela.ws/book/21712/9
> *English:* "They agreed on the hijra. Then they said: from which of the months? They said: from Ramaḍān. Then they said: Muḥarram is when people turn home from their Hajj, and it is a sacred month — so they settled on it."
**عبرت:** The calendar on your wall is the residue of one afternoon's careful argument, and nothing you use daily is as accidental as it looks.
**Hands-up?** "Hands up if you thought the Islamic year begins on the day of the هجرة itself."

---

### UTS/E-U5 · The register: paid on precedence, not on blood
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** 15 AH per الکامل `[SOURCED — disputed, see note §3.1]` · **Map:** no change; put the ladder on screen.
**What happened:** Money was now arriving in Medina from Syria and Iraq in quantities no one had ever handled. عمر ؓ built a register — a ديوان — and fixed a yearly stipend for every Muslim on it. The grading was by السابقة: how early you came to Islam and what you were present at. بدر at the top; then those between بدر and الحديبية; then those who came in at the Conquest and fought the ردة; then القادسية and اليرموك; and so on down. Then three of the greatest men of قريش by birth — صفوان بن أمية, الحارث بن هشام, سہیل بن عمرو ؓ — found themselves in a lower band than men whose grandfathers no one could name. They refused the money. عمر ؓ told them why. They took it.
**The statement:**
> فَقَالَ: إِنِّي إِنَّمَا أَعْطَيْتُكُمْ عَلَى السَّابِقَةِ فِي الْإِسْلَامِ لَا عَلَى الْأَحْسَابِ. قَالُوا: فَنَعَمْ إِذًا، وَأَخَذُوا
> — الکامل فی التاریخ ج۲ ص۳۳۱ · https://shamela.ws/book/21712/1022
> *English:* "He said: I have given to you according to precedence in Islam, not according to lineage. They said: then yes — and they took it."
**عبرت:** They accepted the answer, and that acceptance is the achievement — a principle is only real when the people it costs agree to it.
**Hands-up?** "Suppose the state has to pay everybody something. What do you pay them for?"

---

### UTS/E-U6 · Begin with the Prophet's ﷺ uncle
**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** at the founding of the ديوان `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** no change.
**What happened:** When the register was ready, علي ؓ and عبد الرحمن بن عوف ؓ told him the sensible thing: begin with yourself. He refused. He said he would begin with the Prophet's ﷺ uncle, and then work outward from the Prophet ﷺ, nearest first. So the first name written into the register of the Muslim state was العباس ؓ. عمر ؓ's own name is somewhere further down. Within each band, the register made no distinction at all — strong and weak, Arab and non-Arab, the same figure.
**The statement:**
> قَالَ لَهُ عَلِيٌّ وَعَبْدُ الرَّحْمَنِ بْنُ عَوْفٍ: ابْدَأْ بِنَفْسِكَ. قَالَ: لَا بَلْ أَبْدَأُ بِعَمِّ رَسُولِ اللَّهِ ﷺ، ثُمَّ الْأَقْرَبِ فَالْأَقْرَبِ
> — الکامل فی التاریخ ج۲ ص۳۳۱ · https://shamela.ws/book/21712/1022
> *English:* "ʿAlī and ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAwf said to him: begin with yourself. He said: no — rather I begin with the uncle of the Messenger of God ﷺ, then the nearest and the next nearest."
**عبرت:** Where a man puts his own name on a list he himself is writing is a fact about him that no speech can contradict.
**Hands-up?** no

---

### UTS/E-U7 · The register argued with in public
**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** 15 AH `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** no change.
**What happened:** People objected to the grading, and the objections were not private. Why not put القادسية on a level with the earlier campaigns? Why is a man who fought far from home paid the same as a man who fought on his own doorstep? He answered both. To the first: I was not going to place them in a rank belonging to what they did not live through. To the second, he turned the question around — the man defending his own doorstep has the better claim, not the worse; and he reminded them that nobody had raised this objection when the Anṣār were levelled with the foremost of the المهاجرون, although the Anṣār too were fighting from their own courtyard.
**The statement:**
> فَقَالَ: لَمْ أَكُنْ لِأُلْحِقَهُمْ بِدَرَجَةِ مَنْ لَمْ يُدْرِكُوا … فَهَلَّا قَالَ الْمُهَاجِرُونَ مِثْلَ قَوْلِكُمْ حِينَ سَوَّيْنَا بَيْنَ السَّابِقِينَ مِنْهُمْ وَالْأَنْصَارَ!
> — الکامل فی التاریخ ج۲ ص۳۳۲ · https://shamela.ws/book/21712/1023
> *English:* "He said: I was not going to join them to the rank of what they did not live through … Did the Emigrants say what you are saying, when we levelled their foremost with the Anṣār?"
**عبرت:** A rule you can be argued with about in public is a different kind of rule from one you cannot.
**Hands-up?** no

---

### UTS/E-U8 · What he meant to change, and did not live to do
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** near the end, before 23 AH `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** no change. Pair on screen with the ladder from E-U5.
**What happened:** He built the ladder. And at the end of his life he said he had intended to take it away. Four thousand for everyone, he said — and he had even worked out what the four thousand were for: a thousand you leave with your family, a thousand you take with you as provision, a thousand to equip yourself, a thousand to keep for ease. He died before he did it. The graded register he built is the one his successors inherited, and the flat one he intended never existed.
**The statement:**
> وَقَالَ عُمَرُ قَبْلَ مَوْتِهِ: لَقَدْ هَمَمْتُ أَنْ أَجْعَلَ الْعَطَاءَ أَرْبَعَةَ آلَافٍ أَرْبَعَةَ آلَافٍ، أَلْفًا يَجْعَلُهَا الرَّجُلُ فِي أَهْلِهِ، وَأَلْفًا يُزَوَّدُهَا مَعَهُ، وَأَلْفًا يَتَجَهَّزُ بِهَا، وَأَلْفًا يَتَرَفَّقُ بِهَا. فَمَاتَ قَبْلَ أَنْ يَفْعَلَ.
> — الکامل فی التاریخ ج۲ ص۳۳۲ · https://shamela.ws/book/21712/1023
> *English:* "ʿUmar said before his death: I had resolved to make the stipend four thousand each — a thousand a man leaves with his household, a thousand he takes as provision, a thousand he equips himself with, and a thousand he keeps for ease. But he died before he did it."
**عبرت:** He treated his own arrangement as a first attempt rather than a finished thing; the men who came after inherited the attempt and not the intention.
**Hands-up?** no

---

### UTS/E-U9 · The reserve fund he refused to keep
**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** at the fixing of the stipends `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** no change.
**What happened:** Someone at the register made an entirely reasonable suggestion. Leave something in the treasury, أمير المؤمنين — a reserve, in case something comes. عمر ؓ's answer was not gentle. He called the suggestion something Satan had put on the man's tongue, and said it would be a fitna for those who came after him. What he would leave to his successors, he said, was what God and His Messenger had prepared — obedience, the thing that had actually got them this far.
**The statement:**
> فَقَالَ: كَلِمَةٌ أَلْقَاهَا الشَّيْطَانُ عَلَى فِيكَ وَقَانِي اللَّهُ شَرَّهَا، وَهِيَ فِتْنَةٌ لِمَنْ بَعْدِي … فَإِذَا كَانَ الْمَالُ ثَمَنَ دِينِ أَحَدِكُمْ هَلَكْتُمْ.
> — الکامل فی التاریخ ج۲ ص۳۳۳ · https://shamela.ws/book/21712/1024
> *English:* "He said: a word Satan cast on your tongue, and God has kept me from its harm; it is a fitna for those after me … And when money is the price of one of you's religion, you are ruined."
**عبرت:** He was more afraid of what a full treasury would do to the men who inherited it than of what an empty one would do to him.
**Hands-up?** no

---

### UTS/E-U10 · Four conditions, in writing, with witnesses
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** standing practice throughout the caliphate `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** put the governor list from ج۷ ص۱۸۱ on screen — Mecca, Syria, Iraq, Yemen, Baḥrayn, ʿUmān, Baṣra, Mosul, the Jazīra.
**What happened:** By 16 AH عمر ؓ had governors in a dozen provinces, some of them a month's ride away. He could not watch them. So he wrote down what they were not allowed to do, had a group of المهاجرون witness it against them, and attached a penalty. Four conditions. Do not ride a Persian horse. Do not eat fine bread. Do not wear fine cloth. And do not shut your door against people with needs. Two of the three great books of the safe list record the same four conditions, independently.
**The statement:**
> وكان عمر إذا استعمل عاملًا كتب له عهدًا وأشهد عليه رهطًا من المهاجرين واشترط عليه أن لا يركب برذونًا، ولا يأكل نقيًا، ولا يلبس رقيقًا، ولا يغلق بابهَ دون ذوي الحاجات. فإن فعل شيئًا من ذلك حَلَّتْ عليه العقوبةُ.
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۲۶۴ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3410
> *English:* "When ʿUmar appointed a governor he wrote him a covenant and had a company of the Emigrants witness it against him, stipulating that he not ride a Persian horse, not eat fine bread, not wear fine cloth, and not shut his door against people with needs. If he did any of these, punishment fell due on him."
**عبرت:** Three of the four conditions are about appetite and the fourth is about access — and it is the fourth that tells you what he thought a governor was.
**Hands-up?** "Three of those four are about what a governor may eat, wear and ride. What do you suppose the fourth one is?"

---

### UTS/E-U11 · The door of the palace at Kufa
**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** 17 AH `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** **Kufa appears on the map.** The Muslims leave المدائن and found a new city.
**What happened:** The Companions could not live at المدائن. The flies and the dust made them ill, their colour changed, their bodies weakened. عمر ؓ wrote back a single sentence of policy: the Arabs will not do well except where their camels do well. So a site was found, and Kufa was laid out from scratch — the mosque first, then an archer shooting in four directions to mark how far the houses would go; roads of forty cubits, thirty, twenty; alleys of seven. Reed houses, which burned; then mud brick, on عمر ؓ's order, on condition of no extravagance. Then سعد ؓ built himself a palace near the market, and the noise of the crowd made it hard to hear, so he took to shutting his door. The phrase reached Medina. عمر ؓ sent محمد بن مسلمة ؓ with instructions: reach Kufa, strike your flint, gather firewood, burn the palace door, come straight back.
**The statement:**
> فلما بلغت هذه الكلمة عمر بن الخطاب بعث محمد بن مسلمة، فأمره إذا انتهى إلى الكوفة أن يقدح زناده ويجمع حطبًا ويحرق باب القصر ثم يرجع من فوره … وأمر سعدًا أن لا يغلق بابه عن الناس
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۱۸۳–۱۸۴ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3329 · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3330
> *English:* "When that word reached ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb he sent Muḥammad ibn Maslama, and ordered him that when he reached Kūfa he should strike his flint, gather firewood, burn the palace door, and return at once … and he ordered Saʿd not to shut his door against the people."
**عبرت:** The fourth condition was not decorative — he burned a door to prove it.
**Hands-up?** no

---

### UTS/E-U12 · خالد ؓ made to stand, and removed
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** 17 AH `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** قنسرين and حمص, northern Syria.
**What happened:** خالد بن الوليد ؓ came back from a raid with great wealth, and gave الأشعث بن قيس ؓ ten thousand. عمر ؓ heard, and wrote to أبو عبيدة ؓ. Stand him up. Uncover his turban. Take off his cap. Bind him with his own turban and ask him where the ten thousand came from — because if it was his own money it is extravagance, and if it was the campaign's money it is a betrayal. Then remove him. It was done in the mosque, in front of everyone. أبو عبيدة ؓ said nothing the whole time, then came down from the pulpit and apologised for what had not been his own doing or his own wish; خالد ؓ accepted the apology and knew he had meant nothing by it. In Medina, عمر ؓ valued his property, took twenty thousand, and told him — in the same breath — that he was noble in his sight and beloved to him, and that he would never work for him again. And then he wrote to every province so that no one could invent a reason.
**The statement:**
> كتب عمر إلى الأمصار: إني لم أعزل خالدًا عن سخطة ولا خيانة، ولكن الناس فتنوا به فأحببت أن يعلموا أن الله هو الصانعُ.
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۱۹۱ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3337
> *English:* "ʿUmar wrote to the garrison cities: I did not remove Khālid out of anger nor for any dishonesty; but the people were beguiled by him, and I wanted them to know that it is God who does."
**عبرت:** He did not accuse the man of anything; he removed him and then told everyone that no accusation had been made — which is harder than either accusing or keeping silent.
**Hands-up?** no
**⚠ Note for the speaker:** الذہبی reports a different cause for this (سیر جراشدون ص۹۸). Tell البدایہ's version and do not assert a motive. See §4.4.

---

### UTS/E-U13 · The spoils of جلولاء, and a man weeping over them
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** 16 AH — الکامل carries جلولاء inside its سنة ست عشرة block (ج۲ ص۳۳۷–۳۵۱); but الليث بن سعد, in سیر جراشدون ص۸۸, puts جلولاء in **19 AH** (§6.1). `[SOURCED — and disputed]` · **Map:** جلولاء and حلوان, east of المدائن — the Persian frontier.
**What happened:** The fifth of the spoils of جلولاء came to Medina. عمر ؓ swore no roof would cover it until it was divided, so عبد الرحمن بن عوف ؓ and عبد الله بن الأرقم ؓ spent the night guarding it in the mosque. In the morning he came out among the people and uncovered it — ruby, chrysolite, jewels — and he wept. عبد الرحمن ؓ asked him why; by God, he said, this is a place for thanks, not tears. And عمر ؓ told him what he was actually looking at.
**The statement:**
> فَقَالَ عُمَرُ: وَاللَّهِ مَا ذَلِكَ يُبْكِينِي، وَبِاللَّهِ مَا أَعْطَى اللَّهُ هَذَا قَوْمًا إِلَّا تَحَاسَدُوا وَتَبَاغَضُوا، وَلَا تَحَاسَدُوا إِلَّا أَلْقَى اللَّهُ بَأْسَهُمْ بَيْنَهُمْ.
> — الکامل فی التاریخ ج۲ ص۳۴۷–۳۴۸ · https://shamela.ws/book/21712/1038 · https://shamela.ws/book/21712/1039
> *English:* "ʿUmar said: by God, that is not what makes me weep. By God, God has never given this to a people but they envied one another and hated one another; and they never envied one another but God cast their violence among themselves."
**عبرت:** He was looking at the treasure and seeing what it usually does to the people who win it.
**Hands-up?** no

---

### UTS/E-U14 · The land that was not divided
**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** 16 AH, after جلولاء — same dispute as E-U13 `[SOURCED — and disputed]` · **Map:** the السواد — the whole irrigated belt of southern Iraq, between حلوان and القادسية. Shade it and leave it shaded.
**What happened:** The richest farmland any Muslim army had ever taken was now in Muslim hands. By one reading of the rules it belonged to the fighters, to be divided like any other spoil. عمر ؓ refused to divide it. ابن الاثیر gives practical reasons — thickets, woodland, water-sinks, land that had belonged to the fire-temples and the post roads and to Chosroes himself, none of it parcellable — and then one reason that is not practical at all: he feared fitna among the Muslims. Having refused to divide it, he then forbade its sale, on the ground that it had never been divided in the first place. جرير ؓ bought a field on the Euphrates bank; عمر ؓ cancelled the purchase.
**The statement:**
> وَمَنَعَ عُمَرُ مِنْ قِسْمَةِ السَّوَادِ … وَخَافَ أَيْضًا الْفِتْنَةَ بَيْنَ الْمُسْلِمِينَ، وَمَنَعَ مِنْ بِيعِهِ لِأَنَّهُ لَمْ يُقَسَّمْ
> — الکامل فی التاریخ ج۲ ص۳۴۸ · https://shamela.ws/book/21712/1039
> *English:* "ʿUmar forbade the division of al-Sawād … And he also feared fitna among the Muslims. And he forbade its sale, because it had not been divided."
**عبرت:** The generation that took the land did not take the land, and what they gave up was given up for a generation they would never meet.
**Hands-up?** no
**⚠ Note for the speaker:** the verse he is usually said to have argued from is **not** in any of our sources. Do not put a verse in his mouth here. See §X.1.

---

### UTS/E-U15 · Judges for the new cities
**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** 18 AH `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** الكوفة and البصرة, both of them cities that did not exist ten years earlier.
**What happened:** A state that has just invented a calendar, a payroll and two cities eventually needs somebody to settle disputes in them. In 18 AH عمر ؓ appointed شريح بن الحارث الكندي judge over Kufa and كعب بن سور الأزدي judge over Basra. The detail worth noticing is not the appointment; it is what the chronicles do afterwards. From that year on, the year-by-year record lists the judges alongside the governors, as a standing office — this year's judges were last year's judges. That is what an institution looks like from the outside.
**The statement:**
> وفيها استقضى عمر شريحًا على الكوفة، وكعب بن سور على البصرة.
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۲۰۷ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3353
> *English:* "In it ʿUmar appointed Shurayḥ as judge over Kūfa, and Kaʿb ibn Sūr over Baṣra."
**عبرت:** Conquest is an event and a judiciary is a habit, and only one of the two is still working the following year.
**Hands-up?** no

---

### UTS/E-U16 · عام الرمادة: the oath
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** 18 AH `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** the whole Ḥijāz. Show the tribes converging on Medina from every direction.
**What happened:** The rain failed across the Ḥijāz and the ground went black — so black that they named the year after ashes. The wild animals came in among the people. The tribes broke camp and came to Medina because there was nowhere else, and nobody had any food left. عمر ؓ opened the treasury and spent it until there was nothing in it. And he bound himself: no ghee, no fat — ابن الاثیر says no ghee, no milk, no meat — until the people were relieved. In good years his bread was crumbled with milk and ghee. That year it was oil and vinegar. His colour blackened and his body changed until they were afraid for him. It lasted nine months. When his servant bought him a skin of ghee and a skin of milk, thinking to release him, he told him to give both away.
**The statement:**
> وَقَالَ: كَيْفَ يَعْنِينِي شَأْنُ الرَّعِيَّةِ إِذَا لَمْ يُصِبْنِي مَا أَصَابَهُمْ!
> — الکامل فی التاریخ ج۲ ص۳۷۵ · https://shamela.ws/book/21712/1066
> *English:* "And he said: how shall the condition of the subjects concern me, if what befell them does not befall me?"
**عبرت:** He did not go hungry in order to feed anyone — he went hungry so that the hunger of others would not be something he could think about at a distance.
**Hands-up?** no

---

### UTS/E-U17 · The night he found nobody laughing
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** 18 AH `[SOURCED — ابن کثیر calls the chain جيد]` · **Map:** the relief routes: Basra overland, Egypt by the Qulzum sea to Jeddah and up to Mecca.
**What happened:** He went out at night to walk the city, as he did. And something was wrong that he could not name at first. Nobody was laughing. Nobody was talking in their houses the way people talk. And there were no beggars. He asked why, and was told: the beggars asked and were given nothing, so they stopped asking; and the people are in grief and hardship, so they neither talk nor laugh. So he wrote two letters. One to أبو موسى ؓ in Basra, one to عمرو بن العاص ؓ in Egypt. Each was three words long. Each of them sent back a great caravan. The first man through the gate was أبو عبيدة ؓ, with four thousand mounts of food — the last thing he does in this story before the next one kills him. On عمر ؓ's own instruction, the food went out to the country round Medina first.
**The statement:**
> فكتب عمر إلى أبي موسى بالبصرة أن: يا غوثاه لأمة محمد. وكتب إلى عمرو بن العاص بمصر أن: يا غوثاه لأمة محمد.
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۲۰۳ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3349
> *English:* "So ʿUmar wrote to Abū Mūsā in Baṣra: a cry for help for the community of Muḥammad! And he wrote to ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ in Egypt: a cry for help for the community of Muḥammad!"
**عبرت:** He noticed the silence before anyone reported the hunger, and a man who is out walking at night notices things that no report contains.
**Hands-up?** "Seven thousand men ate supper at his table one night. How many people do you think were being fed in total?" *(الذہبی: forty thousand, then sixty thousand.)*

---

### UTS/E-U18 · The prayer for rain
**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** 18 AH `[SOURCED — البخاري]` · **Map:** the muṣallā outside Medina.
**What happened:** He went out to pray for rain, and he took العباس ؓ out with him. And the prayer he made is one of the most quoted sentences in the whole of his caliphate, because of what it concedes: we used to ask through our Prophet ﷺ, and now we ask through his uncle. The report is in صحيح البخاري. On another such occasion people complained that they had not actually heard him ask for rain at all; he answered that he had sought the rain by the heaven's own rain-bringers, and recited سورة نوح — *ask forgiveness of your Lord; He will send the sky upon you in torrents.*
**The statement:**
> اللهم إنّا كُنا نتوسَّلُ إليكَ بنبينا ﷺ فتسقينا، وإنا نتوسَّلُ إليك بعمِّ نبينا فاسْقِنا. قال: فيُسْقَوْنَ.
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۲۰۵ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3351 · (البخاري ۳۷۱۰)
> *English:* "O God, we used to approach You through our Prophet ﷺ and You would give us rain; and we approach You through the uncle of our Prophet — so give us rain. And they were given rain."
**عبرت:** Nine months of drought, a treasury spent to nothing, and the last thing left to try was استغفار.
**Hands-up?** no

---

### UTS/E-U19 · سرغ: the caliph turns the army around
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** 17 AH `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** **سرغ**, on the road from Medina into Syria — mark it. This is the furthest north he gets, and then the arrow reverses.
**What happened:** He was riding north into Syria with the leading men of the Muhājirūn and Anṣār. At سرغ the commanders of the Syrian armies rode out to meet him — أبو عبيدة ؓ, يزيد بن أبي سفيان ؓ, خالد بن الوليد ؓ — with news. The pestilence had fallen on Syria. He consulted, and the room split: you set out for a purpose, do not turn back — against — we do not think you should take the leading Companions into this. In the morning he ordered the return. And أبو عبيدة ؓ said the one thing that could stop him: are you running from God's decree? The answer came back without a pause.
**The statement:**
> فقال أبو عبيدة: أفرارًا من قدر الله؟ قال: نعم! نفرُّ من قدر الله إلى قدر الله، أرأيت لو هبطت واديًا ذا عدوتين إحداهما مخصبة والأخرى مجدبة، فإن رعيت الخِصبة رعيتها بقدر الله، وإن أنت رعيت الجدبة رعيتها بقدر الله؟ ثم قال: لو غيرك يقولها يا أبا عبيدة.
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۱۸۶ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3332
> *English:* "Abū ʿUbayda said: fleeing from God's decree? He said: yes — we flee from God's decree to God's decree. Do you not see, if you came down into a valley with two slopes, one fertile and one barren, and you grazed the fertile you would graze it by God's decree, and if you grazed the barren you would graze it by God's decree? Then he said: if it were anyone but you saying it, Abū ʿUbayda."
**عبرت:** Trust in God's decree is not a reason to walk into the barren half of the valley.
**Hands-up?** no

---

### UTS/E-U20 · The man who had been away
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** 17 AH, at سرغ, the same hour `[SOURCED — البخاري ۵۷۲۹]` · **Map:** same as E-U19.
**What happened:** The argument was not settled by rank. عبد الرحمن بن عوف ؓ had been away on business and arrived late, into the middle of it. He said: I have knowledge of this. And he gave them a ḥadīth of the Prophet ﷺ that answered the exact question in front of them — do not enter a land where it is, and do not leave a land where you are. عمر ؓ praised God, and took the people home. ابن الاثیر notes, in his own voice, that this is the sounder of the two versions, because البخاري and مسلم both carry it.
**The statement:**
> سمعت رسول الله ﷺ يقول: "إذا سمعتم به بأرض قوم فلا تقدموا عليه، وإذا وقع بأرض وأنتم فيها فلا تخرجوا فرارًا منه". فحمد الله عمر … ورجع بالناس.
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۱۸۶ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3332 · (البخاري ۵۷۲۹)
> *English:* "I heard the Messenger of God ﷺ say: 'When you hear of it in a land, do not go to it; and when it falls in a land and you are in it, do not go out fleeing from it.' So ʿUmar praised God — and returned with the people."
**عبرت:** The decision was already right; what the latecomer brought was the knowledge that it was right, and the caliph thanked God rather than himself.
**Hands-up?** "The Commander of the Believers has already given the order. A man walks in late with something to say. What should happen?"

---

### UTS/E-U21 · The letter أبو عبيدة ؓ saw through
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** 18 AH `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** حمص / the Syrian camp.
**What happened:** The plague was burning through the army in Syria, and عمر ؓ wanted أبو عبيدة ؓ out of it. He could not order it — أبو عبيدة ؓ would not leave his men. So he wrote something clever: a need has arisen that I must speak to you about face to face; when you read this letter, do not put it down until you come to me. أبو عبيدة ؓ read it, understood exactly what it was, and said: may God forgive the Commander of the Believers. Then he wrote back, and refused. And when عمر ؓ read the reply, he wept — and the people around him thought the news had come.
**The statement:**
> إنّي قد عرفت حاجتَكَ إليَّ، وإني في جندٍ من المسلمين لا أجدُ بنفسي رغبةً عنهم، فلستُ أريدُ فراقهم حتى يقضيَ الله فيّ وفيهم أمرَهُ وقضاءَهُ، فخلِّني من عزمتك يا أمير المؤمنين، ودعني في جندي.
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۱۸۸ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3334
> *English:* "I have understood your need of me. I am among an army of the Muslims, and I find in myself no desire to be away from them; I do not wish to part from them until God decrees His command and His decree upon me and upon them. So release me from your charge, Commander of the Believers, and leave me with my army."
**عبرت:** أمين الأمة would not be lifted out of a danger he was leaving his men inside.
**Hands-up?** no

---

### UTS/E-U22 · Three sermons, three graves, one day
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** 18 AH `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** عمواس, between Jerusalem and al-Ramla — the town the plague is named after. Then the whole of Syria.
**What happened:** أبو عبيدة ؓ stood up in front of the dying army and preached. He called the affliction their Lord's mercy, their Prophet's ﷺ prayer, and the death of the righteous before them — and asked God to give أبو عبيدة his share of it. He was struck, and he died. He appointed معاذ بن جبل ؓ over the people. معاذ ؓ stood in the same place and preached the same words, and asked God to give the family of معاذ their share. His son عبد الرحمن was struck and died. Then معاذ ؓ prayed for himself, and was struck in the palm of his hand, and would look at it and turn it over and say that he would not take anything of this world in exchange for what was in it. أبو عبيدة ؓ, معاذ ؓ and أبو مالك الأشعري ؓ were struck on one and the same day; البدایہ names شرحبيل بن حسنة ؓ with them on that day too. The same plague killed يزيد بن أبي سفيان ؓ, الحارث بن هشام ؓ, سہیل بن عمرو ؓ and his son أبو جندل ؓ, and الفضل بن العباس ؓ — but the sources say only that they died in it, not that they died on that day, and the speaker should not say so. الحارث بن هشام ؓ had gone out to Syria with seventy of his household. Four came back.
**The statement:**
> أيها الناس، إن هذا الوجعَ رحمةُ ربِّكم ودعوةُ نبيّكم وموتُ الصالحين قبلكم، وإنَّ أبا عبيدة يسأل الله أن يقسم لأبي عبيدة حظَّه.
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۱۸۸ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3334
> *English:* "O people, this affliction is your Lord's mercy, and your Prophet's prayer, and the death of the righteous before you; and Abū ʿUbayda asks God to apportion to Abū ʿUbayda his share."
**عبرت:** The generation that had seen the Prophet ﷺ was thinning out, and it was thinning out in one place at one time.
**Hands-up?** no

---

### UTS/E-U23 · What a caliph does after a plague
**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** 18 AH, after عمواس `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** عمر ؓ's journey to الشام — Medina → أيلة → الجابية.
**What happened:** So many people died in the same houses, in unknown order, that the inheritances of Syria could not be settled. Nobody could say who had predeceased whom. عمر ؓ was minded to tour the provinces and asked which to start with; كعب الأحبار and علي ؓ both argued for Iraq or against it, and he cut the discussion short: the inheritances of the ʿAmwās dead are lost — I begin with Syria. He went, divided the estates himself, reassigned the commands — معاوية ؓ to Damascus, and later شرحبيل ؓ moved with a public explanation that it was not out of anger — and came home in ذو القعدة. On the way, at أيلة, he gave the bishop his own torn shirt to wash and mend, wore it back, and refused the new one the man made him. And in Syria the people asked for بلال ؓ to give the adhān once more; every man there who had known the Prophet ﷺ wept, and عمر ؓ wept hardest of all.
**The statement:**
> فَقَالَ عُمَرُ: إِنَّ مَوَارِيثَ أَهْلِ عَمَوَاسَ قَدْ ضَاعَتْ، أَبْدَأُ بِالشَّامِ فَأُقَسِّمُ الْمَوَارِيثَ وَأُقِيمَ لَهُمْ مَا فِي نَفْسِي، ثُمَّ أَرْجِعُ
> — الکامل فی التاریخ ج۲ ص۳۷۹ · https://shamela.ws/book/21712/1070
> *English:* "ʿUmar said: the inheritances of the people of ʿAmwās have been lost. I shall begin with Syria and divide the inheritances and set up for them what is in my mind; then I shall return."
**عبرت:** The catastrophe was over and the paperwork was not, and he treated the paperwork as part of the catastrophe.
**Hands-up?** no

---

### UTS/E-U24 · Jerusalem asks for him by name
**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** 15 or 16 or 17 AH `[SOURCED — disputed]` · **Map:** **بيت المقدس.** The single biggest move on the Syrian map.
**What happened:** أبو عبيدة ؓ besieged Jerusalem, and the city agreed to terms — on one condition. The Commander of the Believers must come himself. The letter reached Medina and the counsel split: عثمان ؓ said do not go, it will humble them more; علي ؓ said go, it will be easier on the Muslims sitting in the siege lines. He took علي ؓ's advice, left علي ؓ over Medina, and rode. On the road from الجابية his own horse was worn out, so they brought him a برذون — a heavy non-Arab mount; it began to amble under him and he got off at once and struck its face. He never rode one before that or after it. At الجابية he preached, and the sermon survives.
**The statement:**
> أيها الناسُ، أصْلحُوا سَرائرَكُم تَصْلُحْ علانيتكُمْ، واعملُوا لآخرتِكُم تُكْفَوا أمرَ دنياكم … فمن أراد بُحْبُوحَةَ الجنة فليلزمِ الجماعة
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۱۵۸ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3304
> *English:* "O people, put right what is within you and what is outward in you will be put right; work for your hereafter and the affair of your world will be taken care of for you … Whoever wants the midst of the Garden, let him hold to the community."
**عبرت:** The city set the terms of its own surrender, and the term it chose was to see the man in person.
**Hands-up?** no

---

### UTS/E-U25 · "You restrained yourself, so they restrained themselves"
**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** 16 AH, when the spoils of المدائن arrive — الکامل carries فتح المدائن inside its سنة ست عشرة block (ج۲ ص۳۳۸–), and البدایہ ج۷ ص۱۶۳ records the split in so many words: ابن جرير/سيف put the Persian campaigns in 15, «وقال ابن إسحاق والواقدي: إنما كان ذلك في سنة ست عشرة» (https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3309). `[SOURCED — and disputed]` · **Map:** المدائن — Chosroes' capital.
**What happened:** The spoils of المدائن were beyond anything anyone had seen — jewelled carpets, the Persian king's own robes, houses filled to the ceiling with gold and silver vessels, and so much camphor the soldiers mistook it for salt and tried to bake with it. Every horseman's share came to twelve thousand. And the whole of it, down to a mule loaded with the king's crown that a soldier had chased down and taken back off looters, was handed in intact. عمر ؓ looked at it and said one sentence about the men who had sent it. علي ؓ answered him with one sentence about why they had.
**The statement:**
> فروينا أن عمر لما نظر إلى ذلك قال: إنَّ قومًا أدَّوْا هذا لأمناءُ، فقال له علي بن أبي طالب: إنك عَفَفْتَ فَعَفَّتْ رَعيتُكَ، ولو رَتَعْتَ لرَتَعتْ.
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۱۷۳ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3319
> *English:* "ʿUmar said: a people who handed this over are trustworthy men. ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib said to him: you restrained yourself, so your subjects restrained themselves; and had you grazed freely, they would have grazed freely."
**عبرت:** Honesty at the far end of an empire is a report on the man at the near end of it.
**Hands-up?** no

---

### UTS/E-U26 · What is lawful for me out of this money?
**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** at the founding of the ديوان `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** no change.
**What happened:** He put a question to the Muslims that no one had asked him to answer. I was a merchant, he said, and God provided for my household out of my trade; and you have occupied me with this business of yours. So what do you hold is lawful for me out of this money? The people talked a great deal. علي ؓ said nothing until asked directly, and then gave the shortest answer in the room: what keeps you and your household in what is fitting, and nothing beyond that. The people said: the word is علي ؓ's word. He took that, and it was hard. Later عثمان, علي, طلحة and الزبير ؓ agreed among themselves that his allowance ought to be raised, and sent حفصة ؓ to sound him out without naming them. He worked out at once that there were names and demanded them; she would not give them. So he asked her three questions instead — what was the best garment the Prophet ﷺ ever owned in your house, the finest food he ever had here, the softest thing he ever lay on — and sent the answers back as his reply.
**The statement:**
> فَقَالَ: مَا أَصْلَحَكَ وَعِيَالَكَ بِالْمَعْرُوفِ لَيْسَ لَكَ غَيْرُهُ. فَقَالَ الْقَوْمُ: الْقَوْلُ مَا قَالَ عَلِيٌّ.
> — الکامل فی التاریخ ج۲ ص۳۳۳ · https://shamela.ws/book/21712/1024
> *English:* "He said: what keeps you and your household in what is fitting — you have nothing beyond that. The people said: the word is ʿAlī's word."
**عبرت:** He did not set his own salary; he asked, and then he lived with the answer.
**Hands-up?** no

---

### UTS/E-U27 · The list of firsts
**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** closing card for the whole strand `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** the finished map of 23 AH, everything lit.
**What happened:** When ابن کثیر رحمہ اللہ comes to write the death of عمر ؓ, he does not begin with the death. He begins with a list, and the list runs for half a page without pausing. First to be called Commander of the Believers. First to write the dating era. Gathered the people for tarāwīḥ. First to patrol Medina at night. Carried the whip and disciplined with it. Founded the garrison cities and raised the armies. Laid down the land tax. Compiled the registers. Fixed the stipends. Appointed the judges. Organised the provinces. Ten years and five months.
**The statement:**
> وهو أولُ من دُعي أميرَ المؤمنين، وأولُ من كتب التاريخ … ومصَّر الأمصار، وجنَّد الأجناد. ووضع الخَراج، ودوَّنَ الدواوين، وفرض الأعطية، واستقضى القضاة، وكوَّر الكُورَ
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۲۶۳ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3409
> *English:* "He was the first to be called Commander of the Believers, and the first to write the dating era … he founded the garrison cities, and raised the armies. He laid down the land tax, compiled the registers, fixed the stipends, appointed the judges, and organised the provinces."
**عبرت:** Almost nothing on that list is a battle, and the things on it are the things that were still standing a hundred years later.
**Hands-up?** "How many of the things on this list do you still use?"

---

## نهاوند, the assassination, and the شورى

*Source note: [`nahawand-the-shura-and-the-death-of-umar.md`](../docs/research/nahawand-the-shura-and-the-death-of-umar.md) — read it for the pages behind these cards.*

### NTS/E-U1 · Persia decides it has no choice
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** 21 AH `[SOURCED]` (البدایہ ج۷ ص۲۲۵; the sources also record 17 and 19) · **Map:** The Persian heartland east of the Zagros lights up: a single mass of colour gathering at نهاوند, south of همذان.

**What happened:** Ten years of defeats have pushed the last Sasanian king out of his own capital and eastward, city by city. He writes to every governor still standing. They meet and they argue, and the argument that decides them is not about religion. It is that the fighting has reached the seat of the kingdom itself, and will not stop there. A hundred and fifty thousand men gather at نهاوند under a commander the sources name three different ways. Word reaches سعد بن أبي وقاص ؓ at الکوفہ, and سعد ؓ writes to المدینہ.

**The statement:**
> وقالوا: إنَّ محمدًا الذي جاء العربَ بالدين لم يتعرَّضْ لبلادنا، ولا أبو بكر الذي قام بعده تعرَّض لنا في دار مُلكنا، وإن عمر بن الخطاب هذا لمّا طال ملكه انتهكَ حرمَتنا وأخذ بلادنا، ولم يكفه ذلك حتى أغزانا في عُقْر دارنا، وأخذ بيتَ المملكة وليس بمُنْتهِ حتى يخرجكم من بلادكم.
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۲۲۷ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3373
> *English:* "And they said: this Muḥammad who brought the Arabs their religion did not come against our lands; nor did Abū Bakr, who rose after him, come against us in the seat of our kingdom. But this ʿUmar b. al-Khaṭṭāb — when his reign grew long he violated our sanctuary and took our lands, and that did not suffice him until he made war on us in the heart of our own country and took the house of the kingdom; and he will not stop until he drives you out of your lands."

**عبرت:** A state that will not answer a summons is eventually made to answer a question it did not choose.

**Hands-up?** Hands up — who can tell me roughly how far Islam had reached when the Prophet ﷺ died? (Answer on the map: two mosques and a peninsula.) Now look where we are ten years later.

---

### NTS/E-U2 · The caliph proposes to march, and is talked out of it
**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** 21 AH `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** A pin on المدینہ, with a dotted line running north-east toward a point between الکوفہ and البصرہ — then the dotted line is rubbed out.

**What happened:** عمر ؓ calls the people to the mosque. His plan is to march himself and take up a position between the two garrison cities, so he can feed men forward as they are needed. Five men answer him — عثمان ؓ, علی ؓ, طلحة ؓ, الزبير ؓ and عبد الرحمن بن عوف ؓ — each speaking separately. Every one of them tells him to stay where he is. علی ؓ gives him the image that decides it. عمر ؓ takes the advice he did not come in with, and — as he always did — puts it to العباس ؓ before he acts on it.

**The statement:**
> ومكانكَ منهم يا أمير المؤمنين مكان النظام من الخَرز يجمعه ويمسكه، فإذا انحلَّ تفرَّق وما فيه وذهب، ثم لم يجتمعْ بحذافيره أبدًا.
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۲۲۸ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3374
> *English:* "Your position among them, Commander of the Believers, is the position of the thread among the beads: it gathers them and holds them; and if it comes undone they scatter, and what is in it is gone, and it is never gathered whole again."

**عبرت:** A man is not always most useful at the place where the danger is.

**Hands-up?** no

---

### NTS/E-U3 · The first at the spear-points
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** 21 AH `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** A named commander pin appears on the Persian front: النعمان بن مُقَرِّن ؓ.

**What happened:** عمر ؓ asks the council to name him a commander for the Persian front, and says the man must be an Iraqi. They tell him he knows his own soldiers better. So he states the qualification before he states the name — and then names النعمان بن مُقَرِّن ؓ, who had already written asking to be moved from his post so he could fight this war. Then عمر ؓ does something a modern room notices: he writes down, in advance, who takes command if the commander is killed, and who after that, seven deep.

**The statement:**
> أما والله لأوليَنَّ رجلًا يكون أولَ الأسنّةِ إذا لقيها غدًا. قالوا: من يا أميرَ المؤمنين؟ قال: النُّعمان بن مُقَرِّنٍ.
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۲۲۸ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3374
> *English:* "By God, I shall appoint a man who will be the first at the spear-points when he meets them tomorrow. They said: who, Commander of the Believers? He said: al-Nuʿmān b. Muqarrin."

**عبرت:** The test of a commander was set before the name was, and the name had to meet the test.

**Hands-up?** no

---

### NTS/E-U4 · One man is worth a hundred thousand dinars
**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** 21 AH `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** No map move. The letter itself on screen, in Naskh, with the English beneath.

**What happened:** The written orders go out from المدینہ. Most of the letter is the ordinary business of moving an army — march by this route, join at ماه, the Kufans will meet you there. But in the middle of it عمر ؓ puts three prohibitions and a reason, and the reason is a valuation. A room that has just heard the figure 150,000 hears this sentence differently.

**The statement:**
> ولا توطئهم وعرًا فتؤذيَهم، ولا تمنعْهم حقَّهمِ فتكفِّرَهم، ولا تدخلهم غَيْضَةً، فإن رجلًا من المسلمين أحبُّ إليَّ من مئة ألف دينار
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۲۲۹ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3375
> *English:* "Do not make them tread rough ground and so hurt them; do not withhold from them their right and so make them ungrateful; do not take them into a thicket — for one man of the Muslims is dearer to me than a hundred thousand dinars."

**عبرت:** The orders that survive are the ones that say what a life is worth.

**Hands-up?** no

---

### NTS/E-U5 · The head and the two wings
**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** 21 AH `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** Three labels on the Persian plateau — أصبهان at the centre, فارس and أذربيجان to either side — with an arrow through the centre.

**What happened:** The Persian commander of تُستر, الهرمزان, had been captured and brought to المدینہ. When عمر ؓ has to decide where to strike first — أصبهان, or فارس, or أذربيجان — he puts the question to him. The answer is one sentence of pure strategy, from a man who had commanded on the other side, and عمر ؓ acts on it.

**The statement:**
> أصبهان الرأس، وفارس وأذربيجان الجناحان، إن قطعت أحد الجناحين مال الرأس بالجناح الآخر، وإن قطعت الرأس وقع الجناحان
> — سیر أعلام النبلاء جراشدون ص۱۲۷ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/1272
> *English:* "Iṣfahān is the head, and Fārs and Adharbayjān are the two wings. If you cut off one of the two wings, the head leans with the other wing; but if you cut off the head, the two wings fall."

**عبرت:** Counsel was taken from the man best placed to give it, and where he had come from was not the question.

**Hands-up?** Hands up — who would you ask, if you had to plan a campaign against an empire you had never governed?

---

### NTS/E-U6 · The deadlock, and the man who had once claimed prophethood
**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** 21 AH `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** The map holds still: a besieged town, the besiegers unable to force it. Nothing moves for the length of the card.

**What happened:** The Persians go behind their walls and their ditch, and come out only when they choose. It drags on — البدایہ says only «فلما طال على المسلمين هذا الحالُ واستمرَّ» and gives no length, so do not put a number of days or weeks on it. النعمان ؓ calls his senior men and asks how to draw them into the open. The oldest speaks first, by custom, and says wait. عمرو بن معدي كرب ؓ says charge, and is told the walls are fighting for the other side. Then طُلَيْحة الأسدي ؓ speaks — the same طليحة who had claimed prophethood in the ردة and come back to Islam, and whom أبو بكر ؓ had ordered خالد ؓ to consult but never to put in command. His plan is the one the army adopts.

**The statement:**
> وإني أرى أن تبعثَ سريةً فتحدق بهم ويناوشوهم بالقتال ويحمشوهم (١) فإذا برزوا إليهم فليفروا إلينا هرابًا، فإذا استطردوا وراءهم وانتهوا (٢) إلينا عزمنا أيضًا على الفرار كلّنا، فإنهم حينئذ لا يشكّون في الهزيمة فيخرجون من حصونهم عن بكرةِ أبيهم، فإذا تكاملَ خروجُهم رجعنا إليهم فجالدناهم حتى يقضيَ اللهُ بيننا.
> — البدایہ والنہایہ ج۷ ص۲۳۱ · https://shamela.ws/book/30097/3377
> *English:* "I hold that you should send out a detachment to close on them and skirmish and provoke them; then, when they come out against them, let our men flee back to us; and when they pursue and reach us, we too shall all resolve on flight, so that they will not doubt the rout and will come out of their fortresses to the last man. And when their coming-out is complete we shall turn back on them and fight them until God judges between us."

**عبرت:** A man can be kept out of command and still be listened to, and the two are not the same decision.

**Hands-up?** Hands up — does anyone remember, from last week, the name of the man who claimed prophethood in نجد and then came back?

### NTS/E-U7 · Three takbīrs, and a commander who asks to be the first killed
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** 21 AH `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** The single largest map beat of the evening: the Persian front breaks and the line jumps east.

**What happened:** The trap works. The Persians pour out of the town. النعمان ؓ has the army lie flat under their shields and take the arrows, and refuses to charge — men beg him and he says "gently, gently" — until the sun passes the meridian, the hour the Prophet ﷺ used to choose. Then he rides down the line, tells them he will say the takbīr three times and charge on the third, recites the chain of succession one more time, and prays out loud. He asks for two things. He is given both before dark.

**The statement:**
> اللَّهُمَّ أَعْزِزْ دِينَكَ، وَانْصُرْ عِبَادَكَ، وَاجْعَلِ النُّعْمَانَ أَوَّلَ شَهِيدٍ الْيَوْمَ عَلَى إِعْزَازِ دِينِكَ وَنَصْرِ عِبَادِكَ.
> — الکامل فی التاریخ ج۲ ص۳۹۶ · https://shamela.ws/book/21712/1087
> *English:* "O God, give might to Your religion and victory to Your servants, and make al-Nuʿmān the first martyr today upon the giving of might to Your religion and victory to Your servants."

**عبرت:** He asked for the victory first and for himself second, and that is the order the words are in.

**Hands-up?** no

### NTS/E-U8 · The names the Commander of the Believers did not know
**Tier:** CORE · **When:** 21 AH `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** A dispatch rider on the road from نهاوند to المدینہ.

**What happened:** The standard passes to حذيفة ؓ, who hides the commander's death until the battle is decided. When it is over, the army asks for النعمان ؓ and his brother tells them where he is. In المدینہ, عمر ؓ has been sleepless, going out at night to meet the road. السائب ؓ arrives with the news: a great victory, and the commander dead. عمر ؓ says the words of return and weeps until his shoulders shake. Then he asks who else was killed. السائب ؓ names the men of standing — and then says there were others, whose names the Commander of the Believers would not know.

**The statement:**
> أُولَئِكَ الْمُسْتَضْعَفُونَ مِنَ الْمُسْلِمِينَ وَلَكِنَّ الَّذِي أَكْرَمَهُمْ بِالشَّهَادَةِ يَعْرِفُ وُجُوهَهُمْ وَأَنْسَابَهُمْ، وَمَا يَصْنَعُ أُولَئِكَ بِمَعْرِفَةِ عُمَرَ!
> — الکامل فی التاریخ ج۲ ص۳۹۸ · https://shamela.ws/book/21712/1089
> *English:* "Those are the weak among the Muslims — but the One who honoured them with martyrdom knows their faces and their lineages. And what do they want with ʿUmar knowing them?"

**عبرت:** The register that matters was never the one kept in المدینہ.

**Hands-up?** no
